# DMS Platform – QA Audit Report

Audit date: 2026-11-10 · Branch: `Feat-CheckInCheckOut` (clean tree at start) · Auditor: independent QA review (Claude Code)

> **Method and honesty statement.** Docker is not installed on the audit machine (`docker: command not found`), so **no service (Keycloak, PostgreSQL, SeaweedFS, RabbitMQ, Redis, OpenBao, Nginx, backend, frontend) was running**. This audit is therefore a **static code/config review plus execution of the existing automated test suites**. No dynamic/black-box testing (live API calls, browser, load tests) was possible. Findings are marked **FAIL** only when the defect was confirmed by reading the cited code; where exploitability or runtime behaviour could not be confirmed the status is **NOT TESTED** (reason stated). Nothing was marked PASS without evidence. No application source, configuration, migration or documentation file was modified; this report is the only file created. (Running Maven/vitest/tsc/eslint regenerates build output under `backend/dms-core/target` and possibly the git-ignored `frontend/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo`.)

Five parallel read-only reviewers covered: security/auth, documents/files/storage, database/messaging, workflow & misc modules & infra, and frontend. Key findings were re-checked by the lead auditor (tenant update guard, metadata access check, notification claim SQL, outbox query – all confirmed in code).

---

## 1. Executive Summary

| Metric | Result |
|---|---|
| Backend automated tests (`./mvnw -o test`) | 981 run, **0 failures, 39 errors**, 3 skipped. All 39 errors are `PSQL FATAL: password authentication failed for user "postgres"` in 11 DB-backed test classes (migration tests, `DmsCoreApplicationTests`, `ExportJobRepositoryTest`) – environment limitation, **BLOCKED**, not code defects |
| Frontend `tsc --noEmit` | PASS – 0 errors |
| Frontend `eslint .` | PASS – 0 errors, 3 warnings (FE-L3) |
| Frontend `vitest run` | PASS – 48 files / 323 tests |
| Python OCR worker tests | NOT TESTED |
| Live/dynamic testing | BLOCKED (no Docker, no running stack) |
| Issues registered | 0 Critical, 8 High, ~35 Medium, ~30 Low, ~8 Info (some register rows group several related items; see §13) |

**Headline risks (High):**
1. Any ordinary tenant member can rename, change tier of, or **suspend their own organisation** (`PUT /api/v1/tenants/{id}`) – SEC-01.
2. Document metadata and OCR-status endpoints **skip folder/document access checks**; metadata is writable on any tenant document – DOC-01.
3. Every plain document download is **fully buffered in heap**; with no upload size limit configured this is an OOM/DoS path – DOC-02.
4. Email/notification deliveries can be **stuck in `sending` forever** after a crash or exception – NOT-01.
5. Outbox relay is **not multi-instance safe** and marks events published without broker confirmation – OBX-01/02.
6. **No security headers (CSP, frame-ancestors, nosniff, HSTS)** from Next.js or Nginx – FE-H1 / INF-01.
7. Hard-deleting a user fails on most foreign keys – DB-01.

**Overall:** The codebase shows strong engineering discipline (tenant-scoped queries everywhere sampled, bind-parameterised SQL, httpOnly cookies, scan gate/quarantine, advisory-lock/SKIP LOCKED work-claiming in most schedulers, extensive tests). The gaps found are concentrated in (a) a few authorization seams, (b) unbounded file-size defaults, (c) outbox/notification delivery reliability, and (d) edge/infra hardening. Release readiness cannot be fully asserted because runtime verification was impossible.

---

## 2. Application Architecture Overview

- **Backend:** `backend/dms-core` – Spring Boot (Java 17), package `com.oak.dms`, 69 REST controllers, 137 Flyway migrations (`src/main/resources/db`, V35 absent), ~250 test files. Entities reference each other by plain UUID (no JPA relations). Hibernate `ddl-auto: validate`, `open-in-view: false`.
- **Worker:** `backend/workers/ocr-worker` (Python, RabbitMQ consumer, internal HTTP API).
- **Frontend:** `frontend/` Next.js (app router), cookie-based session (httpOnly cookies set by dms-core), `proxy.ts` as route gate, react-query style data layer in `lib/*`.
- **Identity:** Keycloak (realm `oak-dms`, public client `oak-dms-web`); dms-core brokers login/refresh/logout and sets cookies; JWT resource server with `tenant_id` claim from a user attribute.
- **Data/infra:** PostgreSQL, SeaweedFS (S3), RabbitMQ (outbox relay → OCR queues, DLQ), Redis (dashboard/schema cache), OpenBao (secrets, encrypted env file), ClamAV scan gate, Collabora/WOPI editing, Prometheus/Grafana/Loki, Nginx in `infra/production/nginx`, Docker Compose (`infra/local`, `infra/production`), Helm/RKE2 manifests.
- **Cross-cutting:** multi-tenancy via `TenantContext`; RBAC via `@PreAuthorize` + permission codes; hash-chained audit log (global advisory lock); transactional outbox; envelope encryption at rest.

## 3. Modules Reviewed

Packages under `com.oak.dms`: audit, bookmark, checkout, comment, common, compliance, config, dashboard, department, document, editing, encryption, folder, importexport, integration, messaging, metadata, monitoring, notification, observability, ocrworker, outbox, ownership, pin, platform, preview, relationship, reporting, scanning, search, security, sharing, signature, storage, tagging, tenant, user, versioning, watermark, workflow; plus `workers/ocr-worker`, frontend `app/`, `components/`, `lib/`, `proxy.ts`, `next.config.ts`, `infra/*`, Dockerfiles, migrations.
Depth varied: deep on security, document/file handling, DB/outbox, notification, workflow, retention; lighter on sharing, signature internals, relationship, ownership, import mapping (see §14).
`search/` contains only `.gitkeep` and `tagging/` is empty – **no code exists; nothing to test** (Info INF-I2).

## 5. Functional Testing Findings

All functional findings are from code review; none were reproduced live.

### FUN-01 · Notification deliveries stuck in `sending` (High) – see NOT-01
### FUN-02 · Login ignores `?from=` redirect (Medium) – see FE-M1
### FUN-03 · Department delete ignores owned documents/retention policies (Medium) – see DEP-01
### FUN-04 · Audit export silently truncates at 10,000 rows (Medium) – see AUD-02

**Workflow module – PASS (static):** task state machine guarded with ordered locking instance→stage→tasks and re-checked guards; sign-and-approve fails closed; template transitions guarded; cancel restricted to initiator/tenant admin; DTOs validated; state changes audited.
**Comment/bookmark/pin/retention – PASS (static)** apart from BKM-01.

## 6. Backend API Findings

### API-01 · Unvalidated sort property / uncapped page size (Low) – FAIL
- Files: `document/DocumentController.java:167,192,557,566`; `application.yml` (no `spring.data.web.pageable.max-page-size`).
- Description: `@PageableDefault` routes accept any `sort=` entity property and Spring's default max page size (2000) applies.
- Repro: `GET /api/v1/documents?sort=checkedOutBy,asc&size=2000`.
- Expected: whitelisted sort fields, max page ~100–200. Actual: arbitrary entity property sort (information inference on non-exposed fields), up to 2000 rows/page.
- Fix: set max page size; whitelist sort properties.

### API-02 · Bookmark page overflow → 500 (Low) – FAIL
- File: `bookmark/BookmarkService.java:154` (`int from = Math.min(pageIndex * pageSize, total)`).
- Repro: `GET /api/v1/bookmarks?page=2147483647&size=20` → int overflow → negative `subList` index.
- Expected: empty page. Actual: 500. Fix: use `long` arithmetic/clamp.

### API-03 · Mixed validation style / error shapes (Info)
- `DocumentLifecycleController.java:50-67`, `RetentionController.java:69,77,100`, `DepartmentController.java:115` use `@RequestBody` without `@Valid`; validation is in services (verified complete for retention policies) so the error shape differs from `@Valid` endpoints.

### API-04 · Upload `title` not validated (Low) – FAIL
- `DocumentController.java:121`: no `@NotBlank/@Size`. Column is `varchar(500)` (V32:78). A >500-char title reaches the DB; blank title accepted. Status code from the `DataIntegrityViolation` handler (generic 409 per security review) not verified as appropriate.

### API-05 · Generic `IllegalStateException` mapped to 401 (Low) – FAIL
- `common/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java:104-130`: any ISE → 401 `TENANT_CONTEXT_MISSING`. Internal bugs masquerade as auth failures and may trigger frontend session handling. Fix: dedicated exception type.

### API-06 · Cross-tenant ID probe distinguishable (Low) – FAIL
- `user/UserServiceImpl.java:682-690`, `UserRoleServiceImpl.java:206-213`, `AuditEventServiceImpl.getById` throw `TenantMismatchException` instead of 404, whereas documents collapse to 404. Allows a permitted caller to confirm that a UUID exists in another tenant.

### API-07 · `server.error.include-message/binding-errors: always` (Low)
- `application.yml:113-117`. Affects Boot's fallback `/error` only; `GlobalExceptionHandler` masks 500s. Set to `never`/`on_param`.

### API-08 · Swagger/OpenAPI and `/actuator/prometheus|info` unauthenticated (Low/Info)
- `security/SecurityConfig.java:77-81`. Not proxied by production Nginx; dms-core port 8080 is published on all interfaces in local compose.

## 7. Authentication & Authorization Findings

### SEC-01 · Any tenant member can edit/suspend their own organisation (High) – FAIL
> **Remediation status: FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (see §17 Remediation Log). The original finding below is kept unchanged.
- Files: `tenant/TenantController.java:145-146` (`@PreAuthorize("hasRole('PLATFORM_ADMIN') or @tenantSecurity.isSameTenant(#tenantId)")`), `security/auth/TenantSecurity.java` (only compares tenant id), `tenant/TenantServiceImpl.java:134-146`. `GET /{tenantId}` has the same guard.
- Evidence: controller comment says "an organisation's own administrator", but no admin role is checked; `passwordConfirmation.requireIfPlatformAdmin` only applies to platform admins.
- Repro: as a non-admin member, `PUT /api/v1/tenants/<own tenantId>` with `{"tenantName":"x","subscriptionTier":"enterprise","status":"suspended"}`.
- Expected: 403. Actual (per code): update applies; `status=suspended` locks out all users.
- Fix: require tenant-admin permission; restrict `status`/`subscriptionTier` to PLATFORM_ADMIN.

### SEC-02 · Login rate limiter bypass via spoofed `X-Forwarded-For` (Medium) – FAIL
- `security/auth/LoginAttemptLimiter.java:~66-71` trusts the leftmost XFF entry; limit 20/min/IP. Production Nginx overwrites the header (`dms.conf:56`) so prod is safe if Nginx is the only path; local compose publishes `:8080` directly (`infra/local/docker-compose.yml:528`). Also: cleanup runs only when `now % 500 == 0` → map grows (SEC-09).

### SEC-03 · Keycloak direct password grant enabled and publicly proxied (Medium) – FAIL (config)
- `infra/local/keycloak/oak-dms-realm.json:69` (`directAccessGrantsEnabled: true`, public client), realm mounted in prod (`infra/production/docker-compose.yml:139`), `/realms/` proxied publicly (`dms.conf:138`). dms-core limiter and MFA flow can be bypassed by guessing directly against Keycloak; mitigated only by `bruteForceProtected: true` (lockout tuning not verified).

### SEC-04 · No JWT audience/azp validation (Medium) – FAIL
- `SecurityConfig.java:161-163`, `application.yml:84-89` (issuer/jwk only). Any realm-issued token (other clients, service accounts) is accepted; roles read from `realm_access.roles` (`JwtAuthConverter.java:35-43`). Concrete exploit unverified (service-account tokens lack a DMS user row).

### SEC-05 · Role/permission assignment has no privilege-escalation guard (Low/Medium) – FAIL (design)
- `security/rbac/service/impl/UserRoleServiceImpl.java:58-` and `RolePermissionServiceImpl.java:~60-80`: a holder of `OTUA_USERS_ASSIGN_ROLE` can assign any tenant role (including to self); only TENANT_ADMIN visibility is partially hidden (`UserRoleServiceImpl.java:200`).

### SEC-06 · Logout does not invalidate access token; client swallows logout failure (Medium, partly unverified)
- Access JWT lifetime 1800 s (realm JSON:12); refresh token revoked server-side (`KeycloakAuthClient.java:123`). Frontend `components/layout/app-topbar.tsx:138-155` ignores errors and navigates; if cookies persist, `proxy.ts` bounces `/login` → `/` (FE-M3).

### SEC-07 · Insecure default secrets in config (Low; prod protected)
- `application.yml` (DB `postgres/password`, RabbitMQ/Keycloak admin `changeme`, `changeme-…` signing secrets); local compose superadmin `SuperAdmin123@`, Grafana `admin/admin`; realm default client secret `dms-core-admin-secret`. Prod compose uses `:?` for most secrets (exceptions INF-03). Add fail-fast for `changeme` values under the docker profile.

### SEC-08 · Dev stack publishes many ports on 0.0.0.0 (Low, dev only)
- `infra/local/docker-compose.yml`: 5433, 5672, 15672, 6379, 8081, 8333, 8888, 3310, 8080, 9090, 3001, cadvisor privileged.

### SEC-09 · Login-limiter housekeeping (Low) – see SEC-02.

### SEC-10 · OCR internal endpoint minor issues (Low)
- `document/OcrInternalContentController.java`: unbounded `readsPerJob` map; 401 messages reveal which factor failed. `ocr-worker/api.py:97` uses non-constant-time token compare; default token `changeme-local-dev-token` (`config.py`); `/readyz` leaks raw DB exception (`api.py:~136`).

**Checked OK (static):** `anyRequest().authenticated()` default with a limited public list; CORS exact-origin allow-list (no wildcard); CSRF disabled with justified SameSite/httpOnly/Secure cookies; secret comparisons constant-time for scan callback/OCR token; HMAC impersonation cookie re-validated per request; password-reset token hashed, link built from config not Host; Actuator limited to health/info/metrics/prometheus and `/actuator`, `/internal/` blocked at Nginx; production Keycloak `start` with `sslRequired: external`, registration off; secrets files git-ignored (only OpenBao-encrypted file tracked).

## 8. Database Findings

### DB-01 · Hard-deleting a user fails on most FKs (High) – FAIL
- `V14` added `ON DELETE SET NULL/CASCADE` only to tables existing then; later migrations use bare `REFERENCES users(user_id)`: `documents.owner_user_id` NOT NULL (V32:82), `checked_out_by`/`legal_hold_by` (V127:21,26), `document_versions.uploaded_by` (V39:163), `notifications.recipient_user_id` (V103:92), `workflow_instances.initiated_by` (V93:36), import/export tables (V41/V42).
- `user/UserServiceImpl.java:505` `userRepository.delete(user)`; only pre-check is `hasEverSigned`; code comment claims V14 covers all tables.
- Repro: create user who uploaded a document, call `purgeUser`. Expected: purge or clear block message. Actual (per schema): FK violation.
- Fix: migration adding `ON DELETE SET NULL` for audit columns; pre-block or reassign owners/lock holders.
- **Investigation update (2026-11-19) – status unchanged: unresolved (FAIL).** An isolated remediation exists in `verification\db01-fix` and has **not** been applied to the working tree. Facts recorded so far: (a) on a disposable PostgreSQL 18.7 the original defect reproduced in 6 of 6 scenarios (document owner, version uploader, workflow initiator, notification recipient, export requester, import creator), each failing with SQLSTATE 23503, and in each the mock Keycloak `deleteUser` had already been called before the database refused (Keycloak-before-commit inconsistency); (b) the isolated schema-aware guard, flush ordering and tests passed on that disposable database, and the full backend suite ran 1,423 tests with 0 failures and 0 errors there; (c) a cross-system consistency window remains (Keycloak deleted, DMS user kept, after an audit failure or lost connection) and a durable, database-backed Keycloak deletion workflow was approved in principle but is **not implemented**. Outstanding dependencies, see §18: DB-08 (migration numbers V138 to V143 are taken upstream; V141 is occupied; V144 is the next free number to re-check at integration time); SEC-12 (`/me/provision` must refuse a purged identity); `saved_searches.user_id` (upstream V140) and V138 `share_links.created_by` are new bare foreign keys to `users`; the shared-pin policy (`document_pin_assignments.pinned_by` cascades although `docs/pin-audience-design.md` §7 says a deleted pinner's pins stay); Keycloak admin-client connect/read timeouts. Nothing here marks DB-01 fixed.

### DB-02 · Applied migrations edited in place (Medium) – unverified impact
- git history shows later edits to V7, V113, V114, V115, V119, V120, V122 (V113/V122 on 2026-11-02 after first commit). On databases that applied the old file Flyway fails checksum validation. Not verified whether such DBs exist.

### DB-03 · Insecure/lenient Flyway & destructive non-idempotent migrations (Low)
- `flyway.baseline-on-migrate: true`; V37 hard-coded platform-tenant DELETEs; V36:114 `DROP TABLE document_metadata_values`; V31 `DROP COLUMN`; rollback scripts only for V103–V114; V35 version missing (confirm intended).

### DB-04 · Soft-deleted users block email reuse (Low) – unverified intent
- `uq_users_tenant_email UNIQUE (tenant_id,email)` (V1:43) non-partial.

### DB-05 · External call (Keycloak) inside `@Transactional` (Low/Medium)
- `UserServiceImpl` (~450 disable; delete after `userRepository.delete`): Keycloak change can succeed and DB commit fail.

### DB-06 · Purge vs restore/legal-hold race (Medium) – DOC-09.

### DB-07 · Unbounded scheduler pool (Medium) – INF-I… see PERF-03.

### Messaging / outbox
- **OBX-01 · Outbox relay not multi-instance safe (High/Medium) – FAIL.** `outbox/OutboxRelay.java:65-80` + `OutboxEventRepository.findBatchToPublish` (plain `select … order by createdAt`, verified; no `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, no advisory lock). Two replicas publish the same pending rows → duplicate OCR requests.
- **OBX-02 · Events marked `published` without broker confirmation / routing (High/Medium) – FAIL.** `rabbitTemplate.send` then marks published; `RabbitMqConfig.java:100-103` admits reliance on `send()` throwing; no confirm callback, no `mandatory`; only two OCR routing keys bound → other event types dropped by the exchange yet marked published.
- **OBX-03 · Weak retry policy (Medium).** 2 s relay, no backoff, `MAX_ATTEMPTS=5`; `failed` rows never retried; whole batch of ≤100 broker sends in one DB transaction.
- **OBX-04 · Outbox table never purged; non-partial index (Medium).** No delete found for `outbox_events`.
- **OBX-05 · No metric/alert for failed outbox events or DLQ depth (Low).** `countDeadLettered` has no callers; `dms-core.q.ocr-events.dead-letter` has no consumer; `infra/monitoring/prometheus/alerts.yml:239-243` has a stale comment.
- **OBX-06 · OCR consumer poison messages retried 5× (Low).** `ocrworker/OcrEventsConsumer.java`: `UUID.fromString((String) payload.get(..))` NPE/ClassCast not treated as non-retryable (`AmqpRejectAndDontRequeueException`).

**Checked OK (static):** `ddl-auto: validate`, `open-in-view:false`, JDBC batching; no EAGER/N+1 from mappings; no unpaged `findAll()`; composite tenant FKs; tenant-scoped unique constraints; indexes on FKs/sort columns; enum CHECK constraints match Java enums; SKIP LOCKED/advisory locks in notification dispatch, import, recycle-bin purge, OCR leasing; durable RabbitMQ exchange/DLX/DLQ and exponential listener retry; Redis cache TTL and failure tolerance.

## 9. File Management Findings

### DOC-01 · Metadata & OCR-status endpoints skip document/folder access checks (High) – FAIL
> **Remediation status: FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (see §17 Remediation Log). The original finding below is kept unchanged.
- `document/DocumentMetadataServiceImpl.java:457-464` (`requireDocument` = `findById` + tenant compare; used by `getValues` :87-89 and `saveValues` :116-123); `document/DocumentServiceImpl.java:709-716` (`getOcrStatus`); routes `DocumentController.java:625-650` gated only by `DOCM_DOCUMENTS_VIEW`/`OS_OCR_EDIT_TEXT`. Comment at `DocumentServiceImpl.java:1351` says OCR entry points moved to `findVisible` – these were missed.
- Repro: user with view permission requests `GET /documents/{id}/metadata` for a document in a folder hidden from them; user with `OS_OCR_EDIT_TEXT` sends `PUT /documents/{id}/metadata` for any tenant document (including soft-deleted: `findById` ignores `is_deleted`).
- Expected: 404 / edit access required. Actual: read/write succeeds (in-tenant IDOR). Signature lock still enforced; legal hold/check-out enforced only by controller `editGuard`.
- Fix: use `findVisible(documentId, view|edit)`.

### DOC-02 · Every plain download buffers the whole file (High) – FAIL
> **Remediation status: FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (see §17 Remediation Log). The original finding below is kept unchanged.
- `document/DocumentServiceImpl.java:521-523,549-551,559`: `withDefaultWatermark` calls `content.readAllBytes()` before checking whether the tenant default watermark is enabled. Large files → OOM / `Required array size too large`; concurrent downloads multiply heap use.
- Fix: check setting first; stream when no watermark applies; cap when buffering.

### DOC-03 · No effective upload-size / ZIP-bomb limits by default (Medium–High) – FAIL
- `application.yml:54-55` multipart limits `-1`; `:688` scan max size `Long.MAX`; `:626-627` zip limits `Integer.MAX`/`Long.MAX`; V89 DB `max_file_size_bytes` Long.MAX; `scanning/service/impl/IngestionGateImpl.java:134-136,157` thus enforce nothing; `scanning/helper/ZipArchiveCheck.java` inflates every entry synchronously on the request thread; nested zips have no byte cap. Nginx `client_max_body_size 0` + `proxy_request_buffering off` (INF-04) removes the edge cap.
- Repro: upload a high-ratio `.zip`. Expected: rejected at configured limits. Actual: unbounded CPU/disk. Fix: sane defaults + ratio limit.

> **Remediation status: FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (see §17 Remediation Log; scope was narrowed after the investigation - upload size stays unlimited). The original finding above and the investigation notes below are kept unchanged.
>
> **Investigation of DOC-03 (2026-11-11) - corrections and confirmed behaviour**
> - **"No size limit" is a documented, deliberate decision, not an oversight.** `application.yml` (comment at the multipart block), `infra/production/nginx/dms.conf` (`client_max_body_size 0`), `docs/scanning-architecture.md` (V89 "no per-file upload limit any more"; ZIP "no entry-count or unpacked-size limit by default") all state files of any size are accepted and large ones are scanned in segments. The finding is therefore about missing *protective* limits around that decision, not that unlimited size is a bug.
> - **Memory is NOT at risk (measured).** The upload path streams (multipart parts over 1 MB spool to disk; staging upload, hashing, type check and ZIP check are all stream/one-pass). Probe (real `ZipArchiveCheck`, 256 MB heap): a 0.5 MB ZIP expanding to 512 MB allocated about 1 MB.
> - **Confirmed - CPU: a nested ZIP bypasses the unpacked-size limit entirely.** `ZipArchiveCheck` counts `unpacked` only for the top-level entries it inflates; ZIPs nested inside (up to `MAX_NESTING = 3`) are inflated in `blockedInside` (`ZipInputStream`, `getNextEntry` skips by inflating) with no counter, no entry limit and no ratio check. Probe, 512 MB of zeros, limit configured at 128 MB: flat ZIP (0.50 MB) -> refused at 128 MB (the limit works when set); the same payload nested 1 deep (1.2 KB file) and 2 deep (**495 bytes**) -> **VALID, counted 0 MB**, 0.8-0.9 s CPU on the request thread. Cost is linear in the hidden payload (about 0.6 GB/s of CPU), so a few hundred bytes of upload can pin a request thread for as long as the attacker likes. Layered zeros compress about a million to one, so there is no practical ceiling on the payload. (Shipped defaults are no limit at all, so flat bombs are also accepted by this step: 512 MB / 0.3 s.) ClamAV afterwards refuses what it cannot finish (`MaxScanSize` 4000M, `MaxFiles` 10000, alert-on-exceeds), so such a file is not stored as a document, but the CPU in dms-core (and in clamd) is already spent. Needs `docm.documents.create`.
> - **Confirmed - the size limit that does exist cannot be configured at runtime.** The effective limit is `scan_engines.max_file_size_bytes` (checked against the declared length and against the bytes actually received by `SizeLimitingInputStream`, so it is enforced consistently when set). It is seeded from `SCAN_MAX_FILE_SIZE_BYTES` only when no platform-default engine row exists (`ScanEngineBootstrap` returns early otherwise), migration V89 resets existing rows to `Long.MAX_VALUE`, and no controller or frontend screen edits it. So on any existing installation the env var does nothing and a cap needs a manual SQL update. `INGESTION_ZIP_MAX_ENTRIES` / `INGESTION_ZIP_MAX_UNCOMPRESSED_BYTES` and `UPLOAD_MAX_*` *are* read from the environment each start and do work (see above for the nested gap).
> - **Confirmed - no per-tenant storage quota and no upload concurrency limit** (grep: none; `ScanWorkPool` only schedules large-file scans). Disk amplification per upload: multipart spool in `java.io.tmpdir` (container writable layer, no tmpfs/size limit for dms-core) + the staged object + (ZIPs) a temp copy for the check, then the stored document - several times the file size, no ceiling.
> - **No ratio guard** on the ingestion path, although the watermark module already ships one for the same threat (`dms.watermark.*`: `min-inflate-ratio`, `max-zip-entry-size`, `max-uncompressed-bytes`, `max-zip-entries`) and `OoxmlSanityCheck` enforces an expansion quota.
> - **Frontend does not matter for this finding:** `document-upload-form.tsx` has only an `accept=` filter and no size/MIME check; the server validates independently (extension allow-list + magic bytes, ZIP structure/executable blocking), so bypassing the UI gives an attacker nothing the API does not already allow. A 413/415 from the server is shown with the server's own message.
> - **Consistent across upload paths:** new document, new version, check-in and templates all go through the same `IngestionGate`, so they share every limit and every gap. Paths with explicit, working limits: `POST /documents/duplicates/similar` (25 MB), OCR artefact uploads (per-artefact caps), resumable upload chunks (64 MB), bulk import (batch 20 GB, archive 20 GB, 5000 entries - though entry sizes are trusted, see DOC-10), WOPI save (200 MB).
> - **Existing tests:** `ZipArchiveCheckTest` covers entry-count and flat-bomb limits, damaged/empty/encrypted ZIPs, executables and a nested *program*; nothing covers nested expansion, ratio, `FileTooLargeException`/HTTP 413, `SizeLimitingInputStream`, or multipart limits.
> - **Severity re-assessed: Medium** (was "Medium-High"): authenticated users with upload permission only, memory-safe, size policy deliberate; the exploitable parts are CPU on the request thread via nested ZIPs and unbounded disk use.
>
> **Proposed fix (not implemented - awaiting approval)**
> 1. `ZipArchiveCheck`: count inflated bytes and entries for nested archives against the same limits (one shared running total threaded through `blockedInside`), and add an optional maximum inflate ratio; refuse with the existing "split it into smaller archives" message. Files: `scanning/helper/ZipArchiveCheck.java`, `scanning/helper/IngestionProperties.java` (new ratio property), `scanning/service/impl/IngestionGateImpl.java` (pass it).
> 2. Defaults in `application.yml`: finite `zip-max-entries` and `zip-max-uncompressed-bytes` that sit at what ClamAV opens of one archive (10,000 files, 4000 MB) - ZIPs past that are then refused instead of falling to the slow file-by-file path. This is a policy change, to be confirmed by you.
> 3. Make the size cap actually configurable: either have `ScanEngineBootstrap` apply `SCAN_MAX_FILE_SIZE_BYTES` to the existing platform-default row, or a one-line admin setting. Unlimited stays the default if you want it.
> 4. Out of scope unless asked: per-tenant storage quota, upload concurrency limit, tmpfs/disk limits for dms-core.
> Tests proposed: `ZipArchiveCheckTest` (nested bomb refused at the configured limit, nested entry-count, ratio, and the valid nested case still accepted); an IT for 413 on `max_file_size_bytes` (declared and streamed-undeclared); a config test that the bootstrap applies the env value.
> Operational risks: finite ZIP defaults will start refusing very large archives that are accepted today (they would have been scanned file by file); ratio limits can reject legitimately highly compressible data (logs, CSV) - hence optional and generous.

### DOC-04 · Inline serving of `.xml`/`.json` from API origin without CSP/sandbox (Medium) – NOT TESTED in browser
- `document/DocumentController.java:260-291,460-487,278-289`; `scanning/helper/FileTypeInspector.java` (xml→`application/xml`; html/svg/macros excluded); `nosniff` set. XHTML-namespaced XML may execute script in some browsers. Backend sends no CSP. Fix: force `attachment` for non-image/PDF, add `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'`.

> **Remediation status: FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (Option C; see §17 Remediation Log). The original finding above and the investigation notes below are kept unchanged.
>
> **Investigation of DOC-04 - what was verified and what was not**
> - **Routes that serve a stored file with its own MIME type and `Content-Disposition: inline`** (found by reading every controller that streams bytes): (1) `GET /documents/{id}/content?inline=true` (`DocumentController.java:260-291`), (2) `GET /documents/{id}/versions/{versionId}/content?inline=true` (`:460-487`), (3) `GET /workflow/instances/{id}/supporting-documents/{sid}/content` (`WorkflowInstanceController.java:~135-152`) - **always inline**, with no parameter; it serves a pinned version of an ordinary document through `DocumentVersionService.preview`. Not previously listed in the audit. Without `inline=true` routes 1 and 2 send `attachment`. All other routes send fixed types: `preview.pdf` (`application/pdf`, inline), page/slide/thumbnail images (PNG/JPEG), watermark/export/audit downloads (`attachment`), WOPI `GetFile` (`application/octet-stream`), OCR internal endpoints (token-protected).
> - **Content type is server-decided, not client-influenced.** It is the canonical type `FileTypeInspector` derives from the file *extension* (client Content-Type ignored) and stores on the version; the only client inputs are the `inline`/`channel` query parameters (and the original filename, sanitised in the disposition header, see DOC-15). `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` is set on routes 1-3 and the other streaming routes.
> - **What the upload gate accepts (verified with the real `FileTypeInspector`, no upload performed):** `report.xml` containing an XHTML document and `report.xml` containing an SVG document are both **ACCEPTED as `application/xml`**: the only content check for XML is "text and first non-blank character is `<`". `.html`, `.htm`, `.xhtml` and `.svg` filenames are refused by the extension allow-list, and `data.json` containing HTML is refused. (The full gate also runs ClamAV; whether it passes such a file was not tested, as nothing was uploaded to the running system.) The only allowed types a browser renders as an active document are `application/xml`; JSON, `text/plain|csv|tsv|markdown` are shown as text under `nosniff`, Office/ODF/RTF/ZIP/MSG/EML/Visio/DWG are downloaded, images and PDF are viewed passively.
> - **Browser test - CONFIRMED for XHTML and SVG (Chrome 157, headless, throwaway profile, harmless files):** a local scratch server (not the application) returned the same header set routes 1-3 send - `Content-Type: application/xml`, `Content-Disposition: inline; filename=...`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, no CSP - and top-level navigation (what opening the link does) gave: XHTML-namespaced `.xml` -> the script RAN, in the serving origin; SVG-namespaced `.xml` -> the script RAN; plain XML -> shown as an XML tree, no execution; `application/json` containing a `<script>` string -> shown as text; `text/plain` containing HTML -> shown as text. The test script only changed the page's own title/text (no data read or sent). So `nosniff` does not help: the type is already XML, and the browser picks the renderer from the XML namespace.
> - **Proposed header `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'` verified effective for the same two files:** with it added, neither script ran (XHTML and SVG shown inert).
> - **Existing controls (verified):** no `Content-Security-Policy` anywhere in the repository (backend, `infra/production/nginx/dms.conf`, `frontend/next.config.ts`); the running frontend (`localhost:3000/login`) sends no `X-Frame-Options`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, CSP or HSTS (consistent with FE-H1). The dms-core headers could not be read at runtime (nothing answered on :8080 during the check); `SecurityConfig` sets no custom headers, so Spring Security's defaults apply - **unverified**. Authorization on each route is intact (`@PreAuthorize` view permission plus `findVisible` / `requireVisible`, tenant-scoped, soft-delete aware - DOC-01/02 work), so only people who can view the document can open it.
> - **Same origin: yes in production.** `infra/production/nginx/dms.conf` serves the UI (`location /` -> Next.js) and the API (`location /api/`) on one host, so a script running from a document URL runs in the application's origin and can call every API endpoint with the viewer's session cookie (cookies are httpOnly, so the token cannot be read, but requests are authenticated; CSRF is disabled). That impact is **theoretical - not demonstrated**. In the dev setup (UI :3000, API :8080) the origins differ, but the script is on the API origin and can call the API directly.
> - **The frontend itself is not exposed:** the in-app preview fetches the file with `apiFetch` and shows XML/JSON/TXT/MD as text (`renderer "text"`, `blob.text()`), images with `<img>`, PDFs and converted Office files in an iframe from a blob URL of the PDF; it never navigates to an inline URL. The exposure is a **direct visit to the inline URL** (for example a raw API link shared with a colleague), which needs the victim to be signed in and allowed to view the document (top-level navigation sends the SameSite=Lax cookie).
> - **Preconditions for exploitation:** an attacker with `docm.documents.create`, a victim who can view the document and opens the direct link. **Not tested:** the complete attack against the running application (it would require uploading to the dev database), `attachment` behaviour in a browser (it would download a file), and Chrome's PDF viewer under CSP `sandbox` (see compatibility).
> - **Root cause:** inline responses reuse the stored type and an allowance for inline display for every type, and the gate's XML check does not look at the document's namespace, so an XHTML/SVG document can be stored as `application/xml`; no response header restricts what the browser may do with it.
> - **Severity re-assessed: Medium** (confirmed execution in Chrome, same-origin in production, but needs upload permission plus a victim opening a direct link; the app's own UI does not trigger it). It becomes more serious if raw links are routinely shared; the in-app preview would not be affected by the fix.
>
> **Options assessed (nothing implemented)**
> - **A - force `attachment` for everything but PDF and images:** stops direct-navigation rendering of XML (the file downloads instead). The in-app preview is unaffected (it uses `fetch`, and the `inline` parameter still decides the audit event: `DOCUMENT_VIEWED` vs `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED`). Alone it relies on every route using the same helper.
> - **B - `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'`:** verified effective here, and keeps files viewable. Must not be sent on PDF responses (Chrome's built-in PDF viewer is known not to work under a sandbox CSP - not tested here); harmless for images and for `fetch`/blob use by the frontend.
> - **C - A + B (recommended):** `attachment` for all inline requests except PDF and images, plus the CSP on every non-PDF response (including attachments and the supporting-documents route). Defence in depth: either one alone stops the verified behaviour.
>
> **Proposed minimal fix (not implemented - awaiting approval)**
> 1. One small helper (new class in the `document` package) that, from the stored content type and the `inline` flag, returns the disposition (inline only for `application/pdf` and `image/*`, else attachment) and adds the CSP for non-PDF types; keep `nosniff`.
> 2. Use it in `DocumentController` (routes 1 and 2) and `WorkflowInstanceController` (route 3, which today is inline for every type).
> 3. Optional, separate decision: reject XHTML/SVG-rooted content in `FileTypeInspector` (XML family). Not required once 1-2 are in place and changes what users can upload, so not proposed unless you want it.
> Files: `document/DocumentController.java`, `workflow/controller/WorkflowInstanceController.java`, one new helper class, no migration, no frontend change.
> Tests proposed: unit tests of the helper (disposition and CSP per type); MockMvc/IT checks on all three routes - `application/xml` with `inline=true` gets `attachment` and the CSP, PDF and PNG with `inline=true` stay inline without CSP on the PDF, `inline=false` unchanged, supporting-document route gets the same treatment, body and Content-Length unchanged; and a repeat of this headless-browser check against the final headers.
> Compatibility risks: an XML/JSON/TXT file opened from a raw inline URL will download instead of showing in a tab (the in-app preview is unaffected); the CSP must stay off PDF responses; confirm no integration (Collabora/WOPI uses its own route) relies on inline XML.

### DOC-05 · Signature protection inconsistent across edit paths (Medium) – FAIL (violates project rule "never edit watermarks/signatures")
- `checkout/SignedContentGuard` covers check-out/editor/check-in; but `document/DocumentVersionService.java:160-161` (upload new version) and `:177-178` (restore) call only `signatureLockService.guard` (DSI workflow lock) + `editGuard`. A standalone-signed or embedded-signature document is not locked.
- Repro: sign standalone, then `POST /documents/{id}/versions` or `/restore` → succeeds, signed state no longer current (history retained).
- `DocumentMetadataServiceImpl.saveValues`, move, delete share the DSI-only gap (do not alter bytes).
- Fix: call `signedContentGuard.guardSaving/guardOpening` in upload/restore.

> **Remediation status: FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (Option A, extended to cover embedded signatures; see §17 Remediation Log). The original finding above and the investigation notes below are kept unchanged.
>
> **Investigation of DOC-05 - verified facts, reproduced behaviour, inferences**
> - **Verified (code):** two different protections exist. (a) The *signature lock* (`DocumentSignatureLockService.guard`, a column on `documents`) is set ONLY by a workflow approval (`WorkflowSignatureListener.java:128`, `SignatureLockReconcileJob.java:41` are the only callers of `lock(...)`); standalone signing never sets it (`StandaloneSignaturePlacementService`, `docs/checkout-design.md`: "A STANDALONE signing does not set that lock, which is why rules 1-2 are needed"). (b) `SignedContentGuard` (checkout package) reads the DMS's own records - a VALID signature bound to the current version, or a `created_reason = signed_copy` version - and, when opening, the file itself for an embedded signature. (b) is used by `DocumentCheckoutService` (check-out :203, check-in :131), `EditingSessionService` (:145) and the WOPI save (`WopiController.java:246`).
> - **Verified (code): the guard is not called on the plain version routes.** `DocumentVersionService.upload` (guards at `:160-161`) and `restore` (`:177-178`) call `signatureLockService.guard` and `editGuard.guard` only; `DocumentVersionService` has no `SignedContentGuard` field at all (reflection check below). Same for `DocumentMetadataServiceImpl.saveValues` (`:121-123`), `DocumentServiceImpl.moveDocuments` (`:403`) and `deleteDocument` (`:577`). Routes: `POST /documents/{id}/versions` (needs `docm.documents.create` + edit access, `DocumentController.java:491-502`), `POST /documents/{id}/versions/{versionId}/restore` (needs `dvc.versions.restore` + edit access, `:505-510`), and the bulk-import path (`ImportRecordCreator.java:267` calls `DocumentVersionService.upload` to add a version to an existing document). Check-in reaches `upload` too, but its own `guardSaving` runs first, so check-in is protected.
> - **Reproduced behaviour (probe outside the repo, real service classes with stubbed repositories, no database - the persistence layer is a stub, so this shows the code path, not a stored-data result):** for a document whose current version has a VALID signature record and no workflow lock: `DocumentSignatureLockService.guard` (what upload/restore call) - allowed; `SignedContentGuard.guardSaving` (what check-in/editor call) - refused with "carries a digital signature on its current version"; `upload` - **allowed**, `restore` - **allowed**, and the ingestion gate was asked to add a version both times. With the workflow lock set, both are refused (`DOCUMENT_SIGNATURE_LOCKED`) and the gate is not reached. Reflection on the compiled classes: `SignedContentGuard` is a field of `DocumentCheckoutService` and `EditingSessionService` only; `DocumentVersionService`, `DocumentMetadataServiceImpl` and `DocumentServiceImpl` have only `DocumentSignatureLockService`.
> - **Not reproduced end to end:** no real standalone signing + upload against a running system (that would write to the dev database; no throwaway database was used for this investigation). Whether the new version then appears unsigned in the UI is an inference from `DocumentCheckoutService.java:234` (the signed flag is computed from the *current* version) - not observed.
> - **What actually happens to the signed bytes (verified in code):** nothing is overwritten or deleted. A new version is added and becomes current; the signed version stays in history, its signature stays valid and bound to it, and a `signed_copy` version is protected from retention purge (`VersionRetentionService.java:74`). What changes is that the document's *current* version is no longer the signed one, with no friction beyond edit access, and no signature/lock check. So this is a policy-consistency and integrity-of-"current" issue, not data loss or privilege escalation (an editor may add versions by design).
> - **Related paths, same gap, different consequence:** metadata edit, move and delete of a standalone-signed (or embedded-signature) document are also allowed. They do not change the signed bytes, so whether they should be refused is a policy question; the workflow lock does refuse them ("refuses new versions, restore, metadata edits, move and delete", `DocumentSignatureLockService` header). Embedded signatures (rule 3, made elsewhere) are also not looked at on upload/restore, because `guardOpening` is not called there.
> - **Existing tests:** `SignedContentGuardTest`, `DocumentCheckoutIT` and `WopiEditingIT` cover check-out/check-in/editor/save refusal; `DocumentSignatureLockServiceTest` covers the workflow lock. **No test covers upload, restore, bulk-import version, metadata, move or delete on a standalone-signed document.**
> - **Mitigations already present:** the signature stays valid and verifiable for its own version; version history is immutable; audit events are written for uploads/restores (not verified for this scenario); the workflow lock covers workflow-approved documents.
> - **Root cause:** the protection was built as two mechanisms for two kinds of signing, and the version-adding routes were wired to only the older one (the lock), which standalone signing deliberately does not set. `SignedContentGuard` was added later for the editing paths and not applied to upload/restore.
> - **Severity assessment: Medium is reasonable, leaning Low-to-Medium.** It violates the stated rule that all editing paths protect signatures, and is trivially reachable by any editor; but no signed content is altered or lost, and the impact is that the signed version can be superseded as "current". Prerequisites: edit access plus `docm.documents.create` (upload) or `dvc.versions.restore`.
>
> **Remediation options (nothing implemented)**
> - **A - call `signedContentGuard.guardSaving(document)` in `DocumentVersionService.upload` and `restore` (recommended minimal fix).** Same rule as check-in and the editor, cheap database checks, same `409 DOCUMENT_HAS_SIGNATURE` error. Pros: two lines plus one injected field, consistent with `docs/checkout-design.md`; check-in would simply run the same check twice. Cons: does not cover embedded signatures (as check-in does not either; `guardOpening` would add a file read per upload/restore); a bulk-import row targeting a signed document would fail that row; and there is **no supported way to remove a standalone signature** (the API only has verify/evidence and the workflow lock release), so after the fix a standalone-signed document could not receive new versions at all - the same state check-in and the editor are already in. Decide whether that is intended.
> - **B - make standalone signing set the workflow lock too (`DocumentSignatureLockService.lock`).** One mechanism for everything (also refuses metadata edits, move, delete) and an existing, audited unlock with a reason. Cons: larger behaviour change (contradicts the documented design; documents become locked after a standalone signing), the lock is set after commit by a listener, and the unlock path then must be exposed to standalone signers.
> - **C - A plus guarding metadata/move/delete with `guardSaving`.** Fully consistent but changes what users can do with signed documents beyond the finding's headline; only if the policy says signed documents are frozen entirely.
> - **D - accept the behaviour, document it, and show "signature superseded" in the UI.** No code risk; leaves the stated rule unmet.
> **Proposed files if A is approved:** `document/DocumentVersionService.java` (inject `SignedContentGuard`, call it in `upload` and `restore`), a new IT (restore/upload refused for a standalone-signed document, allowed for an unsigned one, check-in unchanged, bulk-import row reports the refusal) plus a unit test, and one sentence in `docs/checkout-design.md`. No migration, no frontend change.
> **Limitations:** no end-to-end run with real signing; the guard's behaviour for embedded signatures on these routes was read from code, not run; the audit trail and UI behaviour after a superseding upload were not observed.

### DOC-06 · Promotion doesn't re-check lock/hold/delete state (Medium) – FAIL
- `document/DocumentVersionPromoter.persist` (:81-170, `findDocument` :133): scan can complete minutes later; signature lock, legal hold, other-user check-out and soft-delete not re-checked; deleted doc → `ResourceNotFoundException` handled as generic failure.

> **Remediation status: FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (Option A; see §17 Remediation Log). The original finding above and the investigation notes below are kept unchanged.
>
> **Investigation of DOC-06 - verified facts, reproduced behaviour, inferences**
> - **Verified (code): the finding is real, and one part of it is only half right.** `DocumentVersionPromoter.persist` (`:81`) adds a new version of an existing document (`IngestionKind.new_version`, `:131-160`): it loads the document with `findDocument` (`:133`, `findByDocumentIdAndTenantIdAndDeletedFalse`), sets `latestVersionId` / `currentVersionId` (`:150-151`) and saves it (`:154`). It does **not** check the workflow signature lock, legal hold, a check-out held by someone else, or any signature record, and it takes **no row lock** on `documents`. `prepare` (`:58`, which also calls `findDocument` at `:68`) is the same. The one thing it does recheck is soft-delete: `findDocument` filters `deleted = false`, so a document deleted in the window throws `ResourceNotFoundException` - which the audit already noted is handled generically (below).
> - **Where the window comes from (verified):** the request-time guards (`DocumentVersionService.upload`/`restore` - signature lock, `editGuard`, and since DOC-05 `SignedContentGuard`) run before `IngestionGateImpl.submit`; promotion happens only after the scan. Scan results are processed (a) inline in the same request - `IngestionGateImpl.java:265` `ingestionProcessor.process(...)` - for ClamAV files up to the segment size (window = the scan time, seconds to minutes), (b) by the background worker for files over one clamd segment (`handOff`, `:261`; the ZIP "large archive" route is unreachable at the DOC-03 defaults) and cloud scanners (pending verdicts), and (c) by the retry scheduler after a failed attempt. Promotion therefore always works on whatever state the document is in by then.
> - **Reproduced (stubbed probe, no database - the REAL `DocumentVersionPromoter.persist` with stubbed repositories, run outside the repo; it shows what `persist` does with the document it is handed, not stored-data results):** free document - promoted (control); **workflow signature lock set - promoted, current version becomes the new one, and the lock still says "locked to the old version"** (an inconsistent state: locked to a signed version that is no longer current); **legal hold placed - promoted; checked out by another user (unexpired) - promoted**; soft-deleted - `ResourceNotFoundException`.
> - **What happens on the soft-delete failure (verified in code):** the exception escapes `IngestionProcessor.promote` (`:208-266`, `prepare` at `:210`, `persist` inside the transaction at `:231`) into `process`'s generic `catch (RuntimeException e)` (`:180-182`) -> `recordFailure(..., "Unexpected error: ResourceNotFoundException")`. The row is retried with backoff (`SCAN_RETRY_*`: up to 10 attempts, 30 s doubling to 15 min, `ScanProperties.java:50-52`) and **each retry scans the whole file again** before failing for good; the uploader finally sees the generic "The file could not be scanned and was not added" (`IngestionTicketMapper.java:52`), not the real reason. A copy made before `persist` is removed by the existing catch (`:252-256`), so no orphaned object (inference from the code, not run).
> - **Mitigations present:** `Document` uses `@DynamicUpdate` (`Document.java:37`), so the promotion's save writes only the columns it changed and cannot overwrite a concurrently set legal-hold, check-out or lock column with a stale value (verified in code); the lock/hold/check-out writes themselves are atomic single-statement updates; signed versions and history are never rewritten; the V39 trigger guarantees only clean-scanned bytes become versions; there is **no database trigger or constraint that refuses a version insert on a locked or held document** (checked: only the clean-scan gate and the content-immutability triggers exist).
> - **Affected paths (every route that ends in the promoter's `new_version` branch):** plain version upload and check-in (`DocumentVersionService.upload`), restore (`rollback`), bulk-import "new version" rows, WOPI/editor save (`WopiController.java:185`), template file replacement (`DocumentTemplateService.java:111`), the signature placement flows (`SignaturePlacementService.java:180`, `StandaloneSignaturePlacementService.java:126,170`, reason `signed_copy`) and a quarantine override release. They differ: a check-in/WOPI holder's own version legitimately arrives while *they* hold the check-out, and `signed_copy` versions are produced by signing itself, so a blanket re-check would be wrong for those.
> - **Existing tests:** `DocumentVersionWorkflowIT` and the fixtures exercise the promoter on the happy path (`WorkflowFixtures.newVersion`); `DocumentUploadIT`/`DocumentCheckoutIT` cover the request-time refusals. **Nothing tests promotion of a new version into a locked, held, checked-out or deleted document.** A safe future test: a database IT (throwaway DB) that builds a document with `WorkflowFixtures`, sets the lock/hold/check-out with SQL, calls the promoter exactly as `WorkflowFixtures.newVersion` does, and asserts the refusal and that no version row appears.
> - **Impact (inference, not demonstrated end to end):** a version can be added to a document that became locked, held or signed while its file was being scanned. Realistic only when the scan takes long or a lock/hold/signing lands in that window; an insider with edit access could also time a large upload (or a retry) to land after an approval or a hold - a bypass of the lock/hold rule, not a privilege escalation, with no data loss (history is kept, the older version remains). The signature-lock inconsistency above and a legal hold being changed while it is in force are the integrity/compliance consequences; the soft-delete case is a wasted-effort/misleading-message problem (up to ten full re-scans, generic final error).
> - **Severity: Medium is reasonable, leaning Low-to-Medium** (narrow race, needs an authorised uploader, no data loss, but defeats the stated lock/hold semantics and is attacker-timeable for large files).
> - **Root cause:** protection was built only at the request edge; the one place that actually creates versions (the promoter, "the only class that creates a DocumentVersion") re-reads the document but applies none of the state rules, takes no lock, and its refusals have no non-retryable outcome in the scan state machine.
>
> **Remediation options (nothing implemented)**
> - **A - authoritative re-check in `DocumentVersionPromoter.persist` (recommended).** In the same transaction, load the document `FOR UPDATE` with the existing `DocumentRepository.lockByDocumentIdAndTenantId` (`:365-367`, `PESSIMISTIC_WRITE`), refuse when it is soft-deleted, signature-locked (`signatureLockedAt != null`), under legal hold, or - for the user-initiated reasons `replacement_upload` and `rollback` only - its current version is signed by record (`SignedContentGuard.guardSaving`, which uses `document.getTenantId()`; the embedded-file check needs `TenantContext`, which the promoter deliberately never reads, and the current file's bytes cannot change in the window, so the record check is what matters). Raise a dedicated non-retryable refusal that `IngestionProcessor.promote` turns into `failTerminal` (clear reason, copied object removed, audit event) instead of the generic retry, so there are no repeated re-scans. Pros: closes the race and fixes the soft-delete re-scan/message problem with the same mechanism; one authoritative place. Cons: touches the scan state machine (`IngestionProcessor`, ticket message), needs a lock-ordering check (`scan_results` row is locked first, then `documents`; other paths that lock `documents` first must not then lock `scan_results`), and the check-out-by-another-user rule must be decided (below).
> - **B - cheaper pre-check in `prepare` only** (before the file copy). Same refusals, less wasted copying, no new locking. Does not close the window between `prepare` and `persist`, and still needs the non-retryable outcome. A weaker version of A.
> - **C - database trigger refusing `document_versions` inserts for locked/held documents.** Strongest invariant, independent of code paths. Cons: a migration, hard to express the per-reason exemptions (check-in by the holder, `signed_copy`), and error mapping through the V39 trigger path; not minimal.
> - **D - accept and document** that locks/holds are evaluated at request time. No risk; leaves the race (and the long re-scan loop for deleted documents).
> - **Recommendation: A, scoped to the absolute rules** (deleted, workflow lock, legal hold, signed-by-record for `replacement_upload`/`rollback`), exempting `signed_copy` and workflow-update versions. **Leave "checked out by another user" out of this change**: the check-in's own lock is released at acceptance (DOC-07), so refusing at promotion would silently discard the holder's file when someone else checks out in that window; that rule should be decided together with DOC-07 (keeping the lock until promotion).
> - **Proposed files if A is approved:** `document/DocumentVersionPromoter.java` (persist, and prepare for the early check), a small new exception class (document package), `scanning/service/impl/IngestionProcessor.java` (`promote`: non-retryable terminal handling), `scanning/helper/IngestionTicketMapper.java` (message for this reason, if wanted), new ITs (promotion into locked / held / signed / deleted documents, check-in and `signed_copy` still promote, no retries/no re-scan on refusal) plus unit tests, and a short note in `docs/scanning-architecture.md`. No frontend change; no migration for A or B.
> **Limitations:** no end-to-end reproduction against a database (the probe stubs the repositories; a real run was not done to avoid touching shared data); the retry behaviour and the cleanup of the copied object are read from code, not run; lock-ordering safety of option A is not verified; an unverified edge case remains where two uploads race and the second promotes over a first that carried an embedded signature (DOC-08 area).

### DOC-07 · Lost-update window after check-in (Medium) – FAIL (documented design)
- `checkout/DocumentCheckoutService.java:134-138` releases lock at `pending`; no base version recorded/compared; infected/failed scan afterwards loses the edit silently.

> **Remediation status: FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (Option B; see §17 Remediation Log). The original finding above and the investigation notes below are kept unchanged.
>
> **Investigation of DOC-07 - verified facts, reproduced behaviour, inferences, untested scenarios**
> - **Verified (code): the finding is real but narrower than "every check-in".** `DocumentCheckoutService.checkIn` (`:123-150`) calls `versionService.upload(...)` (`:134`) and then releases the check-out when `accepted(ticket.status())` (`:137-138`), where `accepted` (`:189-192`) is `pending`, `clean` or `released_override`. A `pending` ticket means no version exists yet - so the lock is dropped *before* the version does. Only a refused ticket (`infected`, `failed`, `unscannable`, `quarantined`, i.e. a 422) keeps it (`:148`). The design doc states this deliberately ("the lock is released as soon as the upload is accepted", `docs/checkout-design.md` step 3), so it is documented design, as the audit said.
> - **Reproduced (stubbed probe, no database - the REAL `DocumentCheckoutService.checkIn` with stubbed repositories, run outside the repo):** with the holder's check-out in place, a ticket status of `pending` -> check-out **RELEASED**; `clean` and `released_override` -> released; `infected`, `failed`, `unscannable`, `quarantined` -> kept. Also an existing database test pins it: `DocumentCheckoutIT.checkIn_uploadsTheFileAsANewVersion_andReleasesTheLock` (gate stubbed to return `pending`) asserts the lock is free afterwards and another user can check out; it passed (25/25) in the DOC-05 and DOC-06 regression runs.
> - **When a check-in is `pending` (verified in code, not run):** the default ClamAV path scans inline and returns `clean` or a refusal, so most check-ins have no window. `pending` is returned when (a) the scanner is unavailable or times out - `IngestionProcessor.process` catches the failure and `recordFailure` leaves the row pending with a retry (up to 10 attempts, 30 s doubling to 15 min, roughly two hours, `ScanProperties.java:50-52`); (b) the file is larger than one clamd segment, which is handed to the background worker (`IngestionGateImpl.java:258-263`); (c) a cloud scanner is configured (verdict arrives later). So the exposure is real but conditional: a ClamAV hiccup at check-in time is enough.
> - **No base version anywhere (verified):** `documents` carries only `checked_out_by/at`, `checkout_expires_at` (V127); `acquireCheckout` (`DocumentRepository.java:~28-36`) is one atomic update that records no version and ignores in-flight ingestions; `checkIn` never compares the current version with the one that was checked out. Grep of the checkout package and migrations finds no base-version column for check-out (the `*_base_version_id` columns belong to signature placement).
> - **The lost-update sequence (composition of verified pieces, NOT run end to end):** A checks in a file, ticket `pending`, lock released; B checks out the document (its current version is still the old one, since A's is not promoted); A's version is promoted - DOC-06 pins that promotion consults no other-user check-out (`DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT.checkOutOwnership_isNotConsulted_thatPolicyBelongsToDoc07`, 12/12 passing) - and becomes current; B checks in a file edited from the old version: `requireHolder` passes, nothing compares versions, and B's version becomes current. A's edits are in the history but absent from the current file, with no conflict raised to anyone. A second route to the same result needs no check-in at all: any user's plain upload that is still `pending` when someone checks the document out is promoted into a document that is now checked out by the other user.
> - **The "silently loses the edit" part (verified, partly):** if A's pending scan later ends `failed` (retries exhausted, or since DOC-06 a promotion refusal) there is no version, the lock is already gone, and nobody is told: the only scan-related notification is for a quarantined threat (`ThreatNotificationListener`); the front end shows one toast at submission ("Checked in. Your file is being scanned and becomes the new version when it is clean.", `lib/checkout/use-checkout.ts:71`) and never polls the ticket; and `DOCUMENT_CHECKED_IN` is audited at acceptance (`:143-146`) with no version id, so the audit trail says "checked in" for an edit that never became a version. The staged file is kept 72 hours for a `failed` row (`failedRetentionHours`), and the holder still has their local file, so this is loss of the *check-in*, not of the only copy.
> - **What already protects the ordinary path (verified):** an expired or taken-over lock cannot be used to check in later - `requireHolder` (`:206-221`) refuses `CHECKOUT_EXPIRED`, `DOCUMENT_CHECKED_OUT` and `DOCUMENT_NOT_CHECKED_OUT`, and `DocumentEditGuard` blocks a plain upload or restore by anyone else while the lock is held. So with a synchronous scan there is no window; the finding is about the pending window and about uploads already in flight when a check-out starts. Online editing in simultaneous mode is, by design, not exclusive.
> - **Signed, locked, held documents:** a signature lock, legal hold or signed version blocks check-out (`refuseIfFrozen`, `:195-204`) and, since DOC-05/06, refuses the upload at request time and again at promotion, so those documents cannot enter this sequence unless the state changes during the window - in which case DOC-06 now ends the ingestion as `failed` and, if the lock was already released at `pending`, the holder is not told (the silent-failure part above).
> - **Relationship to DOC-05 and DOC-06 (nothing changed here):** DOC-05's guard runs in `checkIn` (`guardSaving`) and in `upload` (`guardOpening`) - unaffected. DOC-06 deliberately left the "checked out by another user" rule out of promotion because, with the lock released at acceptance, enforcing it would discard the holder's own pending check-in the moment someone else checks out (their file would end `failed`). The two findings are therefore coupled: that promotion rule only becomes safe once the lock is kept until the version exists. The `signed_copy` exemption is unrelated to check-in.
> - **Existing tests:** `DocumentCheckoutIT` covers check-out/in, refusal by the scanner keeping the lock, expiry take-over, force release, legal hold, signature refusals, and (as above) release at `pending`; `WopiEditingIT` the editor. **Missing:** a check-in that ends `failed`/refused after the lock was released; a check-out by another user between acceptance and promotion and the resulting overwrite; an upload in flight when a check-out starts; any base-version/conflict test (nothing to test today).
> - **Severity: Medium is acceptable, leaning Low-to-Medium.** It needs an asynchronous scan (scanner outage, very large file, cloud scanner) plus a second editor acting within the window; no version is ever deleted (history keeps both), and the holder retains the local file. The consequence is a silent overwrite of "current" and a misleading audit entry, not data destruction.
> - **Root cause:** release-on-acceptance was designed for a scan that finishes inside the request; the code treats `pending` (no version yet) like success, and the lock is the only concurrency control - there is no base version to detect a conflict, and nothing tells the holder how an asynchronous check-in ended.
>
> **Remediation options (nothing implemented)**
> - **A - release the check-out when the check-in's version is promoted (best semantics).** Keep the lock on `pending`; release it in the promotion transaction, only for versions produced by a check-in. Needs a trusted marker for "this ingestion is a check-in" (the uploader alone cannot say: the online editor's saves and a holder's plain upload also come from the holder, and releasing on those would end editing sessions) - and `created_reason` is protected by CHECK constraints on `document_versions` and `scan_results` (V32/V39/V86), so a new reason such as `check_in`, or a new nullable column, needs a migration. Pros: the lock is held exactly until the version exists; the audit event can be written when it really happens; DOC-06's deferred other-user rule becomes safe to add. Cons: migration; changes `IngestionProcessor`/promoter/`DocumentCheckoutService`; DOC-06's reason handling (only `signed_copy` is special, so `check_in` would take the general rules - compatible) and `VersionRetentionService`/other readers of `created_reason` must be checked.
> - **B - keep the lock on `pending` and release it only when a version exists (recommended minimal fix).** In `checkIn`, release only for `clean`/`released_override`; on `pending` leave the check-out in place (the holder discards it, checks in again, or it expires after the idle timeout). Add the deferred promotion rule: refuse (terminal, clear message) when someone *other than the uploader* holds an unexpired check-out - safe now, because the holder's own check-in keeps the lock until promotion and the online editor already holds it. No migration. Pros: closes the window and the DOC-06 follow-up with a few lines; the sync (default) path is unchanged. Cons: after an asynchronous check-in the holder is still shown as the holder of a document whose current version is their own upload until they press Discard (or it expires) - acceptable for a rare path but a UX regression; the existing test that pins release at `pending` must be changed (needs your approval); a lock that expires during a long pending scan lets someone else take over, and the pending promotion is then refused with a clear message rather than overwriting silently; the toast text should say the check-out stays until the file is added.
> - **C - record the base version at check-out and refuse a check-in whose base is no longer current.** Optimistic-concurrency check; also catches in-flight uploads. Needs a migration (new column set by `acquireCheckout`) and care for the online editor, whose own saves legitimately advance the version during a session (the base would have to follow the holder's own versions); it detects the overwrite but not the silent `failed` outcome. Larger than B.
> - **D - observability only:** notify the uploader when an asynchronous check-in ends `failed`/refused, show a "check-in pending" state on the document, and audit the real outcome. No behaviour or schema change; leaves the overwrite window.
> - **Recommendation: B**, optionally followed by A if the extra step after an asynchronous check-in proves annoying; D is worth doing alongside either. Interaction: B does not touch DOC-05's guards, adds the other-user checkout condition inside DOC-06's `requirePromotable` (keeping the `signed_copy` exemption and the existing terminal-refusal handling), and leaves the legal-hold/lock/signature rules as they are.
> - **Proposed files if B is approved:** `checkout/DocumentCheckoutService.java` (release only when a version exists; audit timing), `document/DocumentVersionPromoter.java` (the other-user check-out rule), `frontend/lib/checkout/use-checkout.ts` (toast text), `docs/checkout-design.md` and `docs/scanning-architecture.md`, `DocumentCheckoutIT` (the existing release-at-pending test must change - needs approval) plus new ITs (pending check-in keeps the lock; the holder's own promotion succeeds; another user's pending upload is refused when a check-out starts; expired check-out; no overwrite sequence) and a unit test. No migration for B; A and C need one.
> **Limitations / untested:** the end-to-end overwrite sequence was not run (it needs a database; a safe test would use a disposable database with `DocumentCheckoutIT`'s fixtures, the gate stubbed to `pending`, and `WorkflowFixtures.newVersion` to stand in for the delayed promotion); the retry timings and the large-file and cloud-scanner routes are from code, not run; the online editor's session handling was not traced beyond confirming it takes the same lock; the front end was read, not run.

> **Investigation update (2026-11-19) – status unchanged (remains FIXED – TESTS PASSED per §17; the "no conflict detection, no automatic release" caveats still stand).** Re-reading the current code, the asynchronous check-in and promotion window still has these residual gaps (reviewer-reported; the lead auditor did not re-trace all of them, nothing was run): (1) lease identity is inferred, not stored (`leasePredatesUpload` compares `checked_out_at <= scan.started_at`), so once the lease is released nothing in the row identifies it; (2) after A's pending check-in, if B force-releases or A discards and B checks out, edits and checks in, A's late promotion finds no holder, is allowed and becomes the newest version, silently replacing B's; (3) `discard` releases the lock only and does not withdraw the pending upload; (4) nothing releases the lock after a late promotion, so others stay blocked until expiry, discard or force release; (5) the release after a synchronous clean result is a separate statement after the promotion commit and matches 0 rows if the lease expired and was re-taken, yet `DOCUMENT_CHECKED_IN` is still audited. These are recorded as evidence gaps against the fix, not as a reopened finding; see §18 for the related new items DOC-22 to DOC-24.

### DOC-08 · Version number computed outside writing transaction (Low/Medium)
- `DocumentVersionPromoter.prepare` :57-61 vs `persist`; collisions hit `uq_document_versions_document_number` (V32:131) and surface as scan failures (self-heals via retry). Redundant major/minor assignment :140-152.

> **Status: FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (2026-11-11; option A implemented, see §17 DOC-08). The investigation below is kept as written; it was recorded as INVESTIGATING before the fix. The original finding above is kept unchanged.
>
> **Investigation of DOC-08 - verified facts, reproduced behaviour, inferences, untested scenarios**
> - **Verified (code): the finding is real, and its scope is the version number, not the labels.** `DocumentVersionPromoter.prepare` (`:66-87`, read-only transaction, no lock) picks `versionNumber = latest + 1` from `findTopByDocumentIdOrderByVersionNumberDesc` (`:80-82`) and builds the object key from it (`:85-86`, `buildObjectKey` `:362`: `documents/<tenant>/<doc>/v<N>/<versionId>`). `IngestionProcessor.promote` (`:217-221`) then copies the whole file to that key - minutes for a large file - and only afterwards, in a separate transaction, `persist` inserts the row with `.versionNumber(plan.versionNumber())` (`:105`). Two promotions of the same document that overlap between their `prepare` and their commit therefore plan the same number. The table forbids duplicates: `uq_document_versions_document_number UNIQUE (document_id, version_number)` (V32:131). Since DOC-06 the second `persist` waits on the document row lock, reads the first one's committed row, takes the right *label* from it (`:153-155`, computed under the lock) - but still inserts the stale number.
> - **Reproduced (stubbed probe, no database; real `DocumentVersionPromoter`, repository stubs that report version 1 as the newest row at both prepares):** both `prepare` calls return **version number 2** (object keys `.../v2/<id-a>` and `.../v2/<id-b>`), and the two `persist` calls insert **`version_number = 2` twice** for the same document. The probe's two identical labels (`1.1`) are an artefact of the stub always returning version 1 as latest; against a real database the second would see the first's row and take `1.2`. **The unique-constraint rejection itself was NOT run** (it needs a database; see the proposed test) - it is certain from the schema text but not observed.
> - **What happens next (verified by reading, not run):** the violation surfaces when the promotion transaction flushes at commit; `promote`'s `catch (RuntimeException e)` (`:261`) deletes the copied object and rethrows; `process`'s generic catch (`:189-191`) calls `recordFailure(..., "Unexpected error: DataIntegrityViolationException")` - a counted failed attempt with backoff (30 s doubling, up to 10 attempts). The retry runs the **whole scan again** (the retry path starts at `process`), re-prepares (now N+1) and succeeds. So it self-heals, as the audit said, but each collision costs a full re-scan of the loser's file, one of its 10 attempts, a minimum 30 s delay, and the ticket meanwhile reports "The scan service is temporarily unavailable..." (`IngestionTicketMapper`, `pending` with attempts > 0). Under steady contention a large file could in principle exhaust its attempts and end `failed` (inference; not observed).
> - **Reachable in normal use, not only in tests:** bulk import adds new versions to existing documents with several rows in flight at once (`ImportRecordCreator.java:256` "several rows run concurrently"; two rows naming the same document collide); a user's plain upload overlapping their own check-in or an online-editor save; two editors in simultaneous mode; large files widen the window because the copy sits between `prepare` and `persist`. Concurrency is required - one upload at a time never collides.
> - **What cannot go wrong (verified):** no duplicate number can be stored (the constraint), so history is never corrupted; object keys cannot collide (the version id is part of the key); a refused insert leaves `current_version_id`/`latest_version_id` untouched (same transaction); version rows are never hard-deleted (no delete of `DocumentVersion` in the document or compliance code), so numbers only grow; nothing crosses a tenant or permission boundary (the key is per document and the promoter works from the scan result's tenant).
> - **Redundant assignment (verified):** `persist` sets `majorVersion`/`minorVersion` twice (`:153-155` and again `:159-160`) with identical values - dead code with a stale comment, harmless.
> - **Related effect on signing flows (inference from code, not run):** `SignaturePlacementService.java:181-186` and `StandaloneSignaturePlacementService.java:~260` require the ticket to be `clean` at once and otherwise throw `SIGNATURE_PLACEMENT_NOT_READY` ("please try again"). A number collision during such a placement leaves the stamped copy pending with a retry that promotes it later, after the user was told it did not complete - an unexpected extra `signed_copy` version can then appear.
> - **Relationship to DOC-05/06/07:** nothing in them changed or is changed by this. DOC-06's row lock already serialises the promotions and made the label correct; it did not touch the number, which is decided before the lock exists. DOC-07's lease rule and DOC-05's guard are evaluated in `persist` before the insert and are unaffected; a refused promotion never reaches the insert.
> - **Concurrent-version scenarios asked about, and what the code does:** (1) two uploads accepted against the same version both promote, in commit order - the later one becomes current, regardless of submission order, with no base-version comparison (DOC-07 limitation, unchanged); (2) a signed version becoming current while another upload is pending: a **signature record or `signed_copy` mark** on the new current version refuses the pending one at promotion (DOC-06 `protectedByRecord`, tested); (3) **gap, verified in code, not run:** a version that carries an **embedded** signature (a signed PDF uploaded as a new version is accepted) becomes current with no record, `protectedByRecord` is false, and a pending upload accepted before it is then promoted over it, although the same upload submitted afterwards would be refused (`guardOpening` reads the file at request time; promotion deliberately does not - the DOC-06 limitation note assumed an existing version's bytes cannot change, which is true of a version that already exists but not of one promoted during the window). This is a different defect from the numbering one and is outside the original DOC-08 finding; recorded here so it is not lost; (4) restore, check-in and editor saves go through the same prepare/persist path and share the numbering window.
> - **Existing tests:** none cover the numbering race - no test asserts version numbers, object-key shape or the constraint (grep of the test sources); `DocumentUploadIT.versionHistory_...` and the promotion ITs are sequential; `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` covers locks and check-outs, not numbers. Safe future test: a disposable-database IT that calls `prepare` for two scan results of one document, then `persist` both in two real transactions (latch-ordered), asserts both commit with numbers N+1 and N+2, distinct labels and the right current/latest ids - failing today with the unique violation.
> - **Severity: Low-to-Medium is right, leaning Low.** Integrity is protected by the constraint; the cost is wasted scanning, delay, a misleading ticket message and attempt budget under contention (worse for large files and bulk import), plus the signing-flow oddity above.
> - **Root cause:** the version number is a decision made outside the transaction that writes it, before the lock that serialises the writers, and it is also baked into the object key chosen before the copy.
>
> **Remediation options (nothing implemented)**
> - **A - assign the number in `persist`, under the row lock (recommended).** Compute `latest.versionNumber + 1` where the label is already computed (`:147-155`), use it for the insert, and keep the number in `prepare` only as the hint in the object key (the key is just a path - rows store their own `storage_object_key`, so a hint that differs from the final number is harmless, and nothing parses it). Also delete the duplicate major/minor assignment. Pros: a handful of lines in one file, no migration, no extra query (`persist` already reads `latest`), closes the race completely, no collision means no rescan or wasted attempt; independent of DOC-05/06/07. Cons: the `v<N>` folder in a new object's key may differ from its version number after a race (cosmetic; the key stays unique through the version id); `PromotionPlan.versionNumber` becomes advisory and its doc should say so.
> - **A2 - as A, and drop the number from newly written keys** (`.../<doc>/<versionId>`). Cleanest, removes the mismatch; changes the key layout for new objects only (existing rows keep theirs; storage tooling or documentation that assumes `v<N>/` would need checking), so slightly wider than A.
