> - **Impact.** No tenant crossing (composite foreign keys keep a folder's parent and documents inside its tenant) and no authorization bypass: every path requires an authenticated, authorized caller (`manage` to move or delete, `edit` to create or file). No document content, signature, legal-hold, retention or check-out state is touched. The effects are **availability and integrity of the folder tree**: folders (and the documents in them) that vanish from navigation until repaired by hand, a possibly unbounded database query once a cycle exists, a folder deeper than the rule, a live folder under a deleted one. Triggering needs two requests in the same few-millisecond window (two users reorganising at once, or a script that repeats opposite moves), so it is a rare accident or a deliberate nuisance by a user who can already manage both folders. **Severity: Low likelihood, Medium worst-case effect (the non-terminating query); I would keep it Low unless you weigh the unbounded query, in which case Low/Medium.**
> - **Existing mitigations.** Sibling-name index (above); "not its own parent" check constraint; same-tenant foreign keys; `treePath` and `depthBelow` stop at the depth limit and `treePath` has a visited-set; `countPendingUploads` for long uploads; moves and deletes need `manage`.
> - **Existing tests.** `FolderServiceTest` (mock repository: cycle refusal by returning a canned subtree, names, depth, empty-only delete); `DocumentUploadIT` uses folders only incidentally. **No concurrency test and no real-database test of the tree rules exists**, so the suite would pass with every race above present.
> - **Interaction with completed fixes.** None of SEC-01 and DOC-01..DOC-13 touches `FolderService`. DOC-06/DOC-08 (lock the document row, re-check under the lock) and DOC-09 (lock first, then decide) are the relevant lessons: decide under a lock, not before it. DOC-11 applies in reverse: the new lock must not be held across storage I/O (folder operations do none). The external-sharing work is editing `Folder.java` and `ConfidentialService`/`FolderAccessService` (they save folder rows for access mode / confidential); this finding does not need those files.
> - **Remediation options.**
>   - **A - one transaction-scoped advisory lock per tenant for folder structure changes (recommended).** `create`, `move` and `delete` first take `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('folders:' || tenantId, 0))` (one small native query), then run their existing checks, which now read the committed result of any earlier structure change; the lock is released at commit/rollback. Closes (1), (2) and (3). No migration, no schema change, no change to responses or audit events; folder structure writes are rare and short (no I/O), so per-tenant serialisation costs nothing measurable. Cons: PostgreSQL-specific (the system already is); does not by itself cover (4) or writers outside `FolderService`.
>   - **B - lock exactly the affected rows in a fixed order** (the moved folder and the new parent's ancestor chain, sorted by id, `FOR UPDATE`/`FOR SHARE`), then re-run the cycle/depth checks. Finer grained (unrelated moves do not wait), but more code, a lock-ordering argument to maintain, and the ancestor chain itself can change while being locked; the extra concurrency is not needed for a rarely used operation.
>   - **C - a database constraint trigger** that rejects a parent change that would form a cycle or exceed the depth, taking the same advisory lock inside the trigger. The strongest backstop (covers every writer), but it needs a migration (not allowed without approval), is harder to test and to evolve, and still needs the application checks for good error messages.
>   - **D - make the cycle query safe** (`UNION` instead of `UNION ALL`, or a `CYCLE` clause / depth bound) in `findSubtreeIds`. Cheap and independent; it does not prevent cycles but guarantees the move check and the export cannot spin forever on one. Recommended together with A as defence in depth; also lets a cycle be detected and reported.
>   - **E - do nothing / document.** Not recommended: the one-line cycle check gives a false sense of safety and the failure is silent.
> - **Recommendation: A + D.** A removes the check-then-act gap for all three folder-structure races with the smallest, migration-free change; D makes any cycle that exists (or ever appears by other means, or came from this race before the fix) harmless to the move check and to bulk export. (4) should be handled as a separate, small follow-up on the document side (the promoter and `moveDocuments` taking the folder row `FOR SHARE` and checking `NOT is_deleted`, and `delete` taking the row `FOR UPDATE` before it counts) rather than by serialising promotions on the tenant lock, which would be far too coarse. C only if a database-level guarantee is wanted later.
> - **Proposed boundaries if A + D is approved.** `folder/FolderService` (a private `lockTree(tenantId)` called first in `create`, `move`, `delete`; `rename` needs none: names are index-backed) and one native lock query (in `FolderRepository`) plus the `findSubtreeIds` SQL; no change to `Folder`, `FolderAccessService`, `ConfidentialService`, the controller, the schema or any response/audit; the document-side changes in (4) are a separate step.
> - **Required tests if approved (real PostgreSQL; deterministic - the probe's row-lock technique, no sleeps).** Opposite moves concurrently -> exactly one succeeds, the other gets `FOLDER_MOVE_INTO_ITSELF`, no cycle in the table. Two moves that together exceed depth 20 -> the second is refused `FOLDER_TOO_DEEP`. Create under a parent being deleted -> either the create is refused (404) or the delete is refused (`FOLDER_NOT_EMPTY`), never a live child under a deleted parent. Same-name concurrent creates/moves -> one 409 `FOLDER_NAME_TAKEN`, one folder. The cycle query terminates on a seeded cycle (direct SQL) and reports it. Lock scope: two tenants' folder operations do not block each other; a failing operation rolls back and releases the lock; the lock is taken before the first read (unit test on the call order). Compatibility: `FolderServiceTest`, the folder-access ITs, `DocumentUploadIT`, bulk-import and bulk-export folder tests, audit events unchanged, tenant isolation (SEC-01) and folder access levels (view/edit/manage) unchanged.
> - **Compatibility risks.** A lock wait holds a pooled connection for the length of another folder operation (milliseconds; the pool is 10): acceptable, and a `lock_timeout` on that statement can bound it. A very large tenant tree makes the cycle query slower, as today. Moves performed outside `FolderService` (none found: the only writers are `FolderService`, `FolderAccessService` for access mode and `ConfidentialService` for the confidential flag) are not covered.
> - **Unverified / not run.** The document-side race (4) and `moveDocuments` against `delete`; how the frontend renders a cycle or a folder under a deleted parent; the real service call on a cycle (bounded by timeout instead of run to completion); behaviour under real HTTP concurrency; existing production data (a query for cycles and live children of deleted parents should be run before any fix: `select` with a recursive CTE with cycle detection, read-only). The probe ran in a private copy against a disposable database and has been removed; it is not part of the repository.

### DOC-15 · Content-Disposition sanitisation incomplete (Low)
- `DocumentController.java:528-533` strips only CR/LF/`"`; trailing backslash, non-Latin-1 chars; no RFC 5987 `filename*`. CRLF injection itself is blocked.

> **Remediation status: FIXED – TESTS PASSED** for the confirmed header defect (2026-11-12; option A implemented, see §17 Remediation Log). Verified by the unit and real-Tomcat wire tests and by the database-backed integration regression (419/419 passed, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped; see §17). The web client still shows the ASCII fallback name for non-Latin file names (separate frontend follow-up). The investigation below is kept as written; it was recorded as INVESTIGATING before the fix. The original finding above is kept unchanged.
>
> **Investigation of DOC-15 - labelled: CONFIRMED FROM SOURCE / CONFIRMED BY TEST / INFERRED / NOT VERIFIED**
> - **Conclusion: PARTIALLY CONFIRMED, and materially different from (and worse than) the original wording.** The specific mechanisms named in the finding are weaker than described (no header injection, no reachable trailing-backslash break-out), but the "no RFC 5987 `filename*`" part is confirmed and has a concrete, serious consequence the finding did not name: **for any file name containing a character above U+00FF - which includes every Sinhala and Tamil name - Tomcat silently drops the whole `Content-Disposition` header.** The response is `200` with no filename and no `attachment`.
> - **The code (line numbers as of 2026-11-12; the report's `:528-533` has moved to `:529-535`).** `DocumentController.sanitizeForHeader` (`:529-535`) replaces only CR, LF and `"` and returns `"download"` for null/blank; it is used to hand-build `"<inline|attachment>; filename=\"" + name + "\""` at `downloadDocument` `:286-288`, `previewPdf` `:316`, `previewVersionPdf` `:456` and `downloadVersion` `:484-486`. The same helper is **duplicated** in `WorkflowInstanceController.java:166-171` (used at `:151-152`, supporting-document content). The names come from `DocumentVersion.getOriginalFilename()` (`VersionContent.java:69`), or are derived from it (`DocumentRenditionService.baseName`/`pdfName` `:825-832`, `... (watermarked).pdf` `:380`); the stored name is the uploader's raw `MultipartFile.getOriginalFilename()` (`DocumentController.java:138`, `:500`; column `VARCHAR(500)`, V32/V39) - nothing normalises it. The type check only requires an allowed extension after the last `.` (`FileTypeInspector.extensionOf` `:263-274`, which treats `\` as a path separator). So a user who may upload controls the file name that other users' downloads put into the header.
> - **What was tested (private copy, no database: the real `sanitizeForHeader` by reflection, an embedded Tomcat 11.0.25 as pinned in `pom.xml:35`, raw socket reading of the response header bytes, Spring's `ContentDisposition`, and the frontend's own parsing regex run in Node).**
>   - **CONFIRMED BY TEST - header dropped for non-Latin-1 names.** `ගොනුව.pdf`, `கோப்பு.pdf`, an emoji name and a name with a right-to-left override all produced `HTTP/1.1 200` with **no `Content-Disposition` header at all** (no exception, no log). Consequence 1: the browser gets no filename. Consequence 2: the `attachment` disposition that DOC-04 relies on for non-passive types (`ContentServingPolicy.dispositionType`: only PDF and raster images may be `inline`) is lost for these names; the other DOC-04 controls (canonical Content-Type, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'` for non-PDF) are separate headers and stay. Allowed upload types include `txt, csv, tsv, md, xml, json`, which a browser would then show inline in a sandboxed tab instead of downloading (INFERRED from the header behaviour; not tested in a browser).
>   - **CONFIRMED BY TEST - UI effect.** The frontend (`frontend/lib/documents/api.ts:439-441` and `:488-490`, `preview/api.ts:174-176`, `watermarks/api.ts:283-285`) falls back to the name `"document"` when no filename is found, so a Sinhala/Tamil file downloaded from the UI is saved as `document` with no extension (frontend regex run on the missing header: fallback; not exercised in a real browser).
>   - **CONFIRMED BY TEST - names with `%`, `;`, `%20`.** The same regex plus `decodeURIComponent` throws `URIError` for `Report 100%.pdf` (the download fails in the UI), silently renames `50%20off.pdf` to `50 off.pdf`, and truncates `a;b.pdf` to `a`. These are frontend parsing defects triggered by the server not encoding the name; they exist today for any such name.
>   - **CONFIRMED BY TEST - other characters.** Latin-1 letters (`résumé.pdf`) go out as single raw ISO-8859-1 bytes (`0xE9`), not UTF-8 (how each browser reads that was not tested). Controls other than tab become spaces in Tomcat's writer (tab kept); the C1 control U+0085 passes through. Path separators and traversal text (`..\..\evil.pdf`, `../../etc/x.pdf`) pass through unchanged (browsers strip them; some command-line clients historically did not - not tested). A backslash survives, and a quoted-pair parser drops it (`a\b.pdf` -> `ab.pdf`): cosmetic.
>   - **NOT REPRODUCED - header injection and quote break-out.** CR/LF/`"` are replaced (`a"b.pdf` -> `a_b.pdf`; confirmed), Tomcat does its own control-character handling, and nothing follows the `filename` parameter, so a backslash cannot escape anything useful. **The "trailing backslash" case is not reachable** through these paths: a stored name must end with an allowed extension (`x.pdf\` has extension `pdf\` and is refused at upload), and every derived name ends in `.pdf`. The finding's "CRLF injection itself is blocked" is confirmed.
> - **Which paths are affected.** Hand-built, same defect: the four `DocumentController` sites and `WorkflowInstanceController:151`. Same class, different form: `WatermarkController:187-189` uses `ContentDisposition.attachment().filename(name)` with no charset (the comment above it says RFC 5987 encoding is applied; it is not - Spring prints a raw non-ASCII name, which Tomcat then drops), for a copy named after the document. Correct already: `AuditEventController:120-121` and `ExportJobController:115-118` (`filename(name, UTF_8)`). Fixed server-chosen ASCII names, unaffected: `BulkImportController:210-211` (template files), `OwnershipHistoryService:127`. Reference implementation of the right shape (do not edit - another developer's external-sharing work): `sharing/SharedFileResponse.java:33-36` (ASCII fallback plus `filename*=UTF-8''...`).
> - **Impact and severity.** No tenant, authorization, signature, retention or storage effect; no code execution (the CSP sandbox and `nosniff` stay). The effects are (1) **functional**: every document whose name is in the product's own languages is delivered without a name (and, from the UI, as an extension-less `document`) - high likelihood, not an edge case; (2) **defence in depth**: the `attachment` disposition is lost for those names on non-passive types; (3) a UI download failure for names containing `%`. Original severity Low; **I recommend raising it to Medium** (Low for security, Medium for the certain, locale-wide functional failure and the DOC-04 interaction), your call.
> - **Existing tests.** `ContentServingPolicyTest` (disposition type only); `ContentServingPolicyIT`, `PreviewApiIT` (MockMvc), `SupportingDocumentContentHeadersIT`, `DocumentDownloadStreamCleanupIT` (direct controller calls / MockMvc) assert the header only for plain ASCII names such as `evidence.pdf`. None goes through a real servlet container, so none can see Tomcat dropping the header, and none uses a non-ASCII, quote, backslash, `%` or path name: **the suite passes with the defect present.**
> - **Remediation options.**
>   - **A - one shared helper that builds the whole header, applied to every site (recommended).** `ContentDispositionHeaders.of(type, filename)` (one small class, e.g. in `common/web` or next to `ContentServingPolicy`): (1) normalise the name - take the last path segment after `/` or `\`, drop control characters (C0, DEL, C1), bidi and invisible format controls (U+200B-U+200F, U+202A-U+202E, U+2066-U+2069, U+FEFF), trim, cap the length, fall back to `download`; (2) emit `type; filename="<ASCII fallback>"; filename*=UTF-8''<RFC 5987 percent-encoding>` where the fallback replaces everything outside `[A-Za-z0-9._ -]` with `_` (so `%` and `;` cannot reach a naive parser) and the encoded form keeps the real name; the whole header is pure ASCII, so Tomcat can never drop it. The disposition type still comes from `ContentServingPolicy.dispositionType` (DOC-04 unchanged). Replaces the two private `sanitizeForHeader` copies and `WatermarkController`'s builder call. No migration, no API or schema change; existing ASCII names produce `filename="report.pdf"; filename*=UTF-8''report.pdf`. **Needs a follow-up in the frontend** (separate approval): one shared parser that prefers `filename*` (RFC 5987-decoded, never throwing) over `filename`, used by the four `api.ts` files - without it the UI keeps showing the ASCII fallback (e.g. `___.pdf`), which is still better than today's extension-less `document` and removes the `URIError`, but does not restore the real name.
>   - **B - sanitiser only: restrict the existing helper to printable ASCII, no `filename*`.** Smallest change; stops the header being dropped, and keeps `attachment`, but turns every Sinhala/Tamil name into underscores for every client (no real name anywhere). A stop-gap, not a fix of the stated finding (no RFC 5987).
>   - **C - frontend-only (derive the name from document metadata).** Fixes the UI but not API clients, previews, other callers, nor the dropped `attachment`. Not recommended.
>   - **D - leave as is, document.** Not acceptable: certain failure for the product's own languages.
> - **Recommendation: A, then the frontend parser as its own small step.** A is local, testable without a database, and closes the finding's gap and the dropped-header defect together; B only as an emergency measure.
> - **Proposed boundaries if A is approved.** New helper class and its unit tests; `DocumentController` (four sites + delete `sanitizeForHeader`), `WorkflowInstanceController` (one site + delete its copy), `WatermarkController` (one site). **Not** `SharedFileResponse` (another developer's), `AuditEventController`/`ExportJobController` (already correct; may adopt the helper later), the frontend (separate step), `ContentServingPolicy`, the sandbox/nosniff headers, authorization, storage, schema.
> - **Required tests if approved.** Unit (table-driven): quote, backslash, quote+backslash, `..\`, `../`, a path-only name, tab/NUL/DEL/C1, bidi override, lone surrogate and emoji, Latin-1, Sinhala, Tamil, a 500-character name, `%`, `;`, `'()*`, null/blank; each result is pure ASCII, contains no CR/LF, round-trips through Spring's `ContentDisposition.parse` to the normalised name (via `filename*`), and the fallback has only safe characters. **Wire test through a real embedded Tomcat** (the probe's technique): for every case the `Content-Disposition` header is present, ASCII-only, starts with the intended disposition, and carries the name - this is the test that fails today. Endpoint tests (MockMvc is enough for routing, plus one real-server test): `downloadDocument` (inline and not), `previewPdf`, `previewVersionPdf`, `downloadVersion`, the workflow supporting-document endpoint and the watermark download with a Sinhala name keep their disposition type, CSP sandbox and `nosniff` (DOC-04), authorization and tenant isolation unchanged (existing ITs). Frontend (Node/Vitest) tests for the parser step. Regression: `ContentServingPolicyIT`, `SupportingDocumentContentHeadersIT`, `DocumentDownloadStreamCleanupIT`, `PreviewApiIT`, DOC-02 streaming and DOC-04 tests.
> - **Compatibility risks.** Clients that read only `filename=` now get the ASCII fallback for non-ASCII names (an improvement on "no header"). Clients reading `filename*` get the true name. The frontend's current regex takes the first `filename...` match, which is the fallback - harmless but not the true name until the frontend step. Header length grows by the encoded name (the 500-character cap keeps it well under typical 8 KB header limits).
> - **Interplay with other findings.** DOC-04 (inline restrictions): the dropped header weakens the `attachment` rule for non-passive types but the compensating headers remain; DOC-02 (streaming) and DOC-16 (older-version download watermark) use the same endpoints but are not touched; DOC-18 (stream not closed) unrelated. No overlap with the DOC-09..DOC-14 tracked risks.
> - **Not verified.** Behaviour in real browsers (inline rendering of `txt/xml/json` without `attachment`; how `0xE9` raw bytes and a missing header are handled by each browser); command-line clients and path traversal; the production reverse proxy (nginx passes upstream headers, but a proxy that rewrites or rejects non-ASCII headers was not examined); the database-backed endpoints end to end (the environment was unavailable - WSL and the Postgres container were down, so only the header construction was tested, not an upload followed by a download). The probe ran in a private copy with no database and has been removed; it is not part of the repository.

### DOC-16 · Older-version download skips default watermark (Low) – intent unverified
- `DocumentController.java:460-487`, `DocumentVersionService.download`.

### DOC-17 · "Protected view" not enforced on raw `/content` (Info)
- Documented as design for users with download permission (`PreviewPolicyService`).

### DOC-18 · Stream not closed if response never written (Low)
- `DocumentController.downloadDocument` (:260-291); `importexport/.../ExportArchiveBuilder.addFile` (:239-241).

### DOC-19 · Download audited at open, not completion (Info)
- `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` logged before bytes stream.

### DOC-20 · Zip-slip / path traversal / storage keys – PASS (static)
- UUID-built keys independent of filename; `FileSystemStorageAdapter.resolveAndValidate` normalises+`startsWith`; export names via `safeName`.

**Also PASS (static):** extension allow-list + magic-byte family match, client Content-Type ignored; download routes use `findVisible`/`require` (404 for hidden); scan gate serves only clean/admin-overridden bytes with SHA-256 verified promotion; atomic checkout UPDATE with expiry; WOPI re-checks live document per call and refuses embedded-signature saves; stamping/watermark refuse already-signed files; export re-applies access as requester; resumable upload offsets validated; duplicate-upload race ordered.

## 10. Frontend Findings

### FE-H1 · No security headers in Next.js (High) – FAIL
- `frontend/next.config.ts` has no `headers()`: no CSP, `X-Frame-Options`/`frame-ancestors`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, HSTS. App can be framed (clickjacking). Repro: `curl -I` any page. Fix: add headers (Grafana proxy route needs same-origin frame exception).

### FE-M1 · Login discards `?from=` deep link (Medium) – FAIL
- `proxy.ts:31-34` sets `from`; `app/login/login-page-content.tsx:204` always `router.push("/")`. Fix: validate same-origin path (reject `//`, `/\`) then push.

### FE-M2 · Monitoring proxy forwards user cookies/Authorization to Grafana (Medium) – FAIL
- `app/monitoring/panels/[...path]/route.ts` ~82, ~150: `SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS` omits `cookie`, `authorization`, `x-forwarded-*` → `dms_access_token`/refresh cookies sent to Grafana (whether Grafana logs them is unverified). Fix: allow-list headers; send only `X-WEBAUTH-USER`.

### FE-M3 · Logout swallows errors; stuck on login redirect loop (Medium, partly unverified)
- `components/layout/app-topbar.tsx:138-155` (`catch {}`) + `proxy.ts` cookie-presence redirect.

### FE-M4 · Admin route gating is client-side only; user activity page ungated (Medium)
- `proxy.ts` checks only cookie presence (no validation); `app/admin/users/[userId]/activity/page.tsx:27-60` has no `PermissionGate`. Data is backend-enforced, so UX gap not a leak. Other `admin/users/[userId]/*` subpages not checked.

### FE-L1 · Public-path `startsWith` matching (Low)
- `proxy.ts:6,19`: `/login-anything`, `/error-foo` treated as public (redirect bypass only, no data impact).

### FE-L2 · Upload UI has no size/MIME validation (Low)
- `components/documents/document-upload-form.tsx:375` (`accept` only; bypassable); `:472` raw byte formatting vs `formatBytes` elsewhere. Large file transfers fully before 413/415.

### FE-L3 · ESLint warnings (Low)
- `components/layout/nav-progress.tsx:71` exhaustive-deps; `signatures/sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx:117`, `sign-document-dialog.tsx:81` `watch()` incompatible-library.

### FE-L4 · Scan upload default document type not initialized (Low) – FAIL (found 2026-11-16; remediated, see §17)
- **Found during the FE-L2 isolated verification** (2026-11-16), not part of the original audit. Affected file: `frontend/components/documents/document-upload-form.tsx` (the Scan Documents page, `/documents/scan`).
- **Reproduction:** open `/documents/scan`; the Document type dropdown displays the default ("General", or the first active type). Choose a valid PDF or image: **Upload stays disabled** (the file name is shown, nothing is wrong with the file). Change the dropdown to another type, or to a different type and back, and Upload enables. Confirmed in a browser against the live build and the isolated build, with the same result each time.
- **Root cause:** inconsistent use of the raw and the derived value. `documentType` state starts as `""` (`:114`); the dropdown shows `effectiveDocumentType = documentType || preferredType?.typeCode` (`:159`), so it displays a default that the state does not hold. The Upload button (`disabled={!file || !documentType ...}`, `:494`) and `handleUpload` (`if (!file || !documentType) return` at `:274` and `typeCode === documentType` at `:283`) still read the empty raw state. Selecting the option that is already displayed does not fire a change event in a native select, so the person has to pick another type first.
- **User-visible impact:** the primary action looks ready but is disabled with no explanation; a person who does not touch the dropdown cannot upload. A workaround exists (pick another type and back). No data, security or backend impact: dms-core is unchanged and validates every upload.
- **Proposed remediation (smallest):** make the button, `handleUpload` and its `find` use `effectiveDocumentType` (the value the person sees) in place of the raw `documentType`. Alternative: seed the state from the loaded default.
- **Required tests:** a component test that, with a loaded catalogue and a valid file, Upload is enabled without touching the dropdown and `uploadDocument` receives the default type's id; that choosing another type still wins; that no type available (empty catalogue) keeps Upload disabled; that the loading state keeps it disabled; and a browser check on `/documents/scan`. The FE-L2 component tests select a type explicitly because of this and should keep passing.
- **Related:** FE-L2 (same form; its validation work does not change this behaviour). Not fixed by FE-L2.

### FE-I1 · Stale copy / fragile not-found handling (Info)
- `app/admin/users/[userId]/activity/page.tsx:20-24,29` (`error.message.includes("not found")` instead of `ApiError.status === 404`; recent activity always empty until an endpoint exists).

**PASS (static):** tokens only in httpOnly cookies (no localStorage tokens); single-flight refresh with one retry; no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; no user-controlled redirects; `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` has no secrets; `.env*` ignored; no `console.log`/TODO in app code; dev OTP shortcut gated by `NODE_ENV`; print iframe uses blob URLs (not exploitable). **NOT TESTED:** responsive layout, accessibility, per-page empty/error states, duplicate API calls (light sampling only), browser console.

## 11. Security Audit Findings (OWASP view)

| OWASP | Result |
|---|---|
| A01 Broken access control | FAIL – SEC-01, DOC-01, SEC-05, API-06 |
| A02 Cryptographic failures | PASS (static): at-rest envelope encryption, hashed reset tokens; secure cookies. TLS absent in prod Nginx config (INF-01) |
| A03 Injection | PASS (static) SQLi/header injection; **FAIL** CSV formula injection in audit export (AUD-01); ownership CSV unverified (NOT TESTED) |
| A04 Insecure design | FAIL – DOC-05, DOC-07, SEC-03 |
| A05 Misconfiguration | FAIL – FE-H1, INF-01..04, API-07/08, SEC-07 |
| A06 Vulnerable components | NOT TESTED (no dependency scan run) |
| A07 Auth failures | PARTIAL – SEC-02, SEC-03, SEC-04, SEC-06 |
| A08 Integrity | PASS (static): SHA-256-verified promotion, hash-chained audit |
| A09 Logging/monitoring | PARTIAL – OBX-05; no password/token logging found |
| A10 SSRF | NOT TESTED (webhook/integration code not reviewed in depth) |

### AUD-01 · Audit CSV export formula injection (Medium) – FAIL
- `audit/service/impl/AuditEventServiceImpl.java:147-155`: `csvField` only RFC4180-quotes; `resourceName`, `actorDisplayName`, `failureReason` starting with `= + - @` exported raw. Repro: title a document `=HYPERLINK("http://x","a")`, export, open in Excel. (Document export was reviewed and neutralises injection – PASS.)

### AUD-02 · Audit export silently truncates at 10,000 rows (Medium) – FAIL
- `AuditEventServiceImpl.java:47,103` `PageRequest.of(0, EXPORT_ROW_LIMIT)`; no truncation indicator; compliance exports look complete.

### AUD-03 · Global advisory lock serialises all audit writes (Info)
- `audit/AuditService.java:132` – by design for the hash chain; throughput limit across tenants.

### NOT-01 · Deliveries stuck in `sending` forever (High) – FAIL
- `notification/dispatch/DeliveryClaimRepository.java` claim SQL selects only `WHERE status = 'pending' AND next_attempt_at <= now() AND (locked_until IS NULL OR locked_until < now())` and sets `status='sending'` (verified). Nothing resets expired `sending` rows; `NotificationDispatcher.java:74` comment ("the lease expires and it is retried") is incorrect. Admin retry accepts only failed/skipped (`NotificationAdminServiceImpl.retry`).
- Repro: kill the instance mid-send or throw before the result transaction. Expected: re-claim after lease. Actual: email never sent, UI cannot recover.
- NOT-02 (Medium): mail sent outside a tx; failing result-tx leaves row stuck and, once NOT-01 is fixed, gives at-least-once delivery/duplicate emails (`NotificationDispatcher.java:95-125`).

### DEP-01 · Department delete ignores documents & retention policies (Medium) – FAIL
- `department/service/impl/DepartmentServiceImpl.java:173-186` checks only members, children, workflow-stage participants; `documents.owner_department_id` FK (V123), retention scope `department` and `holding_owner_user_id` reference it. Expected 409 `DEPARTMENT_IN_USE`; actual 204 with dangling references. DEP-02 (Low): N+1 `countMembers` per department (`:71,:303`), page size up to 2000.

### INF-01 · Production Nginx is plain HTTP, no security headers, no rate limiting (Medium) – FAIL (TLS may terminate upstream – unverified)
- `infra/production/nginx/dms.conf:33-34`; session cookie `Secure=true` (`application.yml:155`) would not be stored over `http://`.
### INF-02 · No log rotation in any compose (Medium) – `infra/production|local/docker-compose.yml` (no `logging:` max-size).
### INF-03 · Empty Postgres/Redis passwords allowed in prod compose (Medium) – `infra/production/docker-compose.yml:~39,~87-88` (`${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:-}`, `--requirepass "${REDIS_PASSWORD:-}"`); `EDITING_TOKEN_SECRET:-` (:388) blank disables editing.
### INF-04 · Unbounded request body at Nginx (Medium) – `client_max_body_size 0`, `proxy_request_buffering off` (see DOC-03).
### INF-05 · Unpinned images (Medium) – `collabora/code:latest` (prod :288, no healthcheck, `cap_add: MKNOD`), `chrislusf/seaweedfs:latest`, `alpine:latest` (local :159,:213).
### INF-06 · cadvisor privileged with host `/` and docker socket (Low); exporters lack healthchecks/`user:`.
### INF-07 · Local compose empty correlation secret possible (Low) – `AUDIT_CORRELATION_SECRET: ${AUDIT_CORRELATION_SECRET}` without `:?`.
### INF-I1 · `/api/v1/monitoring/logs` exposes all containers' logs (Info) – gated by `SHAD_VIEW_SYSTEM_LOGS`; whether tenant roles can hold it is unverified.
### INF-I2 · Empty modules `search/`, `tagging/`, `reporting/`, `integration/` (Info).
### INF-I3 · Dashboard heavy per-user scan without `@PreAuthorize` (Info) – ~16 s at 3.7M documents per code comments, cached 60 s per user.

## 12. Performance & Reliability Findings

- **PERF-01 (High)** Whole-file download buffering – DOC-02.
- **PERF-02 (Medium–High)** Unbounded upload/ZIP inflation – DOC-03/DOC-10.
- **PERF-03 (Medium)** Scheduler pool `spring.task.scheduling.pool.size: ${SCHEDULING_POOL_SIZE:2}` (`application.yml`) with ~19 `@Scheduled` jobs incl. the 2 s OutboxRelay; long jobs (retention, recycle purge) can starve the relay (`WatermarkOutputSweeper` comment acknowledges this). Hikari pool 10 – adequacy unverified.
- **PERF-04 (Low/Medium)** Heap buffering in WOPI save/preview – DOC-12.
- **PERF-05 (Low)** `OcrBacklogSweeper`, `UploadSessionService` purge, `BulkExportService` purge, `DmsMetricsGauges` have no lock (idempotency unverified); correct locking found elsewhere (RetentionJob, FingerprintBackfill, PageCountBackfill, RecycleBin, KeyRotation, WatermarkOutputSweeper, OcrFieldsReconciler, notification claim, WorkflowSla, import, scan).
- **PERF-06 (Low)** Outbox table growth + non-partial index – OBX-04.
- Resource config PASS (static): prod compose `mem_limit` + `restart: unless-stopped` on all services; non-root, HEALTHCHECK on dms-core and ocr-worker images.
- Load/soak/memory profiling: **BLOCKED**.

## 14. Blocked and Untested Areas

- **All runtime/dynamic testing – BLOCKED:** Docker absent; no Keycloak/Postgres/SeaweedFS/RabbitMQ/Redis/OpenBao/Nginx running; no live API calls, no browser session, no HAR/console review, no load or large-file tests, no TLS check.
- **39 backend tests – BLOCKED:** `PSQL FATAL: password authentication failed for user "postgres"`. Affected classes: `V134BookmarkPermissionBackfillMigrationTest`, `DmsCoreApplicationTests`, `ExportJobRepositoryTest`, `V41ExportJobsMigrationTest`, `V42BulkImportMigrationTest`, `V47V48ImportExportMigrationTest`, `V103ToV105NotificationMigrationTest`, `V123ToV125OwnershipMigrationTest`, `V106ToV109RelationshipMigrationTest`, `V91ToV94WorkflowMigrationTest`, `V95ToV98WorkflowPhase2MigrationTest`. Re-run against a throwaway DB via `DB_URL` (per project notes).
- **NOT TESTED:** Python ocr-worker test suite; dependency/CVE scan (Maven/npm/pip, container images); SSRF review of integration/webhook code; sharing, relationship, ownership, signature internals, import field mapping only lightly sampled; DOC-04 exploitability and ownership CSV formula injection; Keycloak brute-force tuning; responsive/accessibility/console errors; duplicate API calls; remaining `app/admin/users/[userId]/*` pages; Helm/RKE2/Ceph manifests; Redis behaviour; tenant `tenant_id` mapper tamper-resistance (Keycloak 26 default unverified).
- **Not reviewed due to permission:** plaintext secrets file under `infra/secrets` (a read was blocked; confirmed only that it is git-ignored). No secret values were seen or recorded.

## 15. Recommended Fixes and Priorities

**P0 (before next release):** SEC-01 (add tenant-admin check; restrict status/tier); DOC-01 (`findVisible` in metadata/OCR-status); DOC-03 + DOC-02 (set real size/ZIP limits; stream downloads); NOT-01 (re-claim expired `sending` leases); FE-H1/INF-01 (security headers, TLS, rate limits).
**P1:** OBX-01/02/03/04 (SKIP LOCKED, confirms+mandatory, backoff/requeue, retention, partial index); DB-01 (FK migration or purge guard); DOC-05/06/07/09 (signature/lock/race guards); SEC-02/03/04 (trusted-proxy IP, restrict Keycloak token endpoint, audience validator); FE-M2 (strip cookies to Grafana); INF-03/05 (`:?` secrets, pin images); PERF-03 (scheduler pool).
**P2:** AUD-01/02, DEP-01, DOC-10, FE-M1/M3/M4, API-01/02/04/05/06, log rotation, alerts for outbox/DLQ, DB-02 policy ("never edit applied migrations").
**P3:** remaining Low/Info; add tests listed in TST-01; resolve ESLint warnings.
**Process:** re-run the 39 blocked tests against a throwaway DB; stand up the compose stack and execute the dynamic checks in §14 to convert "static FAIL" items to reproduced results.

## 16. Overall QA Assessment

**Static quality: Good with notable gaps. Release confidence: Moderate (unverified at runtime).** Test hygiene is strong (942 backend tests pass, 323 frontend tests pass, type-check and lint clean), and defensive design is evident in tenant scoping, SQL parameterisation, scan/quarantine gating, locking in most schedulers and fail-closed behaviours. However, 8 High-severity defects were confirmed by code reading, including one authorization flaw that lets any member suspend an organisation (SEC-01), an in-tenant IDOR on document metadata (DOC-01), reliability defects that silently lose notifications/events (NOT-01, OBX-01/02), and missing size limits/edge hardening. No Critical issue was identified, but because no live system could be exercised, absence of Critical findings must not be read as assurance. Recommended: fix P0 items, then perform a full dynamic QA pass on a running stack.

---

## 17. Remediation Log

### SEC-01 – Tenant authorization – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding:** see §7. `PUT /api/v1/tenants/{id}` was guarded by `hasRole('PLATFORM_ADMIN') or @tenantSecurity.isSameTenant(#tenantId)`, which checks tenant membership only.
- **Root cause (confirmed, and reproduced on a real database):** with the original code, the new tests got HTTP 200 where 403 was expected for (a) an ordinary same-tenant member, (b) a same-tenant tenant admin changing `status`, (c) the same admin changing `subscriptionTier`. The `SecurityConfig` filter deliberately leaves this route to `@PreAuthorize`, so no other control applied.
- **Implemented change (Option B, approved):**
  - Guard is now `hasRole('PLATFORM_ADMIN') or (@tenantSecurity.isSameTenant(#tenantId) and @authz.isTenantAdmin())`. Ordinary members and admins of other tenants get 403.
  - Platform admins: unchanged (password confirmation via `passwordConfirmation.require`, may change every field).
  - Tenant admins: new `TenantService.renameOwnTenant` – compares the requested `subscriptionTier` and `status` with the stored values and throws `AccessDeniedException` (→ 403 via `GlobalExceptionHandler`, audited like other denials) **before anything is saved**, so a rejected request changes nothing (not even the name). Otherwise it delegates to the existing `updateTenant`. No DTO / API-contract change; no migration.
  - `GET /api/v1/tenants/{id}` deliberately unchanged (profile screen needs same-tenant member read access).
  - Corrected the misleading comment in `TenantController` and the `TenantSecurity` Javadoc (it checks membership, not authority).
- **Files modified:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/tenant/TenantController.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/tenant/TenantService.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/tenant/TenantServiceImpl.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/security/auth/TenantSecurity.java` (Javadoc only)
  - `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/tenant/TenantAdministrationIT.java` (8 new tests)
- **Tests executed and results** (integration tests ran against a throwaway database `dms_sec01_it` in the `oakdms-postgres` container, dropped afterwards; the dev database was not touched):
  - `TenantAdministrationIT`: 11 run, 0 failures (3 pre-existing + 8 new: member 403; other-tenant admin 403; own admin rename 200; own admin status change 403; own admin tier change 403; stored values unchanged after rejections; platform admin with password header 200 on all fields; platform admin without password rejected; same-tenant member GET 200).
  - Negative check: with the four production files temporarily reverted, 3 of the new tests failed (`expected 403 but was 200`) – confirming the tests detect the vulnerability. Files restored afterwards.
  - Regression ITs: `PasswordConfirmationIT` 15/15, `TenantAdminDetailsIT` 13/13, `AccessDeniedAuditIT` 2/2 – all pass (41 ITs total in that run).
  - Full backend unit suite (`mvnw verify`, same DB): 981 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped. This also clears the 39 DB-auth errors from the original audit run, which were an environment issue.
  - Frontend: `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors; `vitest` for `components/admin`, `lib/tenants`, `components/profile` – 27/27 pass.
- **Remaining limitations:**
  - The full `*IT` suite and full frontend suite were not re-run (only tenant-/auth-related ITs and the affected frontend tests).
  - Tested with mocked JWTs (`ROLE_TENANT_ADMIN` authority); not exercised against a live Keycloak token or the browser.
  - No existing UI lets a tenant admin edit the organisation (the Organisations screen is platform-admin only), so the tenant-admin rename path is API-only today.
  - Changes are uncommitted in the working tree, per project rules.
- **Correction to this report's method note:** Docker is not on the Windows PATH but the stack **is** running in WSL (`wsl docker ps` lists `oakdms-*` containers). The earlier "no running stack" limitation therefore applied only to the Windows shell; dynamic testing is possible via WSL and should be used for the remaining findings.

### DOC-01 – Metadata / OCR-status access control – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding:** see §9. `GET/PUT /documents/{id}/metadata` and `GET /documents/{id}/ocr-status` checked only the tenant.
- **Confirmed root cause (reproduced on a real database before the fix):** `DocumentMetadataServiceImpl.requireDocument` (`findById` + tenant compare) and `DocumentServiceImpl.getOcrStatus` never called `DocumentAccessService`. A same-tenant user not on a restricted document's list got 200 on `/ocr-status` (including OCR-extracted text) and 200 on `PUT /metadata` (value overwritten), while `/content` and `/review` correctly returned 404. Cross-tenant was already 404. **Correction to the original finding:** PUT on a soft-deleted document was already 404 (the controller's `editGuard` excludes deleted documents); GET `/metadata` and `/ocr-status` on a soft-deleted document did return 200.
- **Template compatibility check:** no dependency. `PUT /documents/{id}/metadata` is called only by `frontend/components/documents/document-upload-form.tsx` for the document just uploaded; `DocumentTemplateService` / `/document-templates` never use the metadata service. The view cap on templates therefore affects no existing workflow.
- **Implemented change:**
  - `DocumentMetadataServiceImpl`: new `requireAccessible(documentId, tenantId, level)` = existing tenant-aware non-deleted lookup (`findByDocumentIdAndTenantIdAndDeletedFalse`) followed by `DocumentAccessService.require(forCaller(), doc, level)`. `getValues` requires **view**; `saveValues` requires **edit**, before anything is written (so a rejected PUT changes nothing). `requireDocument` is kept, unchanged, for `applyOcrFields` (background OCR, no user context). New constructor dependency: `DocumentAccessService`.
  - `DocumentServiceImpl.getOcrStatus`: uses the existing `findVisible(documentId, view)` (404 for hidden, deleted and other-tenant documents).
  - No special admin bypass was added; tenant admins get whatever `DocumentAccessService` already gives them. No controller, DTO, migration or frontend change.
- **Files modified:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentMetadataServiceImpl.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentServiceImpl.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentMetadataServiceApplyOcrFieldsTest.java` (only the constructor call: one added mock argument, required by the new dependency)
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/ocrworker/DocumentMetadataAccessIT.java` (in the `ocrworker` package only to reuse the package-private `ScanGateFixture`)
- **Tests executed and results** (integration tests against a throwaway database, since dropped; real `DocumentAccessService`, folder access and database; only the `@authz.hasPermission` code gate is stubbed as granted):
  - `DocumentMetadataAccessIT`: 8/8 pass – restricted/no access → 404 on all three and nothing written; view-only → GET 200 (both), PUT 403, stored value unchanged; editor → PUT 200; folder-hidden document → 404 on all three, folder viewer GET 200 / PUT 403; cross-tenant → 404 on all three; soft-deleted → 404 on all three (even for the owner); ordinary top-level document → all three 200; tenant administrator → 200 on a restricted document they are not listed on.
  - Full backend unit suite: 981 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped (includes `DocumentMetadataServiceApplyOcrFieldsTest` 5/5, covering `applyOcrFields`, and `ReviewEndpointTenantIsolationTest` / `ReviewCorrectionsAreManualTest`, covering `saveReview`).
  - Regression ITs passing: `DocumentUploadIT` 9/9, `OcrEventsConsumerLiveIT` 1/1 (real `applyOcrFields` via RabbitMQ), `OcrFieldsReconcilerIT` 4/4, `DocumentListIT` 6/6, `DocumentRenditionIT` 16/16, `DocumentResponseIT` 2/2, `TenantAdministrationIT` 11/11 (SEC-01 intact).
- **Known failing test, judged unrelated:** `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` (1 test) fails. With the default config (`dms.ocr.enabled` defaults to false) it gets `ocr_status = not_required` instead of `pending`; with `OCR_ENABLED=true` it passes that assertion and then fails on a stale assertion that the storage object key ends with the original filename (keys are UUID-built). It never calls the three endpoints changed here and nothing changed touches upload/enqueue/storage keys. **Not verified against a pristine baseline** (stashing was not permitted), so this is an inference from the code, not a measured result.
- **Remaining limitations:**
  - The negative check ("new tests fail on the old code") was not re-run for DOC-01; the pre-fix behaviour was instead reproduced earlier with a scratch test (outsider got 200 on `/ocr-status` and `PUT /metadata`).
  - Permission-code gates were stubbed, so the tests do not prove which real roles hold those codes; mocked JWTs only (no live Keycloak/browser).
  - `OcrCrossServiceE2EIT`, `SeaweedFsDocumentUploadIT` and the rest of the `*IT` suite were not run; no frontend test was run (no frontend change).
  - Infrastructure note: the WSL stack restarted mid-task and the `oakdms-postgres`, `oakdms-clamav` and `oakdms-rabbitmq` containers were started again with your approval to run the tests; other `oakdms-*` containers (keycloak, seaweedfs, redis) were left as found (stopped).

### DOC-02 – Plain download buffering – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding:** see §9 (`DocumentServiceImpl.withDefaultWatermark` read the whole file with `readAllBytes()` before checking whether the tenant's default watermark applied).
- **Corrected root cause (verified, with measurements):** only ONE path buffered - the plain download (`GET /documents/{id}/content` with `inline=false` and no `channel=preview`). The controller (`StreamingResponseBody`), the storage layer (S3 `ResponseInputStream`, filesystem, streaming cipher), version downloads (`/versions/{id}/content`), inline previews and bulk export already streamed. Allocation measured per thread on the request thread (filesystem storage, 2/8/24 MB files, before the fix): plain download 6.3 / 18.6 / **51.5 MB** (about 2.1x the file, growing linearly, and the copy stays live while the client receives it); version download and inline preview a constant ~2.2 MB. The `readAllBytes()` ran for every plain download even though every input to the watermark decision (settings, type, size cap) is known beforehand, and the default is "watermark off".
- **Implemented change:**
  - `WatermarkDefaultsService`: new `isDownloadWatermarkApplicable(VersionDetails)` (never throws; on any failure the answer is "no", the same "serve the original" fallback as before). The eligibility rules (fonts available, setting on with a template, supported type, `version.sizeBytes` within the per-format cap, template present and active) were moved out of `tryApply` into one private `eligibleTemplate(...)` that both `tryApply` and the new method call - the rules are not duplicated and cannot drift.
  - `DocumentServiceImpl.withDefaultWatermark`: asks that question first. If the watermark does not apply (the usual case) it returns the stored stream untouched - no `readAllBytes()`. If it applies, behaviour is unchanged: the file (at most the configured cap, 25 MB by default) is read, watermarked, and returned with the watermarked length.
  - No change to the controller, storage adapters, version download, inline preview, export, DTOs, migrations, frontend or configuration. Content-Length, Content-Disposition (original filename), Content-Type, `nosniff`, authorization, tenant isolation, soft-delete handling and audit logging are untouched.
- **Files modified:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/watermark/service/WatermarkDefaultsService.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentServiceImpl.java`
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/watermark/DocumentDownloadBufferingIT.java` (13 tests; in the `watermark` package only to reuse the scanner-free `WatermarkFixtures`)
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentDownloadStreamCleanupIT.java` (3 tests)
- **Tests executed and results** (integration tests on a throwaway database, since dropped; real storage, encryption, access checks and watermark code):
  - `DocumentDownloadBufferingIT` 13/13: small document unchanged with its recorded size/type/name; **24 MB document with no watermark - content digest equals the original, content is the storage stream (not a `ByteArrayInputStream`), allocation under a quarter of the file size**; four concurrent 24 MB downloads - every digest correct and combined allocation under a quarter of their combined size; PDF under the cap with the watermark on - still watermarked (bytes differ, Content-Length is the watermarked length, still a valid one-page PDF); PDF over the cap (recorded 30 MB) - streams unchanged; unsupported type - unchanged; retired template - unchanged; watermark switched off - unchanged; hidden document - not found; other tenant's document - not found; soft-deleted - not found; missing storage object - `StorageObjectNotFoundException` before any bytes are sent; end-to-end over HTTP (`asyncDispatch`) - body equals the original, Content-Type, Content-Length, `attachment; filename="annual report.pdf"` and `nosniff` all as before.
  - `DocumentDownloadStreamCleanupIT` 3/3: through the real `DocumentController` - a stream that completes, a storage stream that fails part-way, and a client that disconnects part-way (`Broken pipe`) all leave the storage stream closed; the response headers are asserted on each.
  - Allocation assertions are a generous fraction of the file size (streamed cost is ~2 MB of fixed buffers; the buffered one was ~51 MB for 24 MB), measured with the JVM's per-thread allocation counter, so they do not depend on exact numbers.
  - Regression: full backend unit suite 981 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped (includes `ExportArchiveBuilderTest`). Integration tests, 186 run / 0 failures: `DocumentUploadIT` 9/9, `DocumentRenditionIT` 16/16, `DocumentListIT` 6/6, all 17 `Watermark*IT` classes (including `WatermarkServiceIT` 22, `WatermarkPipelineIT` 8, `WatermarkOfficePipelineIT` 4), plus `DocumentMetadataAccessIT` 8/8 (DOC-01) and `TenantAdministrationIT` 11/11 (SEC-01) still passing.
- **Remaining limitations:**
  - The size cap is judged on `version.sizeBytes` (the recorded size - the same number the watermark policy always used), not re-measured against the stream. A stored file whose bytes outgrew its recorded size would still be read whole on the watermark path; not expected, since the scan gate records the size it measured, but it is not enforced.
  - When the watermark does apply, the file is still buffered (the watermark libraries need the whole file); that is bounded by the per-format cap (25 MB by default), so concurrent watermarked downloads cost up to cap x concurrency.
  - The `channel=preview` watermarked-PDF download (preview-policy feature, bounded to 200 pages) still renders in memory by design; it was out of scope.
  - No real heap-exhaustion run was done (by design); the effect was shown by per-thread allocation measurements. The new tests were not re-run against the pre-fix code (the pre-fix cost was measured separately, above); only filesystem storage, not SeaweedFS over the network, was exercised.
  - Audit is still logged when the download opens, not when it completes (DOC-19), and Tomcat's async timeout for very long streams was not examined; both are unchanged by this fix.

### DOC-03 – ZIP content limits and nested-ZIP accounting – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Scope as approved:** upload size stays UNLIMITED (product requirement). Only the ZIP-content protections changed. Explicitly not changed: Spring multipart (`-1`), Nginx `client_max_body_size 0`, `scan_engines.max_file_size_bytes` (and it was not made configurable), the frontend, resumable-upload limits, ClamAV configuration; no storage quota, concurrency limit or compression-ratio limit was added.
- **Root cause (verified in the investigation):** `ZipArchiveCheck` counted unpacked bytes and entries only for the top-level archive; ZIPs nested inside (up to 3 deep) were inflated in a separate pass (`blockedInside`) with no counters, so a nested bomb was accepted and counted as 0 MB (512 MB of zeros in a 495-byte file: VALID, 0.9 s CPU, even with a limit set). The shipped defaults for both limits were also "no limit".
- **Implemented change:**
  - `ZipArchiveCheck`: one shared `Budget` (entries, unpacked bytes) threaded through the top-level pass and every nested level. A `Meter` stream counts every byte as it is read (and the CRC at the top level). Each top-level entry is now read ONCE: its head, the nested-archive look (from the same stream) and the rest are one pass, whereas before a nested archive's entry was inflated twice. Nested entries are drained through the meter, so bytes that used to be skipped inside `ZipInputStream.getNextEntry` are counted. Crossing a limit throws an internal `LimitExceeded` (not an `IOException`, so the existing "unreadable nested archive is left to ClamAV" handling cannot swallow it) that the top level turns into the existing INVALID result - the same refusal path (HTTP 415 with the "split it into smaller archives" message, now ending "(counting any archives inside it)").
  - Unchanged: `MAX_NESTING = 3` (deeper archives are not opened, so no CPU is spent on them), blocked extensions and executable-by-content detection at every level, CRC validation and the damaged / encrypted / empty / unsupported-compression handling, the OOXML presence check.
  - `Result.entries()` / `uncompressedBytes()` are now cumulative totals (they feed the gate's `largeArchive` routing, which now sees nested content too).
  - `IngestionProperties` and `application.yml`: defaults changed from "no limit" to **10,000 entries** and **4,194,304,000 bytes (4000 MiB)** - clamd's `MaxFiles` and `MaxScanSize` and equal to `dms.scanning.clamav.archive-scan-files/-bytes` - overridable with `INGESTION_ZIP_MAX_ENTRIES` / `INGESTION_ZIP_MAX_UNCOMPRESSED_BYTES`. They are read only by the ZIP check, so they cannot act on non-ZIP uploads. `IngestionGateImpl` needed no change (it already passes the properties).
  - `docs/scanning-architecture.md`: the one sentence that said ZIP limits "have no limit by default" was corrected.
- **Files modified:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/helper/ZipArchiveCheck.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/helper/IngestionProperties.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/resources/application.yml`
  - `docs/scanning-architecture.md` (one paragraph)
  - `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/helper/ZipArchiveCheckTest.java` (7 new tests)
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/document/IngestionZipLimitsIT.java` (5 tests)
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/IngestionLimitDefaultsIT.java` (2 tests)
- **Tests executed and results** (integration tests on a throwaway database, since dropped; real storage and ClamAV):
  - `ZipArchiveCheckTest` 26/26 (19 existing - executables, scripts, CRC, damaged, truncated, encrypted, empty, flat limits, OneDrive-style, etc. - all unchanged and passing, plus 7 new): a nested bomb is refused at the same limit as flat at 1, 2 and 3 levels; the unpacked limit is cumulative across levels (600 KB + 600 KB nested refused; each alone accepted); nested entries count toward the shared entry limit (6 + 1 + 6 = 13 > 10 refused; each level alone accepted); a valid nested ZIP is accepted and reported with exact cumulative entries (5) and bytes; the depth limit is kept (3 deep refused, 4 and 6 deep not opened); a program in a nested ZIP is still found; a corrupt outer entry holding a ZIP is still INVALID.
  - `IngestionZipLimitsIT` 5/5 through the real gate with the limits lowered to 4 entries / 20,000 bytes: a **300 KB ordinary .txt (15x the ZIP limit) is accepted and scanned clean**; a small ZIP within the limits is accepted; a flat bomb (a few hundred bytes, 200 KB unpacked) and a 3-level nested bomb are refused ("Unpacked"); a flat ZIP with 5 entries and a nested one totalling 6 are refused ("more than 4 files").
  - `IngestionLimitDefaultsIT` 2/2: shipped defaults are 10,000 / 4,194,304,000 and equal the clamd-aligned scan properties; `spring.servlet.multipart.max-file-size` / `max-request-size` still `-1`; `ScanProperties.maxFileSizeBytes` and the stored platform-default engine row still `Long.MAX_VALUE`.
  - Probe against the real classes (512 MB of zeros, 256 MB heap, limit 128 MB): before - nested 1 and 2 deep accepted, counted 0 MB, 0.8-0.9 s CPU; after - both refused at 128 MB in 0.1 s CPU, the same as the flat case.
  - Regression: full backend unit suite 988 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped. Integration tests: `DocumentUploadIT` 9/9, `DocumentCheckoutIT` 25/25, `WopiEditingIT` 39/39, `DocumentDownloadBufferingIT` 13/13, `DocumentDownloadStreamCleanupIT` 3/3, `DocumentMetadataAccessIT` 8/8, `TenantAdministrationIT` 11/11 (SEC-01/DOC-01/DOC-02 intact), plus the two new classes. The `Import*IT` / `Bulk*IT` / `Export*IT` patterns matched no integration-test classes. 115 of 116 integration tests pass; the one failure, `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT`, is the unrelated test described under DOC-01 (default `dms.ocr.enabled=false` gives `ocr_status = not_required`, not `pending`; it fails with OCR on at a stale storage-key-suffix assertion) and is unchanged by this work.
- **ClamAV consistency check (verified against the running clamd 1.4.3, harmless small ZIPs sent over INSTREAM):** a flat ZIP of 9,000 files is accepted and one of 10,500 is refused (`Heuristics.Limits.Exceeded.MaxFiles`), so the entry limit means what we set. But ClamAV applies `MaxFiles` **per archive**, not cumulatively: 6,000 files plus a nested ZIP of 6,000 files (12,001 in total) is accepted by ClamAV, whereas the cumulative limit approved for this fix refuses it. The cumulative rule is therefore stricter than ClamAV for entries (a deliberate safe superset, bounding total CPU). `MaxScanSize` (bytes) was not tested with real data (it would need >4 GB), so whether ClamAV totals it across nesting is **unverified**.
- **Compatibility risks / limitations:**
  - Behaviour change to call out: a ZIP whose contents total more than 4000 MiB or more than 10,000 entries (nested included) is now refused with 415 instead of being scanned file by file. Because stored (uncompressed) ZIPs hold roughly their own size, in practice a ZIP of more than about 4 GB of content is no longer accepted by default, even though the ZIP's own size is not limited. Operators who need larger ZIPs can raise `INGESTION_ZIP_MAX_UNCOMPRESSED_BYTES` / `INGESTION_ZIP_MAX_ENTRIES` (the file-by-file scan path still exists above clamd's limits). The `largeArchive` routing is therefore unreachable at the shipped defaults.
  - Cumulative entry counting also refuses some nested archives that ClamAV alone would accept (see above).
  - Deeper than 3 levels is still not inspected here (ClamAV's `MaxRecursion` / limits are the guard; they refuse what they cannot finish).
  - Not tested: ZIPs near the real 4000 MiB / 10,000-entry defaults (resource-intensive; not run), ZIP64 archives with such sizes, bulk-import and resumable-upload ZIP paths (they do not use this check and were not changed), and any load or concurrency behaviour. No compression-ratio limit was added; a ratio guard remains available as a separate proposal if the CPU cost of legitimately large nested content becomes a concern.

### DOC-04 – Inline XML/JSON serving without CSP sandbox – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding / confirmed behaviour:** see §9 and the investigation notes under DOC-04. Chrome 157 (headless, a local scratch server replicating the app's header set, harmless files) executed the script of an XHTML-namespaced and of an SVG-namespaced `.xml` document in the serving origin when opened by navigation; `nosniff` did not prevent it; `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'` did. The gate accepts such files as `application/xml`.
- **Approved approach (Option C):** both a disposition policy and the CSP sandbox. The instruction message was cut off at "Apply the helper consistently to: 1"; the three routes agreed in the investigation were used - the two document content routes and the workflow supporting-document route.
- **Implemented change:**
  - NEW `document/ContentServingPolicy` (one small shared helper). `dispositionType(inlineRequested, contentType)` returns `inline` only when inline was requested AND the stored, server-determined type is on a closed allow-list: `application/pdf`, `image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `image/gif`, `image/webp`, `image/bmp`; everything else (XML, JSON, text/plain|csv|markdown, HTML/XHTML, **SVG - `image/svg+xml` is explicitly not allowed**, TIFF/HEIC/DWG, Office/ODF, ZIP, unknown or missing types) is `attachment`, so a type added to the upload list later is safe by default. `withSandbox(...)` adds `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'` to every response except PDF (Chrome's PDF viewer cannot show a sandboxed response). Matching ignores case and type parameters.
  - `DocumentController`: `GET /documents/{id}/content` and `GET /documents/{id}/versions/{versionId}/content` use the helper for the disposition and the CSP. With `inline=false` the response is an attachment exactly as before.
  - `WorkflowInstanceController`: the supporting-document content route (no `inline` parameter, previously always inline for every type) now applies the same policy (treated as an inline request).
  - Unchanged: every other header (`nosniff`, Content-Type, Content-Length, the sanitised filename), authorization, the `inline` parameter's effect on the audit event (`DOCUMENT_VIEWED` vs `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED`), `preview.pdf`, page/slide/thumbnail images, WOPI, exports and watermark downloads. The in-app preview is unaffected: it reads the files with `fetch`, which ignores both headers.
- **Files modified:**
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/ContentServingPolicy.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentController.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/workflow/controller/WorkflowInstanceController.java`
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/document/ContentServingPolicyTest.java` (33 tests)
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/watermark/ContentServingPolicyIT.java` (7 tests; in the `watermark` package to reuse its scanner-free fixtures)
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/document/SupportingDocumentContentHeadersIT.java` (5 tests)
- **Tests executed and results** (integration tests on a throwaway database, since dropped):
  - `ContentServingPolicyTest` 33/33: PDF, PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, BMP inline when asked; XML, JSON, text, CSV, Markdown, HTML, XHTML, SVG (also upper-case and with parameters), RTF, EML, ZIP, octet-stream, TIFF, HEIC, DWG, Office types attachment even when inline is asked; never inline without the inline request; missing/blank type treated as unsafe; the CSP is exactly `sandbox; default-src 'none'`, on every type but PDF, and no other header is touched.
  - `ContentServingPolicyIT` 7/7, over HTTP through the real controller, access checks, storage and encryption, on both document routes: an XHTML document stored as `.xml` requested inline is an `attachment` with the CSP; the same without `inline` is an attachment with the CSP; JSON and plain text likewise; a PDF requested inline stays `inline` with no CSP; a PDF without `inline` is an attachment as before; a PNG stays `inline`; a version carrying the type `image/svg+xml` (set directly, since `.svg` cannot be uploaded) is an attachment with the CSP. Each also asserts the body bytes, Content-Length, filename and `nosniff` are unchanged.
  - `SupportingDocumentContentHeadersIT` 5/5 on the workflow route (service stubbed): XML attachment + CSP; JSON, text and octet-stream attachment + CSP; PDF inline without CSP; PNG inline; SVG attachment + CSP; body, Content-Length, filename and `nosniff` unchanged.
  - Regression: full backend unit suite 1021 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped. Integration tests 123 run, 0 failures: `DocumentUploadIT` 9, `DocumentRenditionIT` 16, `PreviewApiIT` 6, `DocumentDownloadBufferingIT` 13, `DocumentDownloadStreamCleanupIT` 3, `WorkflowSupportingDocumentIT` 8, `WorkflowDocumentAccessIT` 12, `WorkflowPhase2AccessIT` 2, `VersionRetentionAndNotificationsIT` 16, `DocumentMetadataAccessIT` 8, `TenantAdministrationIT` 11, `IngestionZipLimitsIT` 5, `IngestionLimitDefaultsIT` 2, plus the three new classes - the SEC-01, DOC-01, DOC-02 and DOC-03 tests still pass.
- **Remaining limitations / not verified:**
  - The final headers were not re-checked in a browser: the headers are asserted exactly by the tests above and the CSP effect was verified in Chrome during the investigation, but `Content-Disposition: attachment` behaviour was not exercised in a browser (it would download a file), and nothing was uploaded to the running application. Re-running the headless-browser check against a real deployment is still advisable.
  - Chrome's PDF viewer with no CSP is the unchanged behaviour; its behaviour under a sandbox CSP (the reason PDFs are excluded) was not tested.
  - Frontend tests were not run (no frontend change); the in-app preview was reviewed in the investigation, not re-tested in a browser.
  - Behaviour change to expect: an XML, JSON, text or other non-PDF/non-image file opened from a raw inline URL now downloads instead of showing in a tab; images other than PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP/BMP (for example TIFF) also download. Optional follow-up not done: rejecting XHTML/SVG-rooted content in `FileTypeInspector`. The `Content-Disposition` filename sanitising (DOC-15) and the missing frontend/Nginx security headers (FE-H1, INF-01) are separate findings and unchanged.

### DOC-05 – Signature protection on version upload and restore – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the investigation notes under DOC-05. Upload (`POST /documents/{id}/versions`) and restore (`.../versions/{v}/restore`) checked only the workflow signature lock and the legal-hold/check-out guard, so a document whose current version was signed by a standalone signing (a valid signature record or a `signed_copy` mark) or carried a signature inside the file could have a new version made current, unlike on check-in and in the editor.
- **Approved approach and one clarification:** Option A (reuse the existing `SignedContentGuard`; no new mechanism, no automatic unlock). The investigation proposed `guardSaving`, which looks at the DMS's own records only; the implementation instruction required embedded signatures to be refused as well and not to be replaced by a records-only check, so the fix calls **`guardOpening`**, which runs `guardSaving` first and then, for formats that can hold a signature, reads the current stored file with the existing `EmbeddedSignatureDetector`. That is the same guard check-out and the editor use.
- **Implemented change (one production file):** `DocumentVersionService` gets a `SignedContentGuard` dependency and calls `signedContentGuard.guardOpening(document)` in `upload(...)` and in `restore(...)`, directly after the existing `signatureLockService.guard` and `editGuard.guard` and before anything else - before the ingestion gate, before the old version's file is opened (restore), before any audit event, storage write, scan row or version row. A signed current version is refused with the existing `409 DOCUMENT_HAS_SIGNATURE` (`CheckoutException`, handled by the existing exception mapping; nothing new). Unsigned documents behave as before.
- **All three operation paths are covered:** (1) `POST /documents/{id}/versions` and (2) restore call these two methods directly; (3) bulk import adding a version to an existing document calls the very same `DocumentVersionService.upload(...)` (`ImportRecordCreator.java:267`), so no scope expansion was needed. `ImportRecordCreator` already records any 4xx `ApiException` as that row's rejection (`isRejection`) - the same handling as for a workflow lock or a check-out; the row's failure reason code shown is the existing `SCAN_REJECTED`. Check-in calls `upload` after its own `guardSaving`, so it now also reads the current file once more for embedded signatures (no behaviour change: a file that was checked out had already passed that check).
- **Standalone and embedded signatures:** standalone signing still does not set the workflow lock (documented design unchanged; no lock is created). Its signature is protected through the guard's own rules: a valid signature record bound to the current version, or a `signed_copy` mark. Signatures inside the file (PDF `/ByteRange` signature dictionary, Office `_xmlsignatures/`, OpenDocument `documentsignatures.xml`) are detected by reading the current stored version through the existing detector.
- **Signature records and history after a rejection:** nothing is written, so they are unchanged (asserted in the integration test by comparing version count, current/latest version ids, each version's reason and scan status, scan-result rows and signature rows before and after, and by the gate never being called). Signatures are never removed or invalidated, and no unlock was added.
- **Not changed (as instructed):** metadata editing, move, delete, signature creation/verification, workflow approval and lock semantics, frontend, schema, upload size policy.
- **Files changed:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentVersionService.java`
  - `docs/checkout-design.md` (three sentences: upload/restore and bulk-import version are covered; the file read applies on upload/restore; metadata/move/delete are not)
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/checkout/DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT.java` (8 tests)
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/test/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentVersionServiceSignatureGuardTest.java` (7 tests)
- **Tests executed and results** (integration tests on a throwaway database, since dropped; real database, storage, encryption, access checks and guard; ingestion gate mocked so "never reaches the gate" is assertable):
  - `DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT` 8/8: unsigned document - upload and restore allowed (202); `signed_copy` current version - upload and restore 409 `DOCUMENT_HAS_SIGNATURE`, gate never called, snapshot unchanged; **embedded signatures read from real stored content - PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint and OpenDocument - upload and restore 409 for each**, snapshot unchanged; the same types with no signature inside (including a text file that merely mentions `/ByteRange`) unaffected; workflow-locked document - existing `DOCUMENT_SIGNATURE_LOCKED` on both routes even when also signed by mark; document checked out by another user - existing `DOCUMENT_CHECKED_OUT`; user without permission 403, user from another tenant 404 on a signed document (the signed state is never revealed); bulk-import call arguments - allowed when unsigned, refused as a 4xx `ApiException` with the same code when signed.
  - `DocumentVersionServiceSignatureGuardTest` 7/7: the real guard over stubbed repositories - a VALID signature record on the current version refuses upload and restore, with no gate call, no file opened and no audit event; a valid signature on an OLDER version only, or an invalidated record, does not block; a `signed_copy` mark refuses; with a stub guard the guard runs before the gate on both operations; the workflow lock and a legal hold answer first with their own errors and the guard is not reached.
  - Regression: backend unit suite 1028 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped (includes `SignedContentGuardTest` 9, `EmbeddedSignatureDetectorTest` 9, `DocumentSignatureLockServiceTest` 8, `ImportRecordCreatorTest` 19, `BulkImportWorkerTest` 12). Integration tests 345 run, 0 failures across 31 classes: `DocumentCheckoutIT` 25/25 (check-out, check-in and the signature/lock refusals), `WopiEditingIT` 39/39 (editor and WOPI save), `VersionRetentionAndNotificationsIT` 16, `DocumentUploadIT` 9, `DocumentRenditionIT` 16, `PreviewApiIT` 6, the workflow ITs (including `WorkflowSupportingDocumentIT`, `WorkflowConcurrencyIT` 60), and the SEC-01..DOC-04 tests (`TenantAdministrationIT`, `DocumentMetadataAccessIT`, `DocumentDownloadBufferingIT`, `DocumentDownloadStreamCleanupIT`, `IngestionZipLimitsIT`, `IngestionLimitDefaultsIT`, `ContentServingPolicyIT`, `SupportingDocumentContentHeadersIT`). `WorkflowScenariosIT` reported 0 tests run (nothing to execute in that class as selected). `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` (the known unrelated OCR-default failure described under DOC-01) was not part of this run. The full integration suite was NOT run - only the classes above.
- **Remaining limitations / compatibility:**
  - There is no supported way to remove or release a standalone signature (the API offers verify/evidence and the workflow-lock release only), so a document whose current version is signed this way can no longer receive a new version by upload, restore, check-in or editor until a mechanism for that exists. That is the same state check-in and the editor were already in; deciding whether signatures should be removable is a separate product decision and nothing was added here.
  - A bulk-import row targeting such a document now fails with the 409 message and the existing `SCAN_REJECTED` reason label (the label is the importer's generic 4xx rejection label).
  - Upload and restore now read the current stored file once for the embedded-signature check on formats that can hold signatures (PDF, Office, OpenDocument); if that file cannot be read the request is refused with the guard's existing `503 DOCUMENT_FILE_UNAVAILABLE` rather than assumed unsigned.
  - Metadata edit, move and delete of a signed document remain allowed (they do not change the signed bytes; see the investigation notes). A real standalone signing (key service, signing) followed by an upload was not run end to end; the valid-signature-record case is covered at unit level and the `signed_copy` and embedded cases at integration level.

### DOC-06 – Document state re-checked when a version is promoted – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the DOC-06 investigation notes. A clean-scanned file could be promoted to a new version of a document that had meanwhile been deleted, signature-locked, put under legal hold or signed; a deleted document turned into a generic, repeatedly retried (and re-scanned) failure.
- **Pre-implementation checks (all three passed, no redesign needed):**
  - **Locking and transactions:** the promotion transaction (`IngestionProcessor.promote`) locks the `scan_results` row first, then `persist` runs; `DocumentRepository.lockByDocumentIdAndTenantId` is already "the first lock of the workflow lock order (documents -> workflow_instances -> stage instances -> tasks)", and the synchronous `DocumentVersionChangeListener` already takes it inside this same transaction, so taking it at the start of `persist` is consistent with the documented order. A scan-result row belongs to exactly one ingestion, so no other transaction takes a document lock and then that row (no cross-order cycle found; `OwnershipLocks` locks documents only, in id order). Workflow lock/legal-hold/checkout writes are single-statement updates that wait on the row lock; soft-delete is a plain JPA save. The lock query returns an entity already in the persistence context unchanged (the pitfall noted in `DocumentRepository`/`OwnershipLocks`), so the entity is refreshed after the lock is held. Transaction isolation is READ COMMITTED: a `FOR UPDATE` read returns the latest committed row.
  - **Operation identity:** the decision uses only the scan result's `created_reason`, set by server code at submission (`replacement_upload`: plain upload, check-in, bulk import, editor save, template replacement; `rollback`: restore; `signed_copy`: all three signature-placement paths; `workflow_update` and `migration` are documented but nothing creates them). It is never client-supplied. Exactly one exemption is needed and used: `signed_copy` (signing creates that version itself, often on top of an earlier one).
  - **Terminal failure handling:** `failTerminal` already ends a row as `failed` with a reason and audit event; the ticket mapper already keys user messages off a failure-reason prefix (used for scanner limits), so no schema change and no new status was needed. Check-in already keeps its lock when the ticket is refused (`failed`), and the editor save already answers 422 for a non-accepted ticket.
- **Implemented change:**
  - NEW `scanning/exception/PromotionRefusedException` (code + message; stackless; `toFailureReason()` = `promotion-refused:<code>:<message>`; `userMessage(...)` to read it back).
  - `DocumentVersionPromoter.persist` (new-version branch): loads the document with `lockByDocumentIdAndTenantId` (soft-deleted or another tenant's document -> `DOCUMENT_UNAVAILABLE`, never telling them apart), refreshes it, and `requirePromotable` - **before any version insert, current/latest change, audit write or other change**: refuses a workflow signature lock (`DOCUMENT_SIGNATURE_LOCKED`), a legal hold (`DOCUMENT_LEGAL_HOLD`) and, via the existing `SignedContentGuard.protectedByRecord` (DOC-05's rule, not duplicated), a signed current version (`DOCUMENT_HAS_SIGNATURE`). `prepare` makes the same check unlocked, so a refusal usually costs no file copy. Deleted documents are refused for every reason; the lock, hold and signature rules do not apply to `signed_copy`.
  - `IngestionProcessor.process`: a new `catch (PromotionRefusedException)` before the generic one -> `failTerminal(..., e.toFailureReason())` + `purgeStaging` (the ingestion's own staged file). No `recordFailure`, so no attempt counted, no backoff, no retry, no second scan. The existing cleanup in `promote` removes a copy already made (the object key is unique to the attempt).
  - `IngestionTicketMapper`: a `failed` result with that reason says "The file passed the malware scan but was not added: <reason>"; any other failure keeps the generic message.
  - Not changed: upload-size limits, metadata/move/delete policies, no signature-unlock mechanism, no other-user check-out check (DOC-07), nothing for DOC-08.
- **Preserved:** signed history and signature records (nothing is written on a refusal); clean-scan requirement and the V39 trigger; tenant isolation (a request for another tenant's document is stopped even earlier, by the tenant-scoped key on `scan_results`); check-in by its holder, editor saves, restore, bulk import, template replacement and signing all promote exactly as before when the document is open to a new version.
