A quarantine release re-scan uses the original reason and is treated like any upload: if the document is no longer open, the release attempt fails with the clear reason and the record stays quarantined for a later attempt (same terminal handling as any failed release attempt).
- **Files changed:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/exception/PromotionRefusedException.java` (new)
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentVersionPromoter.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/service/impl/IngestionProcessor.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/helper/IngestionTicketMapper.java`
  - `docs/scanning-architecture.md` (one failure-mode row and one paragraph)
  - NEW tests: `scanning/helper/PromotionRefusalTicketTest` (3), `document/DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` (12), `watermark/VersionPromotionRefusalIT` (6; in the `watermark` package to reuse its fixtures)
- **Tests executed and results** (integration tests on a throwaway database, since dropped; real ClamAV, storage and database):
  - `PromotionRefusalTicketTest` 3/3: reason round-trip, colons in messages, other failures keep the generic message.
  - `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` 12/12 (real promoter): ordinary and restore promotions succeed; workflow lock, legal hold, deleted (explicit `DOCUMENT_UNAVAILABLE`, also for `signed_copy`) and signed-by-record (upload and restore) are refused and a before/after snapshot (version count, scan rows, signature rows, current/latest ids, hold, lock, deletion, each version's reason) is identical; another tenant cannot reach the document; `signed_copy` still promotes onto a signed version (and, pinned as documented behaviour, onto a held one); check-out ownership is not consulted (by another user or by the uploader). **Two real-transaction concurrency tests** (separate threads, latches, and `pg_stat_activity` to see the blocked session - no sleeps): (1) another transaction holds an uncommitted workflow lock on the row -> the promotion is observed BLOCKED on the row lock, and after that transaction commits it refuses on the committed state, adding no version; (2) a promotion is paused inside its transaction (after the row is updated) -> a concurrent lock waits for it, then both complete, serialised into a consistent state (the lock names the version that is current, two versions exist).
  - `VersionPromotionRefusalIT` 6/6 through the real gate: for a lock, a hold, a deletion and a signed version applied after acceptance the ticket is `failed` with the specific message; the scan row is `failed` with `promotion-refused:<code>:...`, `attempt_count` 0, no `next_attempt_at`, `completed_at` set, `staging_purged_at` set; the scan timer rose by exactly 1 (scanned once, never again); staging and the document's storage hold exactly the files they held before (no stray copy); one `DOCUMENT_SCAN_FAILED` audit event; version count, current/latest ids, hold, lock and deletion unchanged and the existing content is still served; an ordinary document still takes the version (control); another tenant's request is rejected earlier by the tenant-scoped key.
  - Regression: backend unit suite 1031 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped. 443 integration tests, 0 failures, in the classes selected: `DocumentCheckoutIT` 25, `WopiEditingIT` 39, `DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT` 8 (DOC-05), `DocumentVersionWorkflowIT` 4, `DocumentUploadIT` 9, `RecordsRetentionIT` 40, `VersionRetentionAndNotificationsIT` 16, all `Workflow*IT` (including `WorkflowConcurrencyIT` 60 and `WorkflowSupportingDocumentIT` 8; `WorkflowScenariosIT` reported 0 tests), `OwnershipTransferIT` 13, `OwnershipConcurrencyIT` 8, `RelationshipConcurrencyIT` 3, `RelationshipLifecycleIT` 4, `DocumentRenditionIT`, `PreviewApiIT`, `DocumentListIT`, `DocumentResponseIT`, and the SEC-01..DOC-05 tests (`TenantAdministrationIT`, `DocumentMetadataAccessIT`, `DocumentDownloadBufferingIT`, `DocumentDownloadStreamCleanupIT`, `IngestionZipLimitsIT`, `IngestionLimitDefaultsIT`, `ContentServingPolicyIT`, `SupportingDocumentContentHeadersIT`).
  - **Not run:** the full integration suite; `SeaweedFsDocumentUploadIT` and `OcrCrossServiceE2EIT` (need external services that were not started); `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` (the known unrelated OCR-default failure described under DOC-01); the Watermark* ITs; frontend tests (no frontend change). There is no dedicated quarantine-release IT in the repository, so the release-attempt compatibility above is from code review, not run.
- **Remaining risks / limitations:**
  - The state is re-checked only when the version is promoted; a lock, hold or deletion that arrives after that commit is, correctly, later than the version.
  - `signed_copy` is exempt from the lock, hold and signature rules (deletion is still refused). Standalone signing has request-time lock and hold guards, but the workflow signature placement (`SignaturePlacementService`) has none, so a hold placed during a workflow's stamping step is not caught. Left as is on purpose; recorded as a `FOLLOW-UP` comment in `DocumentVersionPromoter` and pinned by a test.
  - The check-out-by-another-user rule is intentionally not applied (DOC-07); a check-in's own lock is still released when its upload is accepted, as before.
  - The row-lock wait has no timeout of its own: a transaction that holds the document row for long (for example a workflow submission) delays a promotion until it ends.
  - Embedded signatures inside a file are not re-read at promotion (the current version's bytes cannot change in the window); a race between two uploads where the second promotes over a first that carried an embedded signature remains (DOC-08 area).
  - The concurrency guarantees were verified with real PostgreSQL transactions at READ COMMITTED on a single database; behaviour under other isolation levels, or across multiple application instances, was not separately tested (the row lock is database-level, so it applies to all instances).

### DOC-07 – Check-out kept while a check-in is pending, and checked again at promotion – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the DOC-07 investigation notes. The check-out was released as soon as a check-in was *accepted*, including `pending` (no version yet), so someone else could check out the old version and edit it while the holder's file was still being scanned, then be overwritten when it arrived; promotion looked at no check-out.
- **Pre-implementation checks (no blocker, no migration needed):**
  - **Ownership and expiry:** the check-out is `documents.checked_out_by`, `checked_out_at`, `checkout_expires_at`, set together by one atomic statement (`acquireCheckout`) and cleared by `releaseCheckout` / `releaseCheckoutAnyHolder`. The expiry is fixed at acquisition (nothing refreshes it); an expired lease stays on the row until a takeover (`acquireCheckout` accepts `checkout_expires_at <= now`), a discard or a release; a force-release clears every column. `checked_out_at` is set once per acquisition, so `(holder, taken-at)` identifies one lease.
  - **Trusted identity of the upload:** the gate records `scan_results.uploaded_by` (from the authenticated context) and `scan_results.started_at` (set when it accepts the file) - nothing client-supplied; both are available to the promoter, which never reads the request context. The current request's user is not used.
  - **State can change during scanning:** yes - that is the finding; the promotion's row lock (DOC-06) makes the check authoritative.
  - **Automatic release after promotion is NOT implemented, by design:** the pair above proves the holder's lease existed when the file was submitted, but nothing persisted says that version came from a *check-in* rather than a plain upload or an online-editor save by the same holder (all use the reason `replacement_upload`), and releasing on those would end editing sessions. A marker needs a new `created_reason`/column - protected by CHECK constraints - i.e. a migration, which was not made. This is the case the instructions said to report rather than work around.
  - **Ingestion outcomes of a pending check-in (traced):** clean -> promoted; scan failure, exhausted retries or no cloud verdict -> `failed`; infected -> quarantined; promotion refused (DOC-06 rules and the new check-out rule) -> terminal `failed` with the reason. With the lock now kept, all of these leave the holder still holding the check-out (as the synchronous refusals already did). The user sees the toast at submission only; no polling or notification was added (out of scope).
- **Implemented change:**
  - `DocumentCheckoutService.checkIn`: releases the check-out (and audits `DOCUMENT_CHECKED_IN`) only when a version exists - ticket `clean` or `released_override`. `pending` keeps the lock and writes no "checked in" event (it was misleading: no version existed); `infected`, `failed`, `unscannable`, `quarantined` keep the lock as before.
  - `DocumentVersionPromoter.requireCheckoutAllows` (called from `requirePromotable`, so in `prepare` unlocked and in `persist` under the row lock, after the DOC-06 checks, for every reason except `signed_copy`): nobody holds an unexpired check-out - allowed; someone else holds one - refused `DOCUMENT_CHECKED_OUT`; the uploader holds one taken after the upload was submitted (a new lease) - refused `DOCUMENT_CHECKED_OUT`; the uploader holds one taken at or before submission (the check-in's own lease) - allowed; the uploader's lease from before the submission has expired with nobody taking over - refused `CHECKOUT_EXPIRED`. A missing `started_at` fails closed. Never decided by user name alone; only server-recorded facts.
  - Refusals use the DOC-06 `PromotionRefusedException` path: terminal `failed` at once, no retry, no re-scan, no attempt counted, copy and staged file cleaned up, one `DOCUMENT_SCAN_FAILED` audit event, a clear ticket message; nothing else is changed, including the other user's check-out.
  - `frontend/lib/checkout/use-checkout.ts`: for a `pending` check-in the toast says "Check-in submitted. Your file is being scanned, and the document remains checked out until processing completes or the checkout is released."; the synchronous message and the error handling are unchanged.
  - Docs: `docs/checkout-design.md` (pending retention, the promotion table, the limitation) and `docs/scanning-architecture.md` (the refusal row and paragraph).
- **Cases (all tested):** original lease active - allowed; original lease expired, nobody took over - refused `CHECKOUT_EXPIRED`; another user took over - refused; same user took a new lease after expiry - refused; force-released - no check-out left on the row, so the promotion proceeds like any upload (**known limitation**); pending upload followed by a check-out (by anyone) - refused; signing-generated `signed_copy` - exempt, as in DOC-06; workflow lock, legal hold, deletion and signed document - the DOC-06 refusals still come first.
- **Files changed:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/checkout/DocumentCheckoutService.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentVersionPromoter.java`
  - `frontend/lib/checkout/use-checkout.ts`
  - `docs/checkout-design.md`, `docs/scanning-architecture.md`
  - Existing tests changed on purpose: `DocumentCheckoutIT` (the test that expected release at `pending` was replaced by one asserting the new policy: lock kept, Bob refused with `DOCUMENT_CHECKED_OUT`, no `DOCUMENT_CHECKED_IN` audit, holder can still discard; plus separate tests for `clean`, `released_override` and the refused statuses) and `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` (the DOC-06 test that pinned "check-out ownership is not consulted" was replaced by the new rule's tests, since the deferral it pinned is now implemented). `VersionPromotionRefusalIT` gained four gate-level tests. No other test was edited.
  - New tests: `document/DocumentVersionPromoterCheckoutRuleTest` (14), `frontend/lib/checkout/use-checkout.test.tsx` (3)
- **Tests executed and results** (integration tests on a throwaway database, since dropped; real ClamAV, storage and PostgreSQL):
  - `DocumentVersionPromoterCheckoutRuleTest` 14/14 (real promoter, stubbed repositories): every lease case above, fail-closed missing submission time, `signed_copy` exempt, restore judged by the same rule, DOC-06 rules still first.
  - `DocumentCheckoutIT` 28/28 (25 before: one replaced, four added): pending retains the lock and refuses Bob; clean and released-override release and audit; failed, unscannable and quarantined keep it like infected.
  - `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` 20/20 (12 before: one replaced, nine added): on real rows - another user's active check-out refuses and leaves it untouched; the holder's own pending check-in promotes and is not released; a pending upload followed by a check-out (by Bob or by the uploader) is refused; expiry, takeover and new-lease cases refused; force-release proceeds (limitation); `signed_copy` exempt; legal hold still wins. **Two new real-transaction concurrency tests** (separate threads, latches, and `pg_stat_activity` to see the blocked session - no sleeps): (1) another transaction takes the check-out with the real `acquireCheckout` and holds the row uncommitted -> the promotion is observed blocked, then refuses on the committed state and adds no version; (2) a promotion paused inside its transaction -> a concurrent `acquireCheckout` waits, then succeeds after it, and the end state is consistent (new version current, Bob holds, two versions).
  - `VersionPromotionRefusalIT` 10/10 through the real gate: someone else's check-out -> terminal `failed`, scanned once, no retry, nothing left in staging or document storage, one audit event, versions and the other user's check-out untouched; the uploader's own earlier lease promotes and is not released; a later lease and an expired lease are refused with the specific reasons.
  - Regression: backend unit suite 1045 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped. 458 integration tests, 0 failures, in the classes selected: `DocumentCheckoutIT`, `WopiEditingIT` 39, `DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT` 8 (DOC-05), `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT`, `VersionPromotionRefusalIT`, `DocumentVersionWorkflowIT` 4, `DocumentUploadIT` 9, `RecordsRetentionIT` 40, `VersionRetentionAndNotificationsIT` 16, all `Workflow*IT` (`WorkflowScenariosIT` reported 0 tests), `OwnershipTransferIT`, `OwnershipConcurrencyIT`, `RelationshipConcurrencyIT`, `RelationshipLifecycleIT`, `DocumentRenditionIT`, `PreviewApiIT`, `DocumentListIT`, `DocumentResponseIT`, and the SEC-01..DOC-04 tests.
  - Frontend: full vitest suite 326 passed (49 files; 323 before plus 3 new), `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors, eslint on `lib/checkout` clean.
  - **Not run:** the full integration suite; `SeaweedFsDocumentUploadIT` and `OcrCrossServiceE2EIT` (external services not started); `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` (the known unrelated OCR-default failure described under DOC-01); the Watermark* ITs; the browser UI (the toast was tested in a unit test only).
- **Remaining risks / limitations:**
  - **No conflict detection and no automatic release.** The holder stays the holder after a late promotion until they discard, check in again or it expires (72 h by default); a force-released check-out cannot be told from none, so that pending check-in promotes like any upload; a document changed by someone else after a lease ended can be overwritten by a stale check-in that happens to find no check-out (needs a recorded base version, a migration).
  - A lease that expires during a long pending scan now makes the late promotion fail with a clear reason instead of silently overwriting later work - the holder re-checks out and re-uploads (the staged copy is kept for the existing failed-retention period, but there is no UI to resubmit it).
  - After an asynchronous check-in the document keeps showing as checked out by the holder; no polling or notification of the final outcome was added.
  - Lease comparison uses the application clock on both sides; clock skew between instances of more than the time between taking a check-out and submitting a file is not guarded.
  - The `DOCUMENT_CHECKED_IN` event is no longer written for a pending check-in (nothing was checked in yet); the ingestion's own events record the eventual outcome.

### DOC-08 – Version number assigned inside the writing transaction – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the DOC-08 investigation (kept as written). `prepare` chose `latest + 1` without a lock, so two overlapping uploads of one document planned the same number and the second insert broke `uq_document_versions_document_number`, costing a rescan, an attempt and a misleading ticket message.
- **Pre-implementation checks (no blocker):** (A) storage keys are opaque: only the download path and the recycle-bin purge read `getStorageObjectKey()` and use it as a stored string; rendition and OCR keys derive from ids; nothing parses `v<N>`; only `DocumentVersionPromoter.buildObjectKey` builds it. (B) `persist` runs in the caller's transaction; the DOC-06 row lock precedes the `latest` query; at READ COMMITTED it sees the other promotion's commit; the constraint is untouched. (C) labels (major/minor) come from the same `latest` row and are separate from the number.
- **Implemented change (option A, no migration):**
  - `DocumentVersionPromoter.persist`: for a new version the number is `latest.versionNumber + 1` (1 when there is none), computed after the lock and the `latest` read; a debug log notes when it differs from the plan's hint. Label rules unchanged; the duplicate major/minor assignment was removed. `prepare` now documents its number as a hint (used only for the `v<N>` folder of the object key).
  - `scanning/service/DocumentPromoter.java`: Javadoc on `PromotionPlan` (number is a hint, key opaque).
  - `docs/scanning-architecture.md`: a short note.
- **Files changed:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentVersionPromoter.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/service/DocumentPromoter.java`
  - `docs/scanning-architecture.md`
  - NEW tests: `document/DocumentVersionPromoterNumberingTest` (6), `watermark/ConcurrentVersionUploadIT` (1)
  - Existing test class extended (additions only): `document/DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` (20 -> 23)
- **Tests executed and results** (throwaway databases, since dropped; real ClamAV, storage and PostgreSQL):
  - `DocumentVersionPromoterNumberingTest` 6/6: first version 1 / "1.0"; sequential 2..5 with labels; two prepared together both propose 2 but are stored 2 and 3 (labels 1.1 / 1.2, key keeps `/v2/`); number taken from the row read at persist; restore and `signed_copy` numbered like any other; label and number from the same row.
  - `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` 23/23: sequential/restore/`signed_copy` numbering on real rows; **two promotions prepared together, run in two real transactions** (the first paused inside its transaction, the second observed blocked on the row lock via `pg_stat_activity`, no sleeps) both commit with numbers 2 and 3, labels 1.1 / 1.2, current/latest on the later commit, stored keys unchanged, both files downloadable, cleanup by stored key works, and a forced duplicate number is still rejected by the unique constraint; a refused promotion takes no number and the next one gets it.
  - `ConcurrentVersionUploadIT` 1/1 through the real gate: two uploads of one document held at a barrier after `prepare` (both hints 2) both end `clean`, numbers {1,2,3}, labels 1.1 / 1.2, one scan each (timer +2), `attempt_count` 0, no `next_attempt_at`, no `DOCUMENT_SCAN_FAILED` event, nothing left in staging, exactly two new files in storage, message not "temporarily unavailable". (Its first run failed on my own assertion - the fixture's seeded first version has no label - and was corrected in the test; the product code was not changed.) **The old behaviour was not run against these tests** (that would have meant modifying the tree), so that they fail without the fix is expected from the code, not observed.
  - Regression: backend unit suite 1051 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped (1045 before plus the 6 new; a first run without the database variables gave 39 errors, all "password authentication failed", and was repeated correctly). Integration run over the selected classes: 462 tests; the first combined run had 5 failures and 14 errors in `DocumentRenditionIT` (could not write the development key file under the temp folder), `DocumentDownloadBufferingIT` (could not open a JPA EntityManager during a concurrent-download test), `WorkflowAccessIT`, `WorkflowPhase2NotificationIT` and `WorkflowScenariosIT$B_Rejection` (an awaited notification mail not seen); none touch version numbering. **Re-run on their own, those classes pass: 66 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors.** I did not establish the exact cause of the first-run failures (resource contention or timing is the likely reason); every other selected class passed in the first run (`DocumentCheckoutIT` 28, `WopiEditingIT` 39, `DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT` 8, `VersionPromotionRefusalIT` 10, `DocumentVersionWorkflowIT` 4, `DocumentUploadIT` 9, `RecordsRetentionIT` 40, `VersionRetentionAndNotificationsIT` 16, `OwnershipTransferIT` 13, `OwnershipConcurrencyIT` 8, relationship ITs, `PreviewApiIT`, `DocumentListIT`, `DocumentResponseIT`, `TenantAdministrationIT`, `ContentServingPolicyIT`, the download, ZIP and limits ITs, the other `Workflow*IT`). No single run executed all of them green together.
  - **Not run:** the full integration suite; `SeaweedFsDocumentUploadIT`, `OcrCrossServiceE2EIT`, `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` (known unrelated OCR-default failure); the Watermark* ITs; frontend tests (no frontend change).
- **Remaining risks / limitations:**
  - The `v<N>` folder in a new object's key is the prepared hint and can lag the stored number after a race (cosmetic; keys stay unique through the version id; nothing parses them). Dropping the number from new keys (option A2) was not done.
  - **Embedded-signature gap (separate, not implemented, as instructed):** a signed PDF promoted as a new version has no signature record, so a pending upload accepted earlier can still be promoted over it.
  - Signing placement still reports "not ready" if its stamped copy is not clean at once; with numbering collisions gone that case arises only from a slow scan, as before.
  - Verified at READ COMMITTED on one database; multiple application instances rely on the database row lock and were not separately tested.
- **Regression verification (2026-11-11, read-only; no code or test changed):**
  - **Original combined run, re-examined.** The log of that run (`reg.log`) contains **two** combined integration runs that finished 20 s apart (11:46:10 and 11:46:30; one reported 5 failures / 14 errors, the other 6 failures / 1 error, both of 462 tests), with interleaved output. My first background launch had survived and I started a second one, so **two Maven runs shared one database (`dms_doc08_reg`), one temp directory (the development key file) and one set of log files at the same time**. This was not noticed when the failures were recorded above, which counted only one of them.
  - **Re-run of the same selection, alone.** Same 26-class selection and same command as before (`./mvnw -B -o failsafe:integration-test failsafe:verify -DfailIfNoTests=false "-Dit.test=<same list>"`, `DB_URL`/`DB_USERNAME`/`DB_PASSWORD` pointing at a new disposable database `dms_doc08_ver` in the existing `oakdms-postgres` container, dropped afterwards; only one Maven/Java process running, checked). Result: **462 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped, BUILD SUCCESS, 6 min 36 s.** The five classes that failed earlier all passed in it (`DocumentRenditionIT` 16, `DocumentDownloadBufferingIT` 13, `WorkflowAccessIT` 10, `WorkflowPhase2NotificationIT` 5, `WorkflowScenariosIT$B_Rejection` 1), as did the DOC-05, DOC-06 and DOC-07 classes (`DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT` 8, `VersionPromotionRefusalIT` 10, `DocumentCheckoutIT` 28, `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` 23) and the new `ConcurrentVersionUploadIT` 1. The original 19 failures/errors did **not** recur.
  - **Cause - supported by evidence, not proven.** Plausible and consistent with the facts: the mail failures ("No matching mail") come from tests whose helper drives the database-backed notification dispatcher each poll (`WorkflowApiSupport.awaitMail`), so a second JVM on the same database can send a pending notification into its own mail sink; the key-file error is a write to a temp file both JVMs use; the EntityManager error fits two runs exhausting connections on one server. I did not reproduce the failures by running two runs together on purpose, so this remains an explanation, not a confirmed root cause. The failures are therefore **not recorded as resolved by a fix**: no fix was made; the result is that a single, isolated run is green.
  - **DOC-08 specifics, all confirmed in that run:** concurrent promotions numbered consecutively (2 and 3; `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` and `ConcurrentVersionUploadIT`); no unique-constraint violation; both versions stored and downloadable; each file scanned once (scan timer +2); both scan rows `attempt_count` 0, no retry scheduled; labels 1.1 / 1.2 correct; the embedded-signature gap above is unchanged and still open.
  - **Remaining uncertainty:** the real cause of the first-run failures is unconfirmed; the Watermark* ITs, the full integration suite and the external-service ITs were still not run.

### DOC-09 – Purge, restore and legal hold coordinated by a document row lock – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the DOC-09 investigation (kept as written). `purge` destroyed a document's files with no lock and no re-check, so a restore (and then a legal hold) that landed in that window left a live document with its content destroyed.
- **Mandatory safety checks (done before any code change):**
  - **Lock coordination:** a `select ... for update` on the document row is one PostgreSQL row lock, so it holds across application instances. The legal-hold placement is a single atomic `UPDATE ... WHERE deleted = false AND legal_hold = false`; it needs the same row lock and re-evaluates its WHERE against the committed state after waiting, so it is compatible (no change to its locking was needed). `spring.jpa.open-in-view` is false, and the decision is in any case made from columns read through JDBC after the lock is granted, not from an entity, so a stale first-level cache cannot be used.
  - **Transaction structure:** `purge` is called from inside the class (from `purgeNow`, `emptyBin` and the sweep), so `@Transactional` on it would not be applied by the Spring proxy. The existing `TransactionTemplate` is used instead (REQUIRED, unchecked exceptions roll back). `OcrArtifactWriter.deleteFor` (REQUIRED) and the encryption key-record delete (`@Transactional`) join that transaction. The lock taken is the document row only (then rows of the dependent tables it deletes); restore, hold, delete and promotion take that row alone, so no cycle was found. `FOR UPDATE` (not `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`) also makes a concurrent insert of a referencing row wait.
  - **File deletion cannot be rolled back.** Answers to the questions asked: (1) deletion fails part way: the transaction rolls back, nothing is marked, some files are gone, the document stays in the bin; (2) all deleted but the update fails: same; (3) rollback after deletion: same - and the encryption key rows are deleted inside the transaction, so they come back with the rollback while the physical objects do not; (4) a crash between deletion and commit: PostgreSQL releases the lock and rolls back - same state; (5) a later purge resumes safely (deleting a gone object is a no-op; tested); (6) **yes, such a document would be restorable although its files are gone - this was already true before this change and is the case the design had to close**; (7) deletes are idempotent; (8) audit and notification are written only after a successful commit.
  - **Decision on the stop condition:** "lock, then purge in one transaction" alone does **not** make storage deletion atomic, and a half-destroyed document could be restored as an empty one. A durable purge-claim (new state, migration, recovery job) was not needed to close that: **restore now also checks, under the lock, that the stored file of every version still in use can be read, and refuses with `DOCUMENT_CONTENT_MISSING` otherwise.** This is a behaviour change of restore that was not spelled out in the approval; it is the smallest mechanism found for check B and is flagged for review. A purge-claim design remains the alternative if you prefer not to probe storage in restore.
- **Implemented change:**
  - `RecycleBinService.purge(document, why, expectedDeletedAt)` is now one transaction: `select ... for update` on the document row (tenant-scoped), then a re-check from the locked row - still in the bin, not purged, **no legal hold**, **no protected version**, and for the sweep still the **same `deleted_at`** it selected (a document restored and deleted again has a new retention period). Only then are the files destroyed and, in the same transaction, versions marked, metadata/access/links/bookmarks removed and `documents.purged_at` set with a guard (`where is_deleted and purged_at is null`; exactly one row or the transaction fails). Result enum `PurgeResult` (`PURGED`, `GONE`, `RESTORED`, `HELD`, `PROTECTED`, `RE_DELETED`). The audit event and the notification follow the commit.
  - `purgeNow`: keeps its early refusals, then maps the locked result to the existing 409 codes (`DOCUMENT_LEGAL_HOLD`, `DOCUMENT_VERSION_PROTECTED`), a new 409 `DOCUMENT_NOT_IN_RECYCLE_BIN` (restored meanwhile) or 404 (already gone). `emptyBin`: a candidate whose state changed is counted as skipped and the rest continue. Sweep: selects `deleted_at` with each candidate and passes it; a stale candidate is skipped and the batch carries on.
  - `restore`: after its permission checks it takes the same row lock, insists the row is still in the bin and not purged (404 otherwise), refreshes the entity, and refuses a document whose live versions' stored files cannot be read (`DOCUMENT_CONTENT_MISSING`, 409).
  - `LegalHoldService.place` and `DocumentRepository.placeLegalHold`: a purged document (`purged_at` set, including a live row damaged by the old race) cannot be placed on hold. A document in the bin already could not be.
  - `docs/checkout-design.md`: the locking protocol, eligibility checks, restore and hold coordination, failure semantics and limits.
- **Files changed:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/RecycleBinService.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentRepository.java` (one condition)
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/checkout/LegalHoldService.java`
  - `docs/checkout-design.md`
  - NEW test: `watermark/RecycleBinPurgeConcurrencyIT` (12; in the `watermark` package to reuse its fixtures)
  - Existing tests changed on purpose (each gained one statement that stores the fixture version's file, because the shared fixture only records a version and never stores bytes, and restore now checks the file): `bookmark/BookmarkIT.sentToTheRecycleBin_hidesTheBookmark_andRestoreBringsItBack`, `relationship/RelationshipLifecycleIT.aSoftDeletedTarget_keepsTheLink_flaggedInTheRecycleBin_andRestoreChangesNothing`, `workflow/WorkflowDocumentAccessIT.aDocumentInAnApprovalCannotGoToTheRecycleBin_untilTheRunEnds`. No assertion was changed.
- **Tests executed and results** (real PostgreSQL in disposable databases, since dropped; real file storage; one Maven run at a time, checked):
  - `RecycleBinPurgeConcurrencyIT` 12/12. Interleavings are forced with latches inside the purge or the restore and "blocked" is observed in `pg_stat_activity`; no sleeps. **A** purge first (paused after its files are gone, lock held): a concurrent restore is observed blocked, then refused (404), the document is purged, one `DOCUMENT_PURGED`, no `DOCUMENT_RESTORED`, not visible. **B** restore first (paused before commit): a purge that was already decided on is observed blocked, then refused (409 `DOCUMENT_NOT_IN_RECYCLE_BIN`), no file destroyed, the document is live and still downloadable, no purge audit. **C** a legal hold cannot be placed on a bin document (during or after a purge) or a purged one; restore then hold then a stale purge candidate: skipped, hold and files intact; a hold or protected version appearing after the list was read: skipped under the lock, files intact. **D** (failure recovery, failures forced after real deletion): storage fails after the first of two versions' files is gone -> nothing marked, no purge audit, exactly one file missing, restore refused `DOCUMENT_CONTENT_MISSING`, retry completes (both gone, one audit); a failure after the files were deleted and after the access list was removed rolls the rows back (the access list is back, no version marked), restore refused, retry succeeds; a failure before any file is touched changes nothing and the document can still be restored and downloaded. **E** entry points: manual purge (A, B), `emptyBin` skips a document restored after its list was read and finishes the others (purged 2, skipped 1), the sweep skips a restored candidate and a candidate that was restored and deleted again (fresh period). **F** sequential: restore, hold and protected-version refusals, purge once, no restore or second purge afterwards, audit counts.
  - **Not tested as such:** a real process crash between deletion and commit (the rolled-back-after-deletion test produces the same persistent state); the version-retention sweep (separate follow-up); a restore and a purge on separate application instances (one JVM with separate threads and connections was used).
  - The new tests were not run against the old code (that would have meant modifying the tree); the old behaviour was reproduced separately during the investigation.
  - Regression: backend unit suite 1051 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped (no unit test was added). Integration selection (the DOC-08 selection of 26 classes plus `RecycleBinPurgeConcurrencyIT`, `BookmarkIT`, `OwnershipOffboardingIT`, `OwnershipBulkIT`): **507 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**, BUILD SUCCESS, 34 min 38 s, in one run on a fresh database. It includes `DocumentUploadIT` 9 (restore, purge, empty-bin and sweep paths), `DocumentCheckoutIT` 28 (hold vs sweep), `RecordsRetentionIT` 40, `BookmarkIT` 11, `RelationshipLifecycleIT` 4, `OwnershipBulkIT` 12, `OwnershipOffboardingIT` 10, `WorkflowDocumentAccessIT` 12, the DOC-05/06/07/08 classes (`DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT` 8, `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` 23, `VersionPromotionRefusalIT` 10, `ConcurrentVersionUploadIT` 1) and the rest of the earlier selection. An earlier run of the same selection had 3 failures (the three restore tests above, caused by the new restore check meeting fixtures without stored files); they were adjusted as described and the whole selection re-run.
  - **Not run:** the full integration suite; `SeaweedFsDocumentUploadIT`, `OcrCrossServiceE2EIT`, `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` (known unrelated OCR-default failure); the Watermark* ITs; frontend tests (no frontend change).
- **Remaining risks / limitations:**
  - The row lock and a database connection are held while storage is being deleted; a restore of that one document waits for it, and a slow object store with many versions lengthens that. There is no lock timeout of its own.
  - Restore now fails for any document in the bin whose stored file cannot be read, for whatever reason (a storage outage makes the restore fail rather than succeed); such a document can then only be purged. The message says so.
  - Physical deletion is still not transactional: after a failure or crash a document may sit in the bin with some files already destroyed until it is purged again (the sweep does this once its retention ends, a manual purge at once).
  - `VersionRetentionService.destroyDue` (version purge) has the same check-then-destroy pattern against a legal hold; not changed (separate follow-up). The `LegalHoldService` class comment about purge not honouring the flag is still stale.
  - The embedded-signature concurrency gap (DOC-08 follow-up) is unchanged.
  - Multiple application instances rely on the database row lock and were not tested separately.

### DOC-10 – Bulk import judges files by the bytes that actually arrive – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the DOC-10 investigation (kept as written). A ZIP entry declaring 100 bytes but holding 8 MiB passed the batch-size cap and was recorded with the wrong size.
- **Pre-implementation checks (no blocker; one design consequence):**
  - **Storage adapters (code inspection; only the file-system adapter behind the encryption wrapper was executed):** `FileSystemStorageAdapter` reads to the end of the stream, ignores the length and returns the real file size, and leaves a partly written file if the stream throws; `S3ObjectStorageAdapter` and `AzureBlobStorageAdapter` echo the declared length as the stored size; `EncryptingObjectStorageService` (enabled by default) records and returns the declared size as the plaintext size and derives the ciphertext length from it. What an S3/Azure client does with a stream longer or shorter than the declared length is not known (a JDK-level check showed the AWS SDK's request-body provider hands out the whole stream, it does not truncate). **Consequence:** a counting stream alone would not be enough - an adapter that stops at the declared length never asks for the excess, and an adapter that reads to the end would store it. So the design does not depend on any adapter's behaviour: the stream hands the adapter exactly the declared number of bytes, and the service checks afterwards, itself, that exactly that many were read and the source holds nothing more.
  - **Batch accounting:** `receiveFile` runs in one transaction under the batch row lock (`lockByImportBatchIdAndTenantIdAndCreatedBy`), so entries into one batch are serialised and the cap check cannot be raced (no separate finding). `sumStagedBytes` sums `staged_size_bytes` of the batch's rows; a rejected upload throws, rolls back and records nothing. `stagedBytes + contentLength` could **overflow** with a forged ZIP64 size near `Long.MAX_VALUE` (the sum turns negative and passes the check) - fixed here by subtraction. `deleteOnRollback` was registered only after the upload returned, so an upload that threw part way left its object behind - fixed here. A row that already has a staged object is never matched for another upload (`ImportFilenameMatcher` filters on `stagedObjectKey == null`), so deleting a failed upload's key cannot remove an earlier accepted file.
  - **Caller contract:** every caller can be held to an exact length: the single-file endpoint passes `MultipartFile.getSize()` (what was received), the ZIP intake passes the entry's declared size, which an honest ZIP matches exactly. A negative length cannot occur from either caller (the intake skips entries with unknown size) and is now refused.
- **Implemented change:**
  - NEW `importexport/storage/ExactLengthInputStream`: counts the bytes read, **never delivers more than the declared length** (reports end-of-stream at that count), is not markable (the count must stay true), and offers `isExactlyConsumed()` - exactly the declared count read and the source at end (reads one byte of the source to find out) - plus `sourceEndedEarly()`.
  - `BulkImportServiceImpl.receiveFile`: refuses a negative length (`400 IMPORT_FILE_SIZE_UNKNOWN`); computes `remaining = max-batch-size - already staged` and refuses a declared length above it (existing `IMPORT_BATCH_SIZE_EXCEEDED`, same message) before touching storage; wraps the content in `ExactLengthInputStream`; after `upload` requires `isExactlyConsumed()` and that the size storage reports equals the counted size, else `400 IMPORT_FILE_SIZE_MISMATCH`; on any failure during the upload or the check deletes the object it may have written (a failed delete is logged as an error and attached to the exception as suppressed, never swallowed) and records nothing; a storage failure that was really caused by a short stream is reported as a size mismatch; records `staged_size_bytes` and passes the size to the intake virus scan from the **counted** value, not from `stored.sizeBytes()`. The scanning policy and the antivirus step are unchanged (the scan runs after the size is verified, as before after the upload).
  - Downstream: the worker (`ImportRecordCreator:267-273`) passes `staged_size_bytes` to the scan gate and document creation; it is now the verified size. No code change was needed there.
  - `docs/scanning-architecture.md`: a paragraph on bulk-import ZIP sizes (untrusted, counted, batch limits on verified size, mismatches refused, partial writes removed).
- **Files changed:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/importexport/service/impl/BulkImportServiceImpl.java`
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/importexport/storage/ExactLengthInputStream.java`
  - `docs/scanning-architecture.md`
  - NEW tests: `importexport/storage/ExactLengthInputStreamTest` (11), `importexport/service/impl/BulkImportReceiveFileSizeTest` (17), `watermark/BulkImportSizeEnforcementIT` (9)
  - Existing test changed on purpose: `BulkImportServiceImplTest` - two tests stubbed storage without reading the stream; their stubs now read it to the end, as every real adapter does (the service now checks that). No assertion changed.
  - Correction to the investigation text: it says `BulkImportServiceImplTest` tests the batch cap with truthful lengths (`:143`); that test is about the template size cap. There was no unit test of the `receiveFile` budget before this change.
- **Tests executed and results** (real PostgreSQL in disposable databases, since dropped; real ClamAV and file storage; one Maven run at a time, checked):
  - `ExactLengthInputStreamTest` 11/11: equal, longer (never delivers the excess, end-of-stream repeatedly), an adapter that stops at the declared length (S3-style), shorter, consumer stops early, zero declared, negative declared, `Long.MAX_VALUE` declared with a short source (no overflow), single-byte and bulk reads counted alike, skip bounded, mark/reset unsupported.
  - `BulkImportReceiveFileSizeTest` 17/17 (service with stubbed storage; storage behaviours are simulated, so this proves the service's own checks): equal size recorded and passed to the scan; longer than declared (file-system-style reader) rejected, object deleted, nothing recorded, no scan; longer than declared with an S3-style reader that never reads past the declared length still rejected; shorter than declared; a short stream that makes storage fail reported as a mismatch; storage reporting a different stored size; an adapter that stops reading early (truncated object) rejected; declared above the remaining budget refused before storage is touched; declared exactly the remaining budget accepted; forged `Long.MAX_VALUE - 100` cannot overflow; several files consume the budget by real size (third refused, a smaller one still fits); a forged small declaration cannot slip through and leaves the budget intact; negative declared refused; zero-length still judged by the type check; storage failure mid-stream removes what was written; a failing cleanup is attached as suppressed; a rejected file leaves earlier accepted rows (key, size, hash) untouched and only its own object key is deleted.
  - `BulkImportSizeEnforcementIT` 9/9 (real database, real file-system staging storage behind the **encryption wrapper** (key rows asserted), real ZIP parsing, real intake service, real virus scan): the original finding - an 8 MiB entry declaring 100 bytes against a 1 MiB cap is **rejected** ("not the size its header says"), no staged item, **no object left in storage, no new encryption key row, batch total unchanged**, and the next honest file is staged with its real size and the bytes held equal the size recorded; an honest ZIP below the limit staged with its real size; an honest ZIP above the limit refused by the budget with nothing stored; several honest entries whose real sizes exceed the cap (two staged, the third refused); several forged entries cannot together exceed the cap (all refused, nothing stored); a rejected entry between two good ones leaves both intact; the archive-from-upload-session path (the session store is stubbed to hand over the finished file, everything after it is real) refuses a forged ZIP and then accepts an honest one; the single-file path refuses a declared length smaller or larger than the content and then stages the honest call (service level - the controller's multipart wiring was not driven through MockMvc); a stream that fails part way leaves no partial object and no item.
  - **Storage adapter coverage:** executed: file-system storage with the default encryption wrapper (integration) and simulated reader behaviours in unit tests. **Not executed:** S3/SeaweedFS-compatible storage and Azure Blob storage (external services not started; no test double of their HTTP behaviour was written). Their handling of a too-long or too-short stream was not observed; the fix does not depend on it, because they now always receive exactly the declared number of bytes, and a length mismatch is detected before the file is accepted.
  - Regression: backend unit suite **1079 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped** (1051 before plus 28 new). Integration selection (the earlier 26 classes plus `RecycleBinPurgeConcurrencyIT`, `BookmarkIT`, `OwnershipOffboardingIT`, `OwnershipBulkIT` and the new `BulkImportSizeEnforcementIT`): **516 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**, BUILD SUCCESS, one run on a fresh database. It includes `DocumentUploadIT` 9, `IngestionZipLimitsIT` 5 and `IngestionLimitDefaultsIT` 2 (DOC-03 and the ingestion limits), `OwnershipBulkIT` 12, the DOC-05/06/07/08/09 classes and the rest of the earlier selection. Related unit classes also ran: `ImportArchiveIntakeTest` 3, `IntakeVirusScannerTest` 4, `ImportRecordCreatorTest` 19, `BulkImportServiceImplTest` 23.
  - **Not run:** the full integration suite; `SeaweedFsDocumentUploadIT`, `OcrCrossServiceE2EIT`, `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` (known unrelated OCR-default failure); the Watermark* ITs; frontend tests (no frontend change); the CSV-template batch creation and the import worker end to end (batches were seeded through repositories); a real upload through the resumable upload-session service.
- **Remaining risks / limitations:**
  - Behaviour on S3/SeaweedFS and Azure with a mismatched length was not observed (see above). The stream is not markable; with the default encryption the S3 adapter already receives a non-markable cipher stream, but a deployment with encryption switched off and an S3 client that relies on mark/reset to retry a failed upload would no longer retry that upload (inference, not tested).
  - A ZIP whose header size differs from its content for a legitimate reason is now refused with a clear message (the JDK reports sizes from the central directory, so ZIPs from normal tools are unaffected; only ZIPs made by common tools were not exhaustively tried).
  - Inflation of an entry is bounded by its declared length, which is bounded by the remaining batch budget; time spent inflating a rejected entry is bounded by that too. The ZIP's own size (`max-archive-size`) and entry count limits are unchanged.
  - Wrong sizes recorded by earlier uploads (before this change) are not corrected.
  - The embedded-signature race and the version-retention race remain open follow-ups.

### DOC-11 – Bulk-import uploads hold no transaction, lock or connection during storage and scan I/O – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the DOC-11 investigation (kept as written, with its two corrections to the DOC-09 record: the "storage I/O inside a transaction" concern is DOC-11, not DOC-12; the encryption key-record deletes use `REQUIRES_NEW` and do **not** roll back with an outer transaction). `receiveFile` ran in one transaction that held the batch row lock and a pooled connection across the storage upload and the virus scan; ten slow uploads starved the whole application, and the `REQUIRES_NEW` key records turned that into a deadlock that only Hikari's 20 s timeout broke.
- **Design checks done before any code change:**
  - **Transaction boundaries (before):** class-level `@Transactional` (`BulkImportServiceImpl:101`) made `receiveFile` one transaction from entry to commit. Its only callers are `BulkImportController.uploadFile` and `ImportArchiveIntake.unpack`; neither is transactional, and nothing calls it from a transactional method. **Finding during implementation:** `@Transactional(propagation = NEVER)` (my first attempt) is **not** enough - it creates an empty transaction with synchronization, which binds an `EntityManager` to the thread for the whole method, and the first repository call during the I/O then keeps a pooled connection until the method returns (DB sessions "idle", Hikari "active"). The new concurrency test caught exactly that (12 concurrent uploads: pool exhausted again, 20 s timeouts, thread dump showed every upload thread waiting for a connection inside a short call while the 10 connections were checked out but idle). The final design gives `receiveFile` **no transactional advice at all**; the class-level annotation was removed and the methods that write (`createBatch`, `submit`, `cancel`) declare `@Transactional` themselves (the readers already did).
  - **Invariants the old lock protected:** tenant and owner (the lock query), batch status `uploading` (cancel / submit cannot interleave), the row is not already staged (no double claim), the cumulative staged-byte cap. The per-batch "maximum item count" is not an upload rule (rows come from the template). Upload permission is checked by `@PreAuthorize` at the endpoint and is not re-checkable in the service layer.
  - **Object keys:** staged objects are only ever found through the key stored on the row (worker, finaliser, `cancel`/`StagedImportFiles`); nothing rebuilds the key, so the key can change shape. Now `imports/<tenant>/<batch>/<item>/<attempt-uuid>`.
  - **Commit outcome:** an exception from `TransactionTemplate.execute` does not say whether the commit happened. The design separates the cases explicitly (below) instead of assuming a rollback.
- **Transaction boundaries (after) - `BulkImportServiceImpl.receiveFile`:**
  1. **Short transaction:** batch row lock, batch still `uploading`, match the row, check the declared size against `max-batch-size - already staged` (overflow-safe), commit, release. Nothing is reserved.
  2. **No transaction:** type check, staging-location lookup, upload to the attempt's own key, DOC-10 exact-length verification, virus scan. A guard (`requireNoTransaction`) fails the call at once if a caller is already inside a transaction.
  3. **Short transaction:** batch row lock again; re-check `uploading`, that the row is still unstaged (`stagedObjectKey` / `scanResultId` null) and that the **counted** size still fits `max-batch-size - already staged`; record the item and its verified size; commit.
- **Races:**
  - cancel commits first -> the upload is refused (`IMPORT_BATCH_NOT_UPLOADING`) and its object removed; upload commits first -> `cancel` sees the item and deletes its object after commit, and the upload's own clean-up never touches it;
  - submit commits first -> refused, object removed, nothing appended to the submitted batch; upload first -> the submitted batch contains the item;
  - two uploads for one row -> one wins, the other is refused (`IMPORT_FILE_ALREADY_RECEIVED`) and removes only its own object; two uploads that fit the cap alone but not together -> one is recorded, the other refused (`IMPORT_BATCH_SIZE_EXCEEDED`) and cleaned up. All decided by the database row lock; no per-instance lock was added.
- **Cleanup and uncertain commit:**
  - a failure in step 2, or a check/write failure inside step 3 (the commit was never attempted) -> the attempt's object is deleted (with its key record); a failed delete is logged as an error and attached to the exception;
  - the checks passed but the commit raised an exception -> the database is asked under the same batch lock (a commit still in flight finishes first) whether the row carries this attempt's key: **recorded** -> success, object kept; **not recorded** -> object deleted, original error rethrown; **cannot be determined** -> object **kept**, error logged, caller gets `503 IMPORT_FILE_STATE_UNCERTAIN`. An accepted file is never deleted on a guess. Such a retained, unreferenced object is not removed automatically (limitation).
  - failure to begin the last transaction (e.g. no connection) is treated as "nothing attempted": the object is deleted.
- **DOC-10 preserved:** the exact-length stream and its post-upload completeness check are unchanged and run in step 2; the budget is checked overflow-safely in steps 1 and 3 (step 3 against the counted size); a rejected attempt records nothing and leaves no object; the existing `BulkImportSizeEnforcementIT` passes unchanged on the new path.
- **Files changed:**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/importexport/service/impl/BulkImportServiceImpl.java`
  - `docs/scanning-architecture.md` (the DOC-10 paragraph corrected; a new DOC-11 paragraph)
  - NEW tests: `importexport/service/impl/BulkImportReceiveFilePhasesTest` (21), `watermark/BulkImportConcurrencyIT` (10)
  - Existing tests changed on purpose: `BulkImportServiceImplTest` and `BulkImportReceiveFileSizeTest` (the service now takes a transaction manager; the staged key now has an attempt suffix; the success-path stubs return the item for the final step). No assertion was weakened.
  - Not changed: the encryption module (`ObjectKeyRecords` still `REQUIRES_NEW`), DOC-09's purge, the worker, `cancel`/`submit` logic, the scanning policy.
- **Tests executed and results** (real PostgreSQL in disposable databases, since dropped; real ClamAV, file storage behind the encryption wrapper, the application's real Hikari pool of 10; one Maven run at a time, checked):
  - `BulkImportReceiveFilePhasesTest` 21/21 (service with a stubbed transaction manager and storage; every step writes an event to one list, so the **order** and the decisions are asserted): the three steps in order with nothing external between a begin and its commit; every attempt gets its own key; refusals in step 1 do no I/O; a caller already inside a transaction is refused; cancel / submit during the I/O refuse the upload and delete only this attempt's object; capacity taken by another upload; the same row taken by another upload (the winner's object is not deleted); exact-boundary budget accepted; storage failure, infected content and a length mismatch never open the last transaction and delete the object; a failing cleanup is attached, not swallowed; a database failure while recording rolls back and deletes the object; a commit that raised but did commit -> success, object kept; a commit that failed and left no row -> object deleted, error rethrown; a commit whose outcome cannot be determined -> object kept, `IMPORT_FILE_STATE_UNCERTAIN`; a failure to begin the last transaction -> object deleted; a guard that `receiveFile` and the class carry no transactional advice while the writing methods do. These tests do **not** prove that no real transaction is open during the I/O - that is the integration test.
  - `BulkImportConcurrencyIT` 10/10 on real infrastructure, deterministic (latches inside the stream and the scanner, no sleeps except a bounded wait loop for the pool to be seen idle): **12 uploads (more than the pool of 10) stalled in storage I/O at once** - the Spring transaction state of every stalled thread is false, **no database session is `idle in transaction`, the batch row can be locked by a `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` from a raw connection outside the pool (so it is not locked), no pooled connection is in use and none is awaited**, an unrelated `select 1`, an upload into the same batch (another row) and an upload into another batch all complete while the 12 are still stalled (their encryption key records get connections), no Spring transaction around the virus scan either, and after release all 12 complete with the right staged sizes and batch total; two uploads whose combined size exceeds the cap both finish their I/O and are finalised together -> exactly one recorded, total within the cap, the refused attempt's object and key record removed; two uploads for one row -> exactly one wins, only the winner's object remains and the row points at it; cancel before the upload is recorded (refused, object removed, batch cancelled) and after (cancel deletes the object, the upload's own clean-up did not); submit before (refused, nothing appended, object removed) and after (the submitted batch contains the item); real ClamAV with the EICAR test string -> `ImportFileBlockedException`, no object, no key record, the refusal recorded on the row, and a later clean file for the same row is accepted and clears it; a stream failing mid-upload leaves no object, no key record and no item, and the row can be uploaded again; a caller inside a transaction is refused before any storage is touched. The first version of the production change (with `Propagation.NEVER`) **failed the first test** (pool exhausted, 20 s timeouts), which is how the connection-holding behaviour of `NEVER` was found; the test passes with the final design.
  - Regression: backend unit suite **1100 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 3 skipped** (1079 before plus 21 new). Integration selection (the earlier 26 classes plus `RecycleBinPurgeConcurrencyIT`, `BookmarkIT`, `OwnershipOffboardingIT`, `OwnershipBulkIT`, `BulkImportSizeEnforcementIT` and the new `BulkImportConcurrencyIT`): **526 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**, BUILD SUCCESS, in one run on a fresh database. It includes `BulkImportSizeEnforcementIT` 9 (DOC-10, unchanged, forged ZIP scenario on the new path), `IngestionZipLimitsIT` 5 and `IngestionLimitDefaultsIT` 2 (DOC-03), `DocumentUploadIT` 9, `OwnershipBulkIT` 12, `RecycleBinPurgeConcurrencyIT` 12 and the DOC-05 to DOC-09 classes. Related unit classes ran too: `BulkImportServiceImplTest` 23, `BulkImportReceiveFileSizeTest` 17, `ImportArchiveIntakeTest` 3, `ExactLengthInputStreamTest` 11, `IntakeVirusScannerTest` 4, `ImportRecordCreatorTest` 19.
  - **Not run:** the full integration suite; `SeaweedFsDocumentUploadIT`, `OcrCrossServiceE2EIT`, `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` (known unrelated OCR-default failure); the Watermark* ITs; frontend tests (no frontend change); S3/SeaweedFS and Azure storage (not started); a commit that genuinely fails against PostgreSQL (the uncertain-commit paths are unit-tested with a stubbed transaction manager - a real lost connection at commit was not produced); the import worker end to end; several application instances; the CSV-template batch creation (batches were seeded through repositories).
  - The new tests were not run against the old code (that would have meant modifying the tree); the old behaviour was reproduced during the investigation.
- **Remaining risks / limitations:**
  - **Behaviour change:** uploads into one batch are no longer serialised: they run in parallel and a conflict (same row, remaining capacity, cancel, submit) is discovered at the end, after the upload work, and refused with the existing error codes; clients must retry. A duplicate filename listed on several rows needs the same retry when two uploads race for it.
  - An object retained after an **uncertain** commit that the database cannot resolve is not cleaned up automatically (it is logged at error level with its key); the staged object keys are no longer predictable from the row, so an orphan from a crash between the upload and the final transaction (a JVM kill) also stays until removed by hand - the same limitation the old code had for a crash after the upload, now with a longer key.
  - Step 2 still makes short non-transactional database reads (staging location, scan engine) and the encryption key records open their own short transactions; these hold a connection only for the call. The nested-connection deadlock on this path no longer exists because no outer transaction holds a connection during them.
  - **DOC-09 is unchanged and the same class of risk remains there** (the purge holds a transaction, the row lock and a connection while deleting files, and its storage deletes use the `REQUIRES_NEW` key records) - inferred, not tested, outside this finding.
  - The S3/Azure retry caveat after DOC-10 (a non-markable stream) is unchanged; an upload retried by an S3 client would now retry outside any transaction.
  - The embedded-signature race and the version-retention race remain open follow-ups.

### DOC-12 – WOPI PutFile streams the save to a private spool file instead of holding it in heap – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the DOC-12 investigation (kept as written). `WopiController.putFile` read the whole save into a `byte[]` (up to 200 MiB, ~2x allocation, retained for the whole ingestion-gate call); measured before the fix: 16 / 32 / 64 MiB saves allocated 32.3 / 64.3 / 128.4 MiB, a chunked 201 MiB request 400.9 MiB, and 3 of 8 concurrent 64 MiB saves failed with `OutOfMemoryError` under a 900 MiB heap.
- **What this entry fixes, and what it deliberately does not (distinct findings):**
  - **PutFile heap buffering: fixed (this entry).**
  - **GetFile: already streaming in constant memory (measured in the investigation); not modified.**
  - **Query-string access token: protocol-related residual risk, unchanged** (token format, lifetime and transport untouched).
  - **PDF preview: bounded by design** (`dms.preview.partial-max-bytes`, 4 x 64 MiB cache); not modified.
  - **Unguarded image-preview branch (`DocumentRenditionService.source`, reads before any size check): separate follow-up, not changed.**
- **Data flow, before -> after.** Before: authorize -> live -> `canWriteNow` -> autosave short-circuit -> declared-length 413 -> `readNBytes` into a `byte[]` -> signature check on a `ByteArrayInputStream` -> gate with `bytes.length` and a second `ByteArrayInputStream` -> audit. After: the same order up to the autosave short-circuit, then **`WopiSaveSpool.receive`**: declared length over the cap -> 413 before anything is created; otherwise the body is streamed (8 KiB buffers) to a private file while it is counted and capped -> `EmbeddedSignatureDetector` on a stream over the file (unchanged) -> the gate gets a stream over the file and the **counted** length -> audit (same event, same `bytes` field) -> file deleted in a `finally`. The controller keeps its response contract: the same 401/404/409/413/422 and the same body.
- **Actual-byte limit.** The existing 200 MiB cap is unchanged (`MAX_SAVE_BYTES`) and enforced on the bytes that arrive through the existing `SizeLimitingInputStream` (verified at the boundary before reuse: it throws when the running count exceeds the cap, so exactly the cap passes and cap + 1 is refused; reading stops within one buffer). A declared Content-Length over the cap is refused before a file is created; Content-Length is never taken as the size; chunked requests work. After the copy the file length is checked against the count. No new size limit was introduced.
- **Embedded-signature compatibility.** `EmbeddedSignatureDetector` is untouched and still authoritative; it reads a stream over the spool file. Real-gate integration tests confirm refusal (409) and no gate call / no version / no scan row for a signed OOXML (`_xmlsignatures/`), a signed ODF (`META-INF/documentsignatures.xml`) and a signed PDF (`/ByteRange`), and acceptance of an unsigned ODF and PDF.
- **Temporary-file security.** A dedicated directory (`dms.editing.spool-dir`, default `<java.io.tmpdir>/oak-dms-wopi-spool`), created 0700 on POSIX and refused if it is not a plain directory (checked without following links) owned by the service user (repaired when ours and too open); files `wopi-save-<random uuid>.part`, created exclusively (`CREATE_NEW`) with owner-only permissions; no request-derived path component; nothing serves the directory; no content or token is logged. The spool holds plaintext while a save is processed - the same as ordinary uploads (Spring multipart temp files) and the encryption wrapper's temporary copy; documented.
- **Disk-budget protection (per instance, small and configurable).** `dms.editing.spool-max-active` (default 32; a further save waits `spool-wait`, default 15 s, then **503**) and `dms.editing.spool-min-free-bytes` (default 512 MiB; a save is **refused 507** unless the volume keeps that much free after counting the declared size, or the whole cap for a chunked request, of the saves in progress). Slots and reservations are returned on every path, including a failed directory creation (tested). No global upload-size restriction was added.
- **Crash-safe cleanup.** `close()` removes the file in a `finally` (a failed delete is logged by file name and left for the sweep). For a JVM that dies: every live spool file holds an operating-system lock on one byte far beyond its data; the scheduled sweep (`spool-sweep-interval` default 15 min, first run after 2 min) deletes a file only if it is a regular file (never a link), named like a spool file, older than `spool-abandoned-after` (default 6 h) **and** that lock can be taken - proof that no save in this process or another instance is using it (a dead process's locks are released by the OS). Other files are never touched. No database, no migration. The cross-process behaviour was tested with a real child JVM that took the lock (the sweep left the file alone) and was then killed (the sweep removed it).
- **Transaction boundaries (verified against PostgreSQL, not inferred).** The controller is not transactional and the spool is a plain bean (no proxy advice). With eight saves stalled half way through their bodies: the Spring transaction state of each request thread is "none", **no database session is `idle in transaction`**, **no pooled connection is in use** (Hikari MXBean) and none is awaited, and an unrelated query still runs. The gate keeps its own short transactions.
- **Failure handling (what is covered and how).** Tested on real files / real gate: invalid, expired and wrong-document token (401, body never read, nothing spooled); lock lost mid-session (409), deleted document (404), autosave (acknowledged, body never read, nothing stored) - none touch the spool; declared oversize (413, nothing created); chunked oversize (413 after the cap, file removed); exactly the cap accepted and cap + 1 refused (real 200 MiB constant, generated stream); stream failure part way (error propagated, file removed, slot returned); directory that cannot be created or is a regular file (error, no leaked slot); disk guard (507) and slot guard (503); signature refusal; real-gate refusal (a password-protected PDF -> 422, no version, spool empty); a failing gate (exception propagates, spool empty); already-deleted file on close; a delete refused by the file system (POSIX only); abandoned files, active files, other files, links, repeated sweeps; many concurrent saves. **Not simulated:** a real `ENOSPC` (disk-full) during the write - the write error would propagate as an `IOException` and the same cleanup applies, but only the free-space guard was exercised; a database failure at the gate's final persistence (the gate is unchanged; a thrown gate exception is covered); a client disconnect on a real servlet container (an `IOException` from the body stream is covered by the stream-failure test); the POSIX permission and delete-refused tests were **skipped on this Windows host** (2 skipped), so 0700/0600 modes and Linux file-lock semantics were not executed here.
- **Files changed:**
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/editing/WopiSaveSpool.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/editing/WopiController.java` (`putFile`; a new constructor dependency; comments)
  - `docs/checkout-design.md` (a new DOC-12 section: spooling, the cap, signature check, location and permissions, disk safeguards and settings, cleanup and crash recovery, operational disk and antivirus notes)
  - NEW tests: `editing/WopiSaveSpoolTest` (23), `editing/WopiSaveMemoryIT` (4), `watermark/WopiSaveSpoolIT` (9, real ingestion gate)
  - Not changed: GetFile, the token codec and its lifetime, the encryption and storage modules, the ingestion gate, any earlier fix, external-sharing code, `application.yml` (all settings have defaults; see the docs).
- **Tests executed and results** (real PostgreSQL in disposable databases, since dropped; real ClamAV and encrypted file storage; one Maven run at a time):
  - `WopiSaveSpoolTest` 23 run, 0 failures, 2 skipped (POSIX-only), stable over repeated runs. It found a real defect in my first version (two simultaneous first requests could treat the other's freshly created directory as "not a plain directory"), fixed before this record.
  - **`WopiSaveSpoolIT` 9/9 with the real ingestion gate** (not the mock used by `WopiEditingIT`): an edited .docx is spooled, checked, handed to the gate and becomes version 2 - `size_bytes` is the counted size, `file_hash` equals the body's SHA-256, scan status clean, reason `replacement_upload`, the stored object is byte-for-byte the body, `current_version_id` points to it, exactly one `DOCUMENT_EDIT_SESSION_SAVED` audit event, the check-out is kept, the spool is empty afterwards; plus the signature, refusal, failure, authorization-before-reading and limit cases above.
  - **`WopiSaveMemoryIT` 4/4** - the same measure as the investigation (bytes allocated by the request thread, exact), the gate stubbed so the controller and spool are measured on their own:

    | Scenario | Before the fix | After |
    |---|---:|---:|
    | PutFile 16 MiB, declared | 32.3 MiB | 3.07 MiB (first call; JIT/class loading) |
    | PutFile 32 MiB, declared | 64.3 MiB | 0.24 MiB |
    | PutFile 64 MiB, declared | 128.4 MiB | 0.29 MiB |
    | PutFile 32 MiB, chunked | 66.6 MiB | 0.20 MiB |
    | PutFile 201 MiB, declared | rejected early | 0.11 MiB (413) |
    | PutFile 201 MiB, no Content-Length | 400.9 MiB | 0.11 MiB (413 after the cap) |

    Allocation no longer scales with the document (4x the size, under 1 MiB difference).
  - **Concurrent saves under a controlled heap** (`-Xmx900m`, 64 MiB each, all held mid-body at once): **K = 8: all 8 saved, 0 `OutOfMemoryError`, worst per-save allocation 1.56 MiB; K = 16: all 16 saved, worst 1.10 MiB** - the investigation's K = 8 case had 3 `OutOfMemoryError` failures. One private file per save in progress while stalled; every file removed afterwards; no cross-request deletion; no pooled connection and no transaction held (above).
  - Regression, one build, one run: backend unit suite **1131 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 5 skipped** (3 earlier + the 2 POSIX-only); integration selection (the DOC-11 selection plus the two new ITs) **539 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**, including `WopiEditingIT` 39 (unchanged and passing), `DocumentCheckoutIT` 28, `DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT` 8 (DOC-05), `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` 23 and `VersionPromotionRefusalIT` 10 (DOC-06/07/08), `RecycleBinPurgeConcurrencyIT` 12 (DOC-09), `BulkImportSizeEnforcementIT` 9 (DOC-10), `BulkImportConcurrencyIT` 10 (DOC-11); unit `WopiTokenCodecTest` 6, `EditingEngineTest` 4, `EmbeddedSignatureDetectorTest` 9. Source hashes of the whole working tree were identical before and after the run (124 files), so the run is of one stable snapshot that includes the external-sharing work as it stood at 16:05.
  - **Not run:** the full integration suite; `SeaweedFsDocumentUploadIT`, `OcrCrossServiceE2EIT`, `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` (known unrelated failure); the Watermark* ITs; frontend tests; a real Collabora engine; Linux/container behaviour (permissions, file locks, an unwritable temp directory); a real disk-full condition; the 200 MiB scale through the **real gate** (the exact 200 MiB boundary was tested at the spool and the 413 paths at the controller; a real gate scan of 200 MiB was not run).
- **Remaining risks / limitations (and follow-ups, not part of this fix):**
  - The spool holds the document in **plaintext on the temp volume** while a save is processed (documented; consistent with ordinary uploads). A host's on-access antivirus sees it (on this Windows development host Defender refused to let the EICAR test string be written to the spool, so the infected-file case was tested with a password-protected PDF instead); on such hosts the spool directory needs an exclusion or saves of flagged content will fail with a 500.
  - OS file locks are the active-file proof: they are reliable on a local volume, not guaranteed on every network file system - keep the spool directory local to each instance. A file left by a crash stays until the next sweep after the age threshold.
  - The 200 MiB cap is still a hard-coded constant, so a document over 200 MiB can be opened in the editor but not saved back (unchanged behaviour); making it configurable is a possible follow-up.
  - **Unchanged follow-ups from the investigation:** the query-string token (valid up to 10 h; a leaked token reads the current version), GetFile's stream not being closed if the async body never runs and the container's async timeout for very large downloads (both unverified), the image-preview size guard, whether Collabora sends a Content-Length on PutFile (a missing one is handled as chunked).
  - The DOC-09 purge pool hazard, the DOC-10 S3/Azure retry behaviour, abandoned per-attempt bulk-import objects, the embedded-signature promotion race and the version-retention versus legal-hold race remain open follow-ups.

### DOC-13 – A failed copy or a failed ingestion request leaves no partial object and no promotable row – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (exception-driven cases)

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the DOC-13 investigation (kept as written). Three behaviours were confirmed: `StorageObjectMover.copyVerified` left a partial destination object when the upload threw (and the retry used a new version id and key, so it was never revisited and nothing garbage-collects the document location); `IngestionGateImpl.submit` let an exception after the row existed escape while the row stayed `pending` (the scheduler could promote it later although the caller was told it failed); and a partial staging object stayed until the 72 h failed-retention sweep.
- **Root cause.** Cleanup existed only for failures after `copyVerified` returned and for a `StorageException` thrown by the staging upload itself; every other exception path left the object and the pending row to chance.
- **What changed.**
  - `StorageObjectMover.copyVerified` (new overload with `Destination.NEW_UNREFERENCED | SHARED`; the shorter overloads keep `SHARED`): any `RuntimeException`/`Error` after the destination may have been written removes the object at that destination key and rethrows the **original** exception; a failed removal is attached as a suppressed exception and logged (key and location, no content). A hash mismatch still always removes the destination. A source that cannot be opened never touches the destination. The source is never deleted.
  - Callers that own a fresh key declare it: `IngestionProcessor.promote` (a new version id per `prepare`), `QuarantineReleaseService.overrideRelease` (a new plan per call) and the re-scan staging copy (the new attempt's own staging key). `IngestionProcessor.isolate` keeps `SHARED`: its deterministic quarantine key is overwritten by a retry, so nothing accumulates, and a committed record might reference it.
  - `IngestionGateImpl.submit`: the steps after the pending row exists (staging upload, ZIP/OOXML checks, duplicate checks) are guarded; an unexpected `RuntimeException` calls the new `IngestionProcessor.abandonUnacceptedSubmission(id, leaseToken, reason)` and is then rethrown unchanged (cleanup failures are suppressed onto it). The hand-off to the scan worker (`handOff`/`process`) is outside the guard.
  - `abandonUnacceptedSubmission` acts only on a row that is still `pending`, only through `failTerminal` (conditional on this request's lease token) and `purgeStaging` (conditional on a terminal status): a row a worker has claimed keeps its row and its staged object; a settled row is left alone; a missing row is ignored.
- **Ownership guarantees (verified from source).** `promote`/`overrideRelease` keys are `documents/{tenant}/{document}/v{n}/{new UUID}` generated per attempt, referenced by nothing before the commit that inserts the version row. The re-scan copy goes to `staging/{tenant}/{new attempt id}`. A retry always uses a different key, so cleanup of attempt A cannot touch attempt B or any committed version (tested).
- **Encryption wrapper decision.** `EncryptingObjectStorageService.undo()` is **unchanged**: it also restores a previous key record when an upload overwrites an existing object, so making it delete the object could destroy committed content; the explicit destination cleanup of exclusively owned keys covers the orphan instead. Through the wrapper the removal calls `delete`, which deletes the object first and the key record afterwards, so a failed object delete keeps the key record. After a failed encrypted upload the wrapper's own `undo()` has already removed (or restored) the key record; verified that no key record remains for the failed destination and that a promoted object has exactly its own key record and decrypts to the uploaded bytes.
- **Transactions and connections.** No new transaction and none held across I/O: `copyVerified` and the cleanup run outside any transaction in every caller (the copy precedes each caller's short transaction), `abandonUnacceptedSubmission` is a read, `failTerminal` (one short lease-conditional transaction), the storage delete, then one short transaction to mark the purge. Verified from source; the new real-database tests ran on the application's normal pool.
- **Asynchronous races (deterministic, no sleeps).** Gate failure vs a worker that already promoted the row: the worker's outcome stands, exactly one new version, no staged object left, nothing undone. Gate failure vs a worker that holds a new lease: the row stays `pending` and leased, the staged object is untouched, and the worker then finishes normally. Attempt A's failure handling vs attempt B (a retry that runs to completion while A's partial object still exists): A's object is removed, B's remains and is the version's object, the content reads back correctly. A failing cleanup: the original upload failure is what is recorded on the row, the leftover object is reported (logged), and the retry still promotes.
- **Retention.** Unchanged (72 h for `failed` rows whose staged object was not purged). A conclusively rejected submission is now purged at once; a row whose staged object cannot be removed stays failed and not-marked-purged, so the existing sweep still finds it (tested: not due at 72 h, due with zero retention).
- **Files changed.**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/helper/StorageObjectMover.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/service/impl/IngestionProcessor.java` (the `promote` copy call; new `abandonUnacceptedSubmission`)
