  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/service/impl/IngestionGateImpl.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/scanning/service/impl/QuarantineReleaseService.java`
  - `docs/scanning-architecture.md` (two failure-mode rows, one corrected row, a DOC-13 paragraph)
  - NEW tests: `scanning/helper/StorageObjectMoverTest` (14), `scanning/service/impl/IngestionFailureHandlingTest` (10), `watermark/IngestionFailureCleanupIT` (8)
  - Not changed: the encryption module, the filesystem/S3/Azure adapters, the scheduler and retry logic, retention settings, external sharing, DOC-01 to DOC-12 code.
- **Tests executed and results** (isolated copy of the verified snapshot plus the working tree; disposable PostgreSQL databases inside the existing container; real ClamAV and RabbitMQ; one Maven run at a time):
  - `StorageObjectMoverTest` 14/14, `IngestionFailureHandlingTest` 10/10, `IngestionFailureCleanupIT` 8/8 (real gate, processor, database, file storage behind the encryption wrapper, ClamAV).
  - Backend unit suite: **1155 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 5 skipped** (1131 before plus 24 new).
  - Full integration suite (all 111 IT classes, one JVM): 920 run, 2 failures, 72 errors, 7 skipped. The 72 errors were all `FATAL: too many clients already` while creating Spring contexts (the suite loads more distinct contexts than the database allows connections); the 9 affected classes (`RecycleBinPurgeConcurrencyIT`, `WatermarkDisabledIT`, `WatermarkOfficePipelineIT`, `WatermarkPipelineIT`, `WatermarkQueueFullIT`, `WatermarkServiceIT`, `WatermarkUnrenderableTextIT`, `WatermarkWorkerFailureIT`, `WopiSaveSpoolIT`) were re-run in a fresh JVM: **72 run, 0 failures, 0 errors**. The 2 failures are `OcrEventsConsumerLiveIT` and `OcrJobTokenHealthGroupIT` (live OCR worker); they fail identically on a copy of the earlier verified snapshot that does not contain this change, so they are pre-existing and unrelated.
  - **Mutation check** (separate copy; three deliberate defects: cleanup disabled in `copyVerified`, the gate's abandon call removed, `abandonUnacceptedSubmission` made to delete the staged object unconditionally): 13 of the 24 unit tests and 5 of the 8 integration tests fail; the unconditional-delete defect is caught by the unit test for a worker-claimed row.
  - The original defects were also reproduced against the unmodified code in the investigation (private probe).
- **Remaining risks / limitations (not fixed here).**
  - **Crash and uncertain-outcome leftovers:** a JVM or host death mid-write, a storage call with an unknown outcome, and a removal that itself fails leave an unreferenced object (after a failed encrypted upload it is undecryptable ciphertext with no key record). Objects orphaned before this change remain. A reconciliation sweep for the document location (option C) and atomic writes in the filesystem adapter (option B) are the future hardening; neither was approved here.
  - If the database is also unavailable when the gate tries to abandon a row, the old behaviour remains for that request: the row ends with the lease expiry, and a row whose hash was recorded can still be scanned and promoted by the scheduler although the caller saw an error.
  - A failure in the hand-off to the scan worker is deliberately not treated as a rejection (the submission is accepted); an uncertain outcome of the pending-row insert has no staged object yet, so nothing is left to clean.
  - `isolate` (quarantine) still leaves a partial object until its retry overwrites the deterministic key.
  - Not verified: real S3 and Azure partial-write behaviour (the S3/Azure adapters were not exercised), Linux file semantics, behaviour under a process kill.

### DOC-14 – Folder structure changes run one at a time per tenant; the subtree query ends on a cycle – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (folder-tree races)

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the DOC-14 investigation (kept as written). `FolderService.create/move/delete` checked the tree and wrote afterwards in READ COMMITTED transactions with no lock, so concurrent changes each passed against the old tree. Reproduced against PostgreSQL: opposite moves made a cycle (both folders unreachable, the cycle query never ending), two moves each inside the depth limit reached depth 21, and a child created while its parent was deleted stayed live under it. Duplicate sibling names were already prevented by `uq_folders_live_sibling_name` (no change). Recommended severity in the investigation: Low likelihood / Medium worst case.
- **Root cause.** The cycle, depth and live-parent rules are tree-shaped, so the database cannot enforce them with a constraint; the application checked them, but not under any lock, and `findSubtreeIds` used `UNION ALL`, which does not end on a cycle.
- **Hierarchy-mutation inventory (verified from source).** Only `FolderService.create`, `move` and `delete` change `parent_folder_id` or the deletion state (writers of the `folders` table: `FolderService`; `FolderAccessService` writes only the access mode and `ConfidentialService` only the confidential flag; there is no folder restore, import, bulk reparenting or background job). Their only callers are the three `FolderController` endpoints. No further mutation path needed locking.
- **What changed.**
  - `FolderService`: a private `lockTree(tenantId)` is the first statement of `create` (after the pure name validation), `move` and `delete`; it calls `FolderRepository.lockHierarchy(key)` with key `folder-tree:{tenantId}`. `rename`, the read operations and the access/confidential writers are deliberately not locked. No other behaviour, message, audit event or response changed.
  - `FolderRepository.lockHierarchy`: `select pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(:key, 0))` (the convention `BookmarkService` already uses). Transaction-scoped: released by commit or rollback, taken inside the service's own `@Transactional` method on the same connection as the validation and writes (no `REQUIRES_NEW`, no session lock, no JVM mutex). The key is hashed by PostgreSQL, so all instances sharing the database agree; one lock per tenant, never a global one. Tenant-key collisions (64 bit) could only add an unnecessary wait; a clash with another application advisory lock would need an identical 64-bit value.
  - `FolderRepository.findSubtreeIds`: `UNION ALL` -> `UNION`. The recursive step carries only the folder id (checked before changing it), so a folder already in the result is not expanded again: valid trees return the same set, a cycle now ends with each folder once, tenant and deleted filters unchanged. A comment warns against adding depth/path columns without a cycle guard.
  - Other recursive walks over parents were inspected and need no change (all carry a visited set or a depth cap): `treePath`, `depthBelow`, `FolderAccessView.governingOf`, `RetentionResolver.folderChain`, `ExportArchiveBuilder`, `ShareTargets`; `findSubtreeIds` was the only recursive SQL.
- **Transactions and locks.** The lock is taken before any folder is read in the transaction (no stale entity in the persistence context), held to commit, and the protected section is a few queries plus the audit row (no storage, scan, network or messaging I/O). Lock order: the folder lock first; `AuditService`'s chain lock only afterwards, and nothing takes the audit lock before a folder lock, so no deadlock. A lock wait holds one pooled connection for the length of another folder change (milliseconds); there is no `lock_timeout`, so a stuck transaction holding the lock would block that tenant's folder changes (reads unaffected).
- **Files changed.**
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/folder/FolderService.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/folder/FolderRepository.java`
  - NEW `docs/folder-tree-design.md` (rules, lock identity and collision analysis, transaction requirements, protected and unprotected paths, lock order, cycle-safe query, operations, the remaining gap) and NEW `docs/folder-integrity-check.sql` (read-only diagnostic)
  - NEW tests: `folder/FolderServiceLockTest` (13), `watermark/FolderTreeConcurrencyIT` (11)
  - Not changed: `Folder.java`, `FolderAccessService`, `ConfidentialService` and the rest of the external-sharing work, the schema (no migration), the controller, `DocumentVersionPromoter`, `DocumentServiceImpl`, SEC-01 and DOC-01 to DOC-13 code.
- **Tests executed and results** (isolated copy of the verified snapshot plus the working tree; disposable PostgreSQL databases inside the existing container; one Maven run at a time):
  - `FolderServiceTest` 6/6 (unchanged) and `FolderServiceLockTest` 13/13: the lock is taken first and once by `create`, `move` and `delete` (verified with ordered mock interactions), not by `rename` or the read operations; the key is `folder-tree:{tenant}` and differs per tenant; a name refused before it is never locked; a lock failure reads and writes nothing; every rule that refused before still refuses after the lock (name taken, into own subtree, has subfolders, depth 20 for create and move, denied caller, missing/deleted parent; normal create, move and delete still succeed). Mocks are not used as the proof of concurrency.
  - `FolderTreeConcurrencyIT` 11/11 on real PostgreSQL, deterministic: the first operation is held inside its critical section by a row lock held from a separate session, and the test waits until the second operation is **seen queued on the advisory lock** (`pg_locks`) before releasing, so "taken before any read" is itself asserted. Results: opposite moves -> one applied, the other refused `FOLDER_MOVE_INTO_ITSELF`, no cycle, both folders reachable; the two depth-limit moves -> the second refused `FOLDER_TOO_DEEP`, `z` stays at depth 1; create against delete in both orders -> the create finds the parent deleted (404), or the delete is refused `FOLDER_NOT_EMPTY`; never a live child under a deleted parent; a seeded cycle (direct SQL) -> `findSubtreeIds` ends under a 5 s statement timeout and lists each folder once, valid-tree membership and the deleted/other-tenant filters unchanged; another tenant's create finished while tenant one was inside its critical section and nobody queued for it; a rolled-back transaction released the lock (held inside it, none afterwards, re-acquirable at once); a validation failure after the lock left nothing held and the next operation proceeded; a lock held by a separate database session (another application instance) blocked the service until it committed (a JVM-local mutex would not pass this); after the operations no pooled connection was active or idle-in-transaction; every test ends asserting that no advisory lock survives in the database.
  - Mutation check (separate copy, the lock calls removed and `UNION` reverted to `UNION ALL`): 8 of the 13 unit tests fail; in the integration suite the depth test fails (the second operation never queues on the advisory lock) and the cycle test fails (query cancelled by the statement timeout); the opposite-move and create/delete tests did not complete within the test's own bounds (a hung run, killed) - a hung run is not a pass, but those two did not produce a clean failure message. The whole mutated suite did not finish, so no full count exists for it.
  - Backend unit suite: **1168 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 5 skipped** (1155 before plus 13 new).
  - Full integration suite, run in six sequential batches in fresh JVMs (no connection exhaustion this time): **956 run (115 classes), 3 failures, 14 errors, 7 skipped.** All of them are in five classes that also fail on a copy of the earlier verified snapshot without this change: `OcrEventsConsumerLiveIT` and `OcrJobTokenHealthGroupIT` (live OCR worker; verified the same way during DOC-13), `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` (the known OCR-default failure), and `ImpersonationIT` (11) and `PermissionCodeAuditIT` (3; 4 on the snapshot), whose Keycloak admin calls return HTTP 401 - environmental, and unrelated to folders. Everything else passed, including all folder-, document-, scanning-, check-out-, purge-, bulk-import, WOPI- and tenant-isolation tests listed under DOC-01 to DOC-13.
- **Read-only integrity diagnostic.** `docs/folder-integrity-check.sql` lists, by id only, cycles, folders deeper than 20, live folders under deleted parents, cross-tenant or missing parents, and (for the follow-up) live documents in deleted folders. It was validated on a disposable database (it found the cycle left by the mutated run). **It has not been run against any development or production database** (no authorized access), so whether existing data already holds a cycle or a child under a deleted parent is **not verified**; run it before relying on the tree, and plan any repair separately (nothing here repairs data).
- **Remaining risks / limitations (not fixed here).**
  - **Document-side placement race (separate follow-up, inferred and not reproduced):** `DocumentVersionPromoter` and `DocumentServiceImpl.moveDocuments` can still file a document into a folder that is being deleted; the existing pending-upload check in `delete` covers most of that window. Fix on the document side (folder row `FOR SHARE` + `NOT is_deleted`; `delete` locking the row `FOR UPDATE` before counting), not with the tenant lock.
  - Folder structure changes of one tenant now queue; a transaction stuck while holding the lock blocks that tenant's folder changes (no `lock_timeout`). Writers of `parent_folder_id` or deletion outside `FolderService` would not take the lock (none exist today; a database constraint trigger would be the stronger backstop and needs a migration).
  - A tree already corrupted before this fix is not repaired.
  - Not verified: frontend behaviour on a cycle or a folder under a deleted parent; real HTTP-level concurrency; production data.

- **DOC-14 verification-database cleanup (recorded 2026-11-12; the approved remediation status above is unchanged).** With explicit authorization, the two remaining disposable databases `dms_doc14_impl` and `dms_doc14_mut` were dropped by exact name, after checking that they held only "Watermark test tenant ..." fixture rows, had no active sessions, no test process was running, and they were the names created for DOC-14; `dms_doc14_base` and `dms_doc14_probe` were already gone. The shared `dms_core_db` and the Postgres volume were not touched.

### DOC-15 – One standards-compliant `Content-Disposition` builder for every file download – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (backend header; the web client's Unicode-name display is a separate follow-up)

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 and the DOC-15 investigation (kept as written). `DocumentController.sanitizeForHeader` (and a copy in `WorkflowInstanceController`) replaced only CR, LF and `"`; the header was hand-built as `filename="<raw name>"`. Confirmed by test: for a name with any character above U+00FF (Sinhala, Tamil, emoji, a bidi override) **Tomcat silently drops the whole header** (HTTP 200, no filename, no `attachment`); the web client's parser throws `URIError` for `%` and truncates at `;`; Latin-1 letters went out as raw bytes. Not reproduced: CR/LF header injection (blocked) and the "trailing backslash" break-out (unreachable: stored names must end in an allowed extension).
- **Root cause.** The header value was built from the raw stored name, with no encoding: no ASCII fallback and no RFC 5987 `filename*`, so any non-Latin-1 name made Tomcat discard the header. Spring's `ContentDisposition` builder was evaluated and rejected for these endpoints: with a UTF-8 charset it emits `filename*` correctly, but its ASCII fallback keeps `%` and `;` (and a raw `%` makes the web client's `decodeURIComponent` throw), so it cannot satisfy the fallback requirements.
- **What changed.**
  - NEW `document/ContentDispositionHeader` (`of(type, name)`, `attachment(name)`, `inline(name)`): returns the complete header value, ASCII only, `type; filename="<fallback>"[; filename*=UTF-8''<percent-encoded name>]`. `filename*` is sent only when the name is not already plain safe ASCII, so ordinary names keep today's exact `filename="report.pdf"` form. The type must be `attachment` or `inline` (anything else is refused); the utility never decides inline versus attachment.
  - Name normalisation (header only; the stored name and storage keys are untouched): `/` and `\` are separators and only the last segment is kept; control characters (C0, DEL, C1), line/paragraph separators, unpaired surrogates and invisible format characters (bidi overrides, zero-width space, word joiner, BOM, soft hyphen, tag characters) are removed; the zero-width joiner and non-joiner are **kept** (Sinhala, Tamil and emoji sequences need them); spaces and leading/trailing joiners are trimmed; empty, `.` and `..` become `document`, `.pdf` alone becomes `document.pdf`; quotes, semicolons and percent signs are valid name content, kept and percent-encoded in `filename*`. Limit: 255 UTF-8 bytes (common file-system limit; at most about 765 encoded characters), cut between code points, keeping an extension of up to 20 bytes.
  - ASCII fallback: letters, digits and ` . _ - ( ) [ ] + , & @ # ! ~` stay, each run of anything else becomes one `_`, never `% ; " \` or a control; if nothing readable is left of the base name it is `document` (extension kept), so a Sinhala `ගොනුව.pdf` falls back to `document.pdf`.
  - Call sites (the four hand-built sites and the Spring builder without a charset): `DocumentController.downloadDocument`, `previewPdf`, `previewVersionPdf`, `downloadVersion` (disposition still from `ContentServingPolicy.dispositionType` or fixed `inline` for the PDF previews); `WorkflowInstanceController.supportingDocumentContent`; `WatermarkController.result` (always `attachment`). Both copies of `sanitizeForHeader` removed after their callers were migrated.
- **DOC-04 preserved.** Disposition type, `Content-Type`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, the sandbox CSP, streaming and authorization are untouched; verified on the wire (below).
- **Files changed.**
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/ContentDispositionHeader.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentController.java` (four sites, helper removed)
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/workflow/controller/WorkflowInstanceController.java` (one site, helper removed)
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/watermark/controller/WatermarkController.java` (one site, unused import removed)
  - NEW `docs/download-headers.md`
  - NEW tests: `document/ContentDispositionHeaderTest` (9), `document/DownloadHeadersWireTest` (3)
  - Not changed: `AuditEventController` and `ExportJobController` (already correct), `BulkImportController` and `OwnershipHistoryService` (fixed ASCII names), `SharedFileResponse` and the rest of the external-sharing work, `ContentServingPolicy`, the frontend (see below), authorization, storage, schema.
- **Tests executed and results** (private copy of the verified snapshot plus the working tree; no database was available - WSL and the Postgres container were down and were not touched):
  - `ContentDispositionHeaderTest` 9/9: 33 international and hostile names (Sinhala, Tamil, emoji, Latin-1, `%`, `%20`, `;`, `"`, `\`, `../`, CR/LF and a header-injection attempt, NUL, C0/C1 controls, bidi override, zero-width characters, line separators, unpaired surrogate, empty, blank, `..`, only-unsupported, 400-character and 900-byte names) each for `attachment` and `inline`: ASCII only, no control characters, exact header shape, fallback free of `% ; " \`, `filename*` decodes to the expected sanitised Unicode name, Spring's `ContentDisposition.parse` returns it, header at most 1100 characters, code points never split, extension kept; exact encodings for Sinhala, Tamil, emoji, `é`; the joiners that spell Sinhala kept; invalid dispositions refused.
  - `DownloadHeadersWireTest` 3/3, **real embedded Tomcat 11.0.25 (the pinned version), the real `DocumentController`, `WorkflowInstanceController` and `WatermarkController` served through Spring MVC, raw response bytes read from a socket:** for 8 routes x 15 names (including Sinhala, Tamil, emoji, `%`, `;`, quotes, backslash, traversal, a CR/LF header-injection name and a name of only unsupported characters) the response is 200 with exactly one ASCII `Content-Disposition`, the intended disposition, the sanitised name, no injected header and the body intact; Sinhala/Tamil/emoji names keep their header (`attachment; filename="document.pdf"; filename*=UTF-8''%E0%B6%9C...`); the DOC-04 matrix (XML, JSON, SVG, text, HTML, PDF, PNG, JPEG x seven hostile or international names x three routes) keeps `inline` only for PDF/PNG/JPEG and `attachment` for the rest, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, the sandbox CSP for everything but PDF, the content type, and the streamed body. Limits: `@PreAuthorize` and the services are mocked (not under test here).
  - **Mutation check (separate copy; the utility made to emit the original `filename="<raw name>"` construction):** all 3 wire tests and 6 of the 9 utility tests fail; through the real controller route the Sinhala header is absent (`Content-Disposition` count 0), reproducing the original defect.
  - **Frontend compatibility (the frontend's own regex and `decodeURIComponent`, extracted from `documents/api.ts`, `preview/api.ts` and `watermarks/api.ts` - all four sites use the same pattern - run against the new headers):** no `URIError`, no truncation, extension kept for every name, `%` and `%20` never decoded, quotes and semicolons never cut; the UI takes the ASCII fallback, so a Sinhala or Tamil file is saved as `document.pdf` (previously the header was dropped and the UI used the extension-less `document`), `Report 100%.pdf` as `Report 100_.pdf`, `café.pdf` as `caf_.pdf`. The confirmed UI download failures are therefore no longer reproducible; **the true Unicode name in the UI needs a separate small frontend change** (prefer `filename*`, decode it without throwing) - not done here.
  - Backend unit suite, whole suite in one JVM: **1180 run, 0 failures, 39 errors, 5 skipped.** All 39 errors are `Connection to localhost:5433 refused` in the 11 database-backed classes that failed identically in the last database-down run (`V134...`, `DmsCoreApplicationTests`, `ExportJobRepositoryTest`, `V41`, `V42`, `V47V48`, `V103...`, `V123...`, `V106...`, `V91...`, `V95...`); the 12 new tests are among the passing ones. (An earlier run showed 27 further errors in four Mockito-based classes: my wire test had created mocks on a Tomcat thread, which Mockito cached with the web-app class loader; fixed by creating every mock on the test thread before Tomcat starts, and re-run clean.)
  - **Database-backed integration tests:** first blocked by an unavailable database, then run and passed - see "Final database-backed regression verification" below.
- **Remaining risks / limitations.**
  - The UI shows the ASCII fallback (e.g. `document.pdf`) for Sinhala/Tamil names until its parser prefers `filename*` (frontend follow-up).
  - Not verified: real-browser behaviour (file naming, inline rendering) and a rewriting reverse proxy. Approximately 70 other integration classes were not re-run (see the final verification below).
  - Behaviour change to expect: a name containing `/` or `\` now downloads as its last segment; control and invisible characters are removed from the downloaded name.
  - Same-class sites not touched: `AuditEventController`, `ExportJobController` (correct), `SharedFileResponse` (another developer's, already correct in shape); a future change could share the utility.

- **Final database-backed regression verification (2026-11-13) - PASSED.**
  - **Environment (isolated and local).** A private snapshot of the working tree (`verification\doc15-verify`, 1,548 source files, byte-identical to the working tree at the start, containing the DOC-15 implementation: `ContentDispositionHeader` used four times in `DocumentController` and once each in `WorkflowInstanceController` and `WatermarkController`, no `sanitizeForHeader` left) and a disposable PostgreSQL database `dms_doc15_verify` (empty at the start, built by Flyway) on the local Ubuntu/WSL2 Docker engine. Dependencies `oakdms-postgres` (2da70708c958), `oakdms-clamav` (dc639b641b39) and `oakdms-rabbitmq` (982e8ebe2de0) were healthy throughout with 0 restarts and an unchanged Postgres start time; the laptop was on AC power with no sleep event during the run. Maven: `./mvnw -B -o`, a fresh JVM per batch, run sequentially. Production and remote systems were not accessed; no application or test code was changed for the verification.
  - **Focused tests: 12/12, 0 skipped.** `ContentDispositionHeaderTest` 9/9; `DownloadHeadersWireTest` 3/3 (real embedded Tomcat 11.0.25 serving the real `DocumentController`, `WorkflowInstanceController` and `WatermarkController`).
  - **Database-backed integration tests: 419/419 passed over 45 integration classes - 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped** (`failsafe:integration-test -Dit.test=<batch>`):

### DOC-16 – The tenant's default download watermark applies to every explicit download, including a named version – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (signed files are deliberately exempt)

- **Original finding / investigation:** see §9 (DOC-16, kept as written) and the reproduction evidence in `verification\doc16-verify` (database `dms_doc16_verify`, both kept). Confirmed by test: `GET /documents/{id}/content` applied the tenant's default download watermark, while `GET /documents/{id}/versions/{versionId}/content` returned the stored bytes unwatermarked - for the current version as well as older ones (the UI's per-version Download button uses it). Inline previews, bulk export (own toggle) and the unsupported / retired-template / over-cap cases correctly returned originals; a view-only user got 403 on the version route and another tenant 404.
- **Root cause.** The default-watermark step lived privately inside `DocumentServiceImpl.serve` (plain download only). `DocumentVersionService.download` opened the stored version and returned it without any watermark decision, so the same file was watermarked or not depending on which URL asked for it.
- **What changed.**
  - NEW `document/DefaultDownloadWatermark` (one component, one policy). It first asks `WatermarkDefaultsService.isDownloadWatermarkApplicable` from the version's recorded details, **before reading a byte**; when the watermark does not apply (not enabled, unsupported type, retired template, over the size cap) the stored stream is returned untouched, so DOC-02 streaming is preserved. Only a file that will really be watermarked is read into memory. It is called with the **requested version's own** number, name, type and size, so `{documentVersion}` names that version, never the document's current one.
  - `DocumentServiceImpl.serve` now calls the component (the private `withDefaultWatermark` is removed); `DocumentVersionService.download` calls it too (`preview` does not). Previews (`inline=true`) and bulk export are unchanged.
  - **Signed files are exempt, byte for byte.** Any change to a digitally signed PDF or Office file breaks its signature, so a file that `EmbeddedSignatureDetector` (the detector the check-out and editing guards use) finds signed is served exactly as stored. The reason is recorded in the existing `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` audit event as `watermarkExemption: SIGNED_CONTENT_EXEMPT` (`audit_events.new_value`), and as an INFO log with ids only (no content). No migration; still exactly one audit event per request. If the detector cannot tell, the watermark engine's own refusal of a signed file remains the safety net (the original is served).
- **Policy confirmed.** `inline=true` unchanged; an available preview rendition for `channel=preview` unchanged; when `channel=preview` has no rendition and falls back to a plain download, the default watermark applies when eligible - now the same on both routes (before, only `/content`).
- **Files changed.**
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DefaultDownloadWatermark.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentServiceImpl.java` (uses the component; `withDefaultWatermark`, one field and three imports removed; the audit `newValue` map gains `watermarkExemption`)
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentVersionService.java` (new field; `download` applies, `preview` does not; audit `newValue` gains `watermarkExemption`)
  - NEW tests: `document/DefaultDownloadWatermarkTest` (8), `watermark/DefaultWatermarkVersionDownloadIT` (12, real PostgreSQL)
  - Existing unit tests adjusted for the constructor change only: `DocumentVersionServiceSignatureGuardTest`, `ReviewCorrectionsAreManualTest`, `ReviewEndpointTenantIsolationTest`
  - Not changed: controllers, authorization, tenant isolation, `ContentDispositionHeader` (DOC-15), preview and bulk-export policies, the watermark engines, schema, the frontend, OCR code. (`DocumentServiceImpl.java`, `DocumentVersionService.java` and `DocumentVersionServiceSignatureGuardTest.java` also carry other developers' uncommitted changes, which is why they do not show up as DOC-16 additions in a `git status` comparison.)
- **Tests executed and results** (private copy `verification\doc16-fix`, byte-identical to the working tree for every DOC-16 file, disposable local database `dms_doc16_fix`; surefire, `./mvnw -B -o`, sequential, fresh JVM per batch):
  - **`DefaultWatermarkVersionDownloadIT` 12/12** (stable over repeated runs; the final run follows a `clean` rebuild): current version and an older version watermarked and still their own content (page counts and text marker); `{documentVersion}` resolves to 1 / 2 / 2 for the older version, the current version by id and the plain download; watermark off: the service returns the storage stream (not a `byte[]`) and a 24 MB download allocates under 6 MB; inline previews unchanged (one `DOCUMENT_VIEWED` each); `channel=preview` without a rendition watermarked on both routes; unsupported type, retired template and a file over the cap served as stored; a signed PDF (real PDFBox signature, signed ranges still matching) and a signed DOCX (structural `_xmlsignatures/` part) served byte-identical on both routes, each audit event carrying `SIGNED_CONTENT_EXEMPT`; exactly one `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` row per request, no exemption on ordinary files; view-only user 403 on the version route and no row written, another tenant 404 on both routes; the DOC-15 Sinhala-name header identical on both routes.
  - **Unit tests 49/49** (6 classes): `DefaultDownloadWatermarkTest` 8, `ContentDispositionHeaderTest` 9, `DownloadHeadersWireTest` 3, `DocumentVersionServiceSignatureGuardTest` 7, `ReviewCorrectionsAreManualTest` 11, `ReviewEndpointTenantIsolationTest` 11.
  - **Mutation check (separate copy; the version-route call changed to skip the watermark):** 6 of the 12 new integration tests failed. This also showed that a restored source file must be recompiled: its old timestamp made Maven reuse the mutated class, which produced a spurious 5 failures + 1 error in the first broad regression run; the final results below were taken after a forced and then a `clean` rebuild.
  - **Regression batches (all after the correct build), 294 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped:** A 34 (`ContentServingPolicyIT` 7, `DocumentDownloadBufferingIT` 13, `DocumentDownloadStreamCleanupIT` 3, `SupportingDocumentContentHeadersIT` 5, `PreviewApiIT` 6); B 84 (`DocumentCheckoutIT` 28, `DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT` 8, `DocumentRenditionIT` 16, `DocumentUploadIT` 9, `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` 23); C 52 (`WopiEditingIT` 39, `WopiSaveMemoryIT` 4, `WopiSaveSpoolIT` 9); D 51 (`Watermark` Disabled 5, DefaultLimits 3, EndpointAuthorization 20, JobIdempotency 7, JobIdentity 4, OfficePipeline 4, Pipeline 8); E 69 (`WatermarkQueueFullIT` 4, `WatermarkServiceIT` 22, `WatermarkTemplateAuthorizationIT` 10, `WatermarkTemplateIT` 13, `WatermarkUnrenderableTextIT` 2, `WatermarkVersionAccessIT` 7, `WatermarkWireValuesIT` 5, `WatermarkWorkerFailureIT` 6); F 4 (`DocumentVersionWorkflowIT` 4). These are 38 distinct classes in the surefire reports; **355 distinct tests passed (294 + 12 + 49) and 1 failed (below)**; the new integration class and the focused set were executed more than once, so the number of executions is higher than 355.
  - **Final focused verification after `mvnw clean`: 48/48** (`DefaultWatermarkVersionDownloadIT` 12, `DefaultDownloadWatermarkTest` 8, `DocumentDownloadBufferingIT` 13, `ContentServingPolicyIT` 7, `DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT` 8), compiled from the working-tree sources (`DocumentVersionService.download` calls the component with watermarking on).
- **Test-harness finding.** A first version of the new integration test retried a request after a `ConcurrentModificationException`. Root cause: with the default executor, `StreamingResponseBody` writes on another thread while the request thread is still finishing the filter chain, and both write headers to the same not-thread-safe `MockHttpServletResponse` (Spring Security's header writer runs on each; the same issue is documented in `WorkflowApiSupport`). The test now streams on the request thread; the retry and the audit-row discounting are gone and the test asserts exactly one event per request. No application defect.
- **Remaining risks / limitations.**
  - **`DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` fails (1 test)** - an independent failure of a stale test, not linked to DOC-16 by any evidence found: it expects OCR status `pending`, but `dms.ocr.enabled` (`OCR_ENABLED`) defaults to false (`DocumentVersionPromoter` marks a version pending only when OCR is enabled); with `OCR_ENABLED=true` it fails on its next assertion, which expects the storage key to end with the original file name, whereas the key is now `documents/<tenant>/<document>/v<n>/<versionId>` (`DocumentVersionPromoter`). Both paths are upload code DOC-16 does not touch. **No direct run without the DOC-16 changes was made**, so "pre-existing" is an inference from the code and the two assertions. Neither the test nor OCR code was changed.
  - `SeaweedFsDocumentUploadIT` executed **0 tests** and is not counted as verified.
  - Not run: the other integration classes of the 116 in the repository (those outside the lists above) and the whole unit suite with PostgreSQL available; verification is of the selected classes only.
  - **Signed files:** a signed file is deliberately served without the watermark (policy, recorded per download); it is read into memory once before the exemption is found, so a large signed file within the size cap is buffered. Detection is the existing structural `EmbeddedSignatureDetector` (PDF `/ByteRange`, Office `_xmlsignatures/`); **no independent cryptographic signature validation was done** (no CMS library in this build; the signed-PDF fixture checks the signed ranges against a digest-only signature, the signed Office fixture is structural). ODF is not supported by the watermark engines. DMS-recorded signatures verify against the stored bytes, which this never changes.
  - `channel=preview` on the version route: the fallback is covered (new test); an **available rendition on the version route is not covered by a test** (`PreviewApiIT` covers `/content?channel=preview` only).
  - Version downloads that are watermarked are buffered in memory (up to the per-format cap) exactly as the plain download already was.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\doc16-verify` and `dms_doc16_verify` (reproduction evidence), `verification\doc16-fix` and `dms_doc16_fix` (verification); nothing dropped.

### DOC-17 – "Protected view" is a preview-channel control and is not applied to ordinary raw `/content` downloads – **CLOSED – BY DESIGN / INFORMATIONAL** (no code change)

- **Original finding:** see §9 (DOC-17, kept as written): "Protected view" not enforced on raw `/content` (Info) - "Documented as design for users with download permission (`PreviewPolicyService`)."
- **Investigation result.** Source-level investigation confirmed that the organisation's preview policy (print, download and copy: allow / watermark / block) is intentionally scoped to the preview channel and is independent of the document's own permissions. A user with view permission on a document can still fetch the original through the ordinary routes whatever the preview policy says. Decision (product owner): **keep the existing behaviour (option A)**; do not enforce the policy at the raw routes (options B and C were not pursued).
- **Evidence** (current working tree, HEAD `dae9154`; line numbers as of this review):
  - `preview/PreviewPolicyService.java` lines 19-33 (class comment; "channel only" at line 22): the policy "applies to the preview channel only and is independent of the document's own permissions"; someone allowed to download "opens the file from the list instead", governed by their document permission; anything but all-allowed makes the preview a protected view drawn on the server.
  - `document/DocumentRenditionService.java`: the policy is enforced inside the protected preview - `pages` (line 308) and `page` (line 322) draw the pages as pictures with the viewer's name burned in; `slide` (line 287) watermarks slide images; `downloadFromPreview` (line 348) returns 403 `PREVIEW_DOWNLOAD_BLOCKED` or a flattened watermarked PDF, or empty for "allow" so the ordinary download runs. The class comment (lines 83-91, "FOLLOW-UP (known gaps, deliberately not fixed here)") records that "the preview policy covers the preview channel only" and that downloading from the file list is governed by the document's own permission.
  - `document/DocumentController.java`: `GET /{id}/content` (line 260, `DOCM_DOCUMENTS_VIEW`) and `GET /{id}/versions/{versionId}/content` (line 461, `DVC_VERSIONS_VIEW`) consult the preview policy only when `channel=preview` (lines 268-271 and 469-472); `inline=true`, `preview.pdf` (303, 442) and thumbnails do not consult it.
  - `preview/PreviewApiIT.theDownloadButtonOfAPreview_followsThePolicy` (line 137): with download set to block, `channel=preview` is 403 while the ordinary `/content` remains 200 ("The preview channel is the only thing restricted: the ordinary download follows the document's permission", line 149).
  - `docs/requirement-registry.md` line 4946, `DPRM-FR-005`: preview security controls apply "independent of the source file's permissions". Consistent with the decision.
  - Frontend `components/documents/document-preview.tsx` (lines 287-347): a restricted policy switches the viewer to the server-drawn pages and never starts the plain preview before the policy is known; the settings text (`components/preview/preview-settings.tsx`) describes the controls as acting "from a preview" / "in previews".
- **Security assessment.**
  - No authorization bypass: every route rechecks document access and tenant; the version route keeps its own permission (`DVC_VERSIONS_VIEW`).
  - No tenant-isolation bypass; no confidentiality gain for anyone who could not already read the document; no integrity or availability effect.
  - Authorized ordinary downloads stay available, and the tenant's default download watermark still applies to them (DOC-16).
  - **A preview restriction is a viewing-experience control, not DRM and not a guarantee against copying, printing or downloading by a user who is authorized to open the document through another route.** A user who can see every page of a document in the preview can already reproduce it; print and copy blocks are deterrents inside the preview, while the server-drawn pages, the watermark and the preview Download rule are the parts the server enforces for the preview channel.
  - Severity: Informational (Low at most, if an organisation expects hard enforcement - not the product's design).
- **Resolution.** No application-code change required; existing behaviour retained by product decision. No test or code was changed in this review.
- **Documentation review.** No document claims the preview policy prevents all copying, printing or downloading. Observations (not changed): (1) `docs/watermarking.md` line 349 lists "blocking un-watermarked downloads" and auto-watermark on download as out of scope for Phase 1, which predates the tenant default download watermark (DOC-16 context) - stale, unrelated to preview policy; (2) the preview-settings page could add one sentence that downloads from the file list follow document permissions and the default download watermark, to avoid being read as a blanket block - a suggested wording follow-up for the product owner, not done here.
- **Verification limitations.** Source-level investigation only; no new runtime reproduction; no new tests executed - existing coverage (`PreviewApiIT`, `ContentServingPolicyIT`, `DocumentRenditionIT`, `document-preview.policy.test.tsx`) was inspected but not rerun. There is no test that pins the raw `inline=true` and `preview.pdf` routes while a restrictive policy is set; if a future change touches these routes, such a test would be the guard.

### DOC-18 – A storage stream that is opened for a download or export is closed when the download or export does not complete – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED**

- **Original finding:** see §9 (DOC-18, kept as written): "Stream not closed if response never written" (Low) - `DocumentController.downloadDocument`, `ExportArchiveBuilder.addFile`. After DOC-02 the controller's streaming body closes the stream whenever it runs (complete, storage failure, client disconnect); the investigation looked for the remaining windows in which the stream is opened but never reaches that body or the ZIP writer.
- **Root cause (reproduced DB-free before the fix; see `verification\doc18-verify`).**
  - **ZIP export:** `ExportArchiveBuilder.addFile` called `zip.putNextEntry(...)` *before* the try-with-resources that owns the downloaded stream, so a failure creating the entry (a write error such as a full disk; an entry path over ZIP's 65,535-byte limit) left the storage stream open.
  - **Audit failure:** `DocumentServiceImpl.serve` and `DocumentVersionService.open` open the stream first and write the `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` / `DOCUMENT_VIEWED` audit event afterwards; an exception from the audit write escaped with the stream still open (normal download, version download, and both previews).
  - **Transaction completion:** both methods are `@Transactional`, so the commit runs in Spring's proxy *after* the method has returned. A commit (or flush) failure surfaces there - the caller gets an exception, never the stream, and no `catch` inside the method can see it.
- **What changed.**
  - `importexport/export/ExportArchiveBuilder.addFile`: the stream is opened in the try-with-resources and `putNextEntry` runs inside it; `closeEntry` stays in an inner `finally`, so it runs only for an entry that really started. Archive contents, names and exception propagation are unchanged.
  - NEW `document/OpenStreamGuard` (package-private; the ownership rules are in its class comment): `closeQuietly` (never throws, logs only the exception class) and `closeUnlessCommitted`, which, when the thread has an actual synchronised transaction, registers an `afterCompletion` callback that closes the stream **unless the real completion status is COMMITTED** (so rollback and "unknown" both close it). With no transaction nothing is registered.
  - `DocumentServiceImpl.serve` and `DocumentVersionService.open`: everything after `versionContent.open` is in a `try`; a `RuntimeException`/`Error` closes the stream and rethrows; a normal return goes through `closeUnlessCommitted`. On a commit the stream stays open and the controller's body (DOC-02) owns it. Transaction propagation and isolation, audit-event content, the DOC-16 watermark call (it still receives the same stream), previews, authorization and tenant isolation are unchanged.
  - Callers reviewed: the controller (no transaction), `DocumentTemplateService.copy` (`NOT_SUPPORTED`; drains the stream itself) and `ExportJobWorker.run` (no outer transaction; each download has its own, committed before the stream is read). No realistic call path has the service join a surrounding transaction; were one to, the callback would only close an unconsumed stream when that transaction rolls back, and closing an already-consumed stream is harmless (reasoned from the JDK stream contract; not separately tested).
- **Files changed.**
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/OpenStreamGuard.java`
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/importexport/export/ExportArchiveBuilder.java` (+11/-7)
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentServiceImpl.java` (`serve`; +28/-22 including re-indentation)
  - `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/document/DocumentVersionService.java` (`open`; +25/-19 including re-indentation)
  - NEW tests: `importexport/export/ExportArchiveStreamCleanupTest` (4), `document/DownloadStreamOwnershipTest` (9), `watermark/DownloadStreamTransactionIT` (5, real PostgreSQL)
  - (`DocumentServiceImpl.java` and `DocumentVersionService.java` also carry other developers' uncommitted changes, so they do not appear as new files in `git status`.)
- **Tests executed and results** (private copy `verification\doc18-fix`, source and tests byte-identical to the working tree; disposable local database `dms_doc18_fix`; `./mvnw -B -o test -Dtest=<classes>`, sequential, fresh JVM per batch):
  - **18 new tests, all passing:** `ExportArchiveStreamCleanupTest` 4/4 (write error at `putNextEntry`, over-long entry name, copy that fails mid-stream, and a normal export that closes the stream with the right content); `DownloadStreamOwnershipTest` 9/9 (for the normal and the version download, and both previews: audit write fails after open; commit fails after return through Spring's real `@Transactional` interceptor over a stub manager that runs after-completion callbacks; committed transaction leaves the stream open and readable; no-transaction control); `DownloadStreamTransactionIT` 5/5 on **real PostgreSQL with Spring's real transaction manager** (committed: an open, readable stream and exactly one audit event per download; surrounding transaction rolled back after the service returned: stream closed and no audit event; surrounding transaction committed: stream open; audit write fails: stream closed on both services; **a real database commit failure** - a version row without a scan result trips the deferred scan-gate trigger (V39) at commit, the root cause is a `SQLException`, the stream is closed on both services, and no audit event or stray version remains).
  - **Mutation check** (separate copy; the three production files reverted to their pre-DOC-18 text): 11 of the 18 new tests failed (6 of 9, 2 of 4, 3 of 5 - every audit-failure, commit-failure, rollback and export-fault case) while the success-path tests passed; the copy was then restored to the working-tree text (hashes equal) and recompiled before the regression runs.
  - **Regression, 294 test executions in four sequential batches (290 passed, 4 errors, explained below):**
    - A, 54/54: the 18 new tests plus `DocumentDownloadStreamCleanupIT` 3, `DocumentDownloadBufferingIT` 13, `DefaultWatermarkVersionDownloadIT` 12, `DefaultDownloadWatermarkTest` 8.
    - B, 60 run, 1 error: `ExportArchiveBuilderTest` 7, `BulkExportServiceTest` 4, `ExportJobServiceTest` 12, `DocumentVersionServiceSignatureGuardTest` 7, `ContentDispositionHeaderTest` 9, `DownloadHeadersWireTest` 3, `SupportingDocumentContentHeadersIT` 5, `PreviewApiIT` 6, `ContentServingPolicyIT` 7 (the error).
    - C, 136/136: `DocumentCheckoutIT` 28, `DocumentVersionSignatureGuardIT` 8, `DocumentRenditionIT` 16, `DocumentUploadIT` 9, `DocumentVersionPromotionGuardIT` 23, `WopiEditingIT` 39, `WopiSaveMemoryIT` 4, `WopiSaveSpoolIT` 9.
    - D, 44 run, 3 errors: `AccessDeniedAuditIT` 2, `SystemActorAuditIT` 2, `MetadataAuditIT` 5, `OwnershipTransferIT` 13, `VersionPromotionRefusalIT` 10, `WorkflowSupportingDocumentIT` 8, `PermissionCodeAuditIT` 4 (the errors).
  - **The four errors.** (1) `ContentServingPolicyIT.jsonAndPlainText_requestedInline_areAttachments_withTheSandbox`: a `ConcurrentModificationException` inside `MockHttpServletResponse` (the previously observed MockMvc response-header race between the streaming thread and the request thread, the same one documented in `WorkflowApiSupport` and met in DOC-16); the class was rerun twice and passed 7/7 both times; no production code or test was changed for it. (2) `PermissionCodeAuditIT` (3 errors): `KeycloakIntegrationException: Failed to create realm role` caused by Keycloak `HTTP 401 Unauthorized` during test setup (`createTestRole`); no DOC-18 source path is implicated, but **no clean baseline run without the DOC-18 changes was made, so its independence is inferred from the failure cause, not demonstrated**. Neither is classed as a confirmed pre-existing defect.
- **Remaining risks / limitations.**
  - The controller's streaming body never being scheduled or run (executor rejection, shutdown) would still leave the stream open; no custom executor or timeout exists and this could not be reproduced - theoretical and unverified. The controller's response construction was exercised with unusual content types and names and does not throw.
  - Other unusual transaction outcomes (a dropped connection at commit, an unknown outcome) are not individually reproduced; the callback closes on any status other than COMMITTED, but only the rollback and constraint-failure-at-commit cases were tested.
  - The full integration suite was not run; verification is of the classes listed above. `DocumentUploadOcrQueueingIT` and the other DOC-16 limitations are unchanged.
  - A signed file or a watermarked download is still buffered as before (DOC-16); this change does not alter memory behaviour.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\doc18-verify` (reproduction, with logs), `verification\doc18-fix` and the database `dms_doc18_fix` (verification, not dropped), and all earlier verification resources.

### DOC-19 – `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` records an authorised download that the server started, not a completed delivery – **CLOSED – BY DESIGN / INFORMATIONAL** (no code change)

- **Original finding:** see §9 (DOC-19, kept as written): "Download audited at open, not completion (Info)" - "`DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` logged before bytes stream." The DOC-02 entry's limitations already note it ("Audit is still logged when the download opens, not when it completes (DOC-19)").
- **Investigation result.** The behaviour is intentional. The event records an authorised download operation that the server initiated: it is written, and committed, before the first byte is sent, and it does not say that the transfer finished or that the client received or stored anything. Decision (product owner): **keep audit-at-open (option A)**; do not add completion or abort events (option B was not pursued).
- **Supporting evidence** (current working tree, HEAD `dae9154`; line numbers as of this review; source-level only):
  - `document/DocumentServiceImpl.serve` (line 508): opens the stream, then `event.log()` (545) writes the audit row, then returns through `OpenStreamGuard.closeUnlessCommitted` (546). `downloadDocument` (469) and `previewDocument` (481) call it. The comment at line ~463 says the row "joins this transaction and is only written when it commits".
  - `document/DocumentVersionService.open` (119): the same order - `.log()` (144), then `closeUnlessCommitted` (145).
  - `audit/service/AuditService.persist` (line 80, `@Transactional`): writes the hash-chained row in the caller's transaction; the table is append-only (`V23`; `ABDR-FR-004`), so a completion record could only be a second event.
  - **Order of events:** the service methods are `@Transactional`, so the audit row is committed when the method returns - before `document/DocumentController.downloadDocument` (260) / `downloadVersion` (461) receive the stream and build the `StreamingResponseBody` (272-276) that sends the bytes on another thread. If the transfer later fails or the client disconnects, the committed event stays. If the audit write or its commit fails, no stream is returned and (DOC-18) the opened stream is closed, so the audit fails closed: bytes cannot go out without an event.
  - `audit/catalog/AuditActionCatalog` lines 104 and 454: `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` is a routine LOW-severity event; the catalog has no download completion or abort action, and nothing defines the event as proof of delivery.
  - Related events use the same semantics (not changed): the guest download (`sharing/PortalService`, ~214-218) and the share-link download (`sharing/PublicShareService`, ~203-207) are logged when the file is served, before the body runs; the OCR internal read audits "first read" at open (`OcrInternalContentController:159`); the watermarked preview download (`DocumentRenditionService:372`) logs after the PDF is built and before it is returned; each file in a bulk export logs `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` with `via: bulk_export` when it is read into the ZIP (the export has its own `EXPORT_COMPLETED`).
  - **Requirements (`docs/requirement-registry.md`):** `ABDR-FR-002` (line 6302) asks for actor, action, affected object, timestamp, source module and outcome for every event; `DVC-FR-015` (line 3913) asks for a version-specific audit record for every version action "including user, timestamp, and action performed"; `SEA-FR-006` (line 1640) asks for share download events to be logged. **None requires a completed-transfer record, bytes delivered or client receipt**; that reading is mine, not stated in the registry.
  - `dashboard/DashboardService`: counts the events directly - `USAGE_ACTIONS` (92-93) and the "most opened" query (~296-309, `count(*) filter (where a.action = 'DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED')`); `ACTIVITY_ACTIONS` (69-71) feeds the activity list. The web labels are "Downloads" (`frontend/components/dashboard/activity-chart.tsx:24`) and "downloaded" (`frontend/components/dashboard/activity.ts:35`).
- **What the event does and does not mean.**
  - *Authorised download initiation* - what the event records (access was checked, the file was found and opened, the audit committed).
  - *Partial streaming* - possible after the event (client aborts, network failure); the event remains and is not annotated.
  - *Successful server-side streaming* - not recorded; even if it were, it would not prove the client stored every byte.
  - *Confirmed client receipt* - cannot be established by the server and is not claimed.
- **Security assessment.** No authorization bypass; no tenant-isolation effect; no additional confidentiality exposure (an aborted transfer still leaves an event, which is the safer direction for an access trail); audit events remain append-only; audit persistence precedes streaming. **Interrupted transfers still count as downloads in the dashboard's download counts and activity list.** Severity stays Informational.
- **Resolution.** No application-code change required. The existing audit-at-open semantics are retained by design decision. Suggested wording (not applied; for the product owner): describe the dashboard's "Downloads" as "download requests started" - for example a tooltip "Each authorised download that was started, including any the browser did not finish" - and describe `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` in the audit catalog comment as "download authorised and started".
- **Verification limitations.** Source-level verification only; no runtime test was executed for DOC-19; existing tests were inspected, not rerun (they assert one `DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED` per successful request: `DocumentRenditionIT:292`, `DocumentUploadIT:129`, `DefaultWatermarkVersionDownloadIT`, `DownloadStreamTransactionIT`); no test aborts a transfer and checks the trail; the event gives no proof of client receipt; no completion or abort event was implemented.

### DOC-20 – Watermark output keys no longer contain user-supplied file names, and stored keys are validated before they are deleted or read – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only)

- **Original finding:** see §9 (DOC-20, kept as written): "Zip-slip / path traversal / storage keys – PASS (static)" - "UUID-built keys independent of filename; `FileSystemStorageAdapter.resolveAndValidate` normalises+`startsWith`; export names via `safeName`." **That static PASS was incomplete.** Document, staging, quarantine, rendition, import and export keys are built from ids, as stated, but the watermark job's output key was not.
- **What was wrong.** `WatermarkJobWorker.store` built the output key as `<tenantId>/tmp/watermarks/<jobId>/<source.filename()>`, where the name is the version's `original_filename` - stored exactly as the client sent it (a plain `VARCHAR(500)`; upload passes `MultipartFile.getOriginalFilename()` through and only the extension is checked). A name such as `../../x.pdf` therefore put parent segments into the key. The filesystem adapter only refuses a key that leaves the location's **base** path, so a key that leaves the job's directory but stays inside the base was accepted. The key is kept on the job row and was then used to **write** the output, to **delete** it (`WatermarkOutputSweeper` and the worker's cleanup) and to **read** it back (`WatermarkJobService.download`).
- **Reproduced** (deterministic, DB-free, a disposable temp directory, the real `store` and the real filesystem adapter; `verification\doc20-verify`, log `logs\d20_repro.log`): a control name stayed in the job directory; `../../x.pdf` wrote to `<tenant>/tmp/x.pdf`; a name with four parent segments followed by another tenant's path **overwrote that tenant's output object**, and the delete by the stored key (what the sweeper does) **removed it**; twelve parent segments were refused by the adapter (beyond the base). Names with a control character or 300 characters only failed the user's own job. On real PostgreSQL and storage, the original sweeper deleted a sibling job's object (seen in the mutation run below). The filename must still end in an allowed extension, so only objects whose names do (other watermark outputs, export ZIPs) were reachable; UUID-named document objects were not. Not run: a real HTTP upload of a `../` name (the tests start from a stored name containing `../`).
- **What changed.**
  - NEW `watermark/job/WatermarkOutputKeys` (public): `keyFor(tenantId, jobId)` = `<tenantId>/tmp/watermarks/<jobId>/output` (two ids and a fixed word); `isInsideJobNamespace(tenantId, jobId, key)` accepts a stored key only if it starts with that job's own prefix and nothing after it is empty, `.`, `..` (split on both `/` and `\`, so the verdict does not depend on the host's path rules) or a control character; `describe(key)` gives length and a short hash for logging, never the key.
  - `WatermarkJobWorker.store`: uses `keyFor` - the original file name never reaches the key. The name the person downloads still comes from the version (`WatermarkJobService.filenameFor`).
  - `WatermarkJobWorker.dropPartialOutput` and `WatermarkOutputSweeper.deleteObject`/`purge`: an unsafe stored key is **never passed to the storage adapter**; a warning logs the job, tenant, key length and hash; the key is **kept on the job row** for review, and the job is still marked expired and purged (so it is not offered and not swept again).
  - `WatermarkJobService.download`: after the status, expiry and access checks and before any storage read, an unsafe key is refused with the existing `410 WATERMARK_EXPIRED` answer ("no longer available"), nothing about the key is sent to the client, and no download audit event is written for it. Valid keys - new `/output` keys and legitimate legacy keys, nested ones included - behave as before (filename, MIME type, size, headers, audit unchanged).
  - Unchanged: upload filename persistence, the storage adapters, authorization and tenant checks, job state transitions, cleanup of legitimate keys. No migration: legacy keys stay in `output_object_key` and age out with the 24-hour output TTL.
- **Files changed.**
  - NEW `backend/dms-core/src/main/java/com/oak/dms/watermark/job/WatermarkOutputKeys.java`
  - `.../watermark/job/WatermarkJobWorker.java` (`store`, `fail`, `dropPartialOutput`), `.../watermark/job/WatermarkOutputSweeper.java` (`purge`, `deleteObject`), `.../watermark/service/WatermarkJobService.java` (`download`, one import)
  - NEW tests: `watermark/job/WatermarkOutputKeysTest` (7), `watermark/job/WatermarkOutputStorageTest` (8), `watermark/service/WatermarkJobServiceDownloadGuardTest` (3), `watermark/WatermarkOutputKeyIT` (7, real PostgreSQL)
  - (`WatermarkDefaultsService.java` and other files in that package carry other developers' uncommitted changes; they were not touched.)
- **Tests executed and results.**
  - **New tests (25):** `WatermarkOutputKeysTest` 7/7 (acceptance of new and legacy keys; refusal of `../`, `..\`, `./`, mixed separators, empty segments, control characters, another job or tenant, absolute, UNC and drive paths, a prefix-matching key containing `../`); `WatermarkOutputStorageTest` 8/8 (the real `store`, `dropPartialOutput`, `deleteObject` and `purge` over the real filesystem adapter in a temp directory: nine file names including `../`, `..\`, cross-tenant traversal, BEL, 300 characters and Sinhala all give the same job-owned key with a readable output and untouched neighbours; new and legacy keys are deleted; unsafe keys are refused with `delete` never called and kept on the row; missing key and failing delete behave as before); `WatermarkJobServiceDownloadGuardTest` 3/3 (new, legacy and nested keys are read with the right bytes, type, name and size; fifteen unsafe keys are refused with 410 without opening storage and without an audit event); `WatermarkOutputKeyIT` 7/7 on **real PostgreSQL and real storage** (a `../../x.pdf` document completes with the id-only key and downloads as `application/pdf` with a `x-watermarked.pdf` name and the right size, nothing written outside the job directory; an expired output is swept and its key cleared; a legitimate legacy key is still swept and still downloads; a hostile legacy key leaves a sibling job's object untouched, stays on the row and is not offered; unsafe keys pointing at a sibling job's or another tenant's object cannot be read and are answered 410 with no key or content in the body and no download audit).
  - **Mutation (separate copy):** key construction reverted - 2 of 15 unit tests failed; original worker and sweeper restored - 7 of 15 failed; the same on PostgreSQL - 2 of 4 integration tests failed (the sibling job's object was deleted); download guard removed - 3 of 10 failed (1 unit, 2 integration: the sibling and cross-tenant reads succeeded). Each was restored and verified by hash.
  - **Regression in the isolated copy** (`verification\doc20-fix`, database `dms_doc20_fix`, `./mvnw -B -o`, sequential): 145 tests in 25 classes after the key and cleanup fix (the watermark job, service, template, authorization, version-access, queue, wire-value, default-limit, disabled, Office and unrenderable-text ITs, plus `FileSystemStorageAdapterTest`), and 99 after the download guard (`WatermarkPipelineIT`, `WatermarkServiceIT`, `WatermarkEndpointAuthorizationIT`, `WatermarkVersionAccessIT`, `WatermarkOfficePipelineIT`, `DownloadHeadersWireTest`, `BulkExportServiceTest`, `FileSystemStorageAdapterTest` and the new tests): 0 failures, errors or skips.
  - **After applying to the development repository** (`./mvnw -B -o` in `backend\dms-core`, sequential, fresh JVM per batch; database `dms_doc20_live`, a new disposable local database; the live sources were byte-identical to the verified isolated files): 82 of 82 passed - `WatermarkOutputKeysTest` 7, `WatermarkOutputStorageTest` 8, `WatermarkJobServiceDownloadGuardTest` 3, `WatermarkOutputKeyIT` 7, `WatermarkPipelineIT` 8, `WatermarkVersionAccessIT` 7, `WatermarkEndpointAuthorizationIT` 20 (batch 1, 60) and `WatermarkServiceIT` 22 (batch 2).
  - **Platform check:** the validator was compiled once and run on Windows and in an ephemeral local Linux container (no network, read-only mounts): all 24 verdicts are identical; where the host's own path rules differ (a backslash is a literal character on Linux) the validator is stricter, not looser.
- **Remaining risks / limitations.**
  - S3-compatible and Azure adapters pass keys literally with no validation; whether the SDKs or a SeaweedFS S3 gateway normalise `..` was **not verified**. The fix removes user text from new keys and guards stored ones, so it does not depend on that.
  - Upload filenames are still stored unsanitised in `original_filename`; other consumers of the name are covered separately (DOC-15 headers, export `safeName`).
  - A legacy output whose name contains a control character or a backslash-dot segment is refused by cleanup and is left in staging (its key stays on the row); such files are rare and the outputs live 24 hours.
  - Database-backed and filesystem tests ran on Windows; Linux was exercised for the validator only. The full application test suite was not run. Nothing was deployed or verified in production, and no remote storage was touched.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\doc20-verify` (reproduction and log), `verification\doc20-fix` (isolated fix, manifest, logs), databases `dms_doc20_fix` and `dms_doc20_live` (not dropped), and all earlier verification resources.

### FE-H1 – Frontend security headers added with CSP Report-Only – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; deployment verification pending)

- **Original finding:** see §10 (FE-H1, kept as written, original severity **High**): "No security headers in Next.js - `frontend/next.config.ts` has no `headers()`: no CSP, `X-Frame-Options`/`frame-ancestors`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, HSTS." The investigation confirmed it from source (Next.js and the repository's Nginx add none) and, on the existing local frontend container (`localhost:3000`), at runtime: `/login`, redirects, `/s/...` and `/monitoring/panels/...` carried none of them. The effective headers of the deployed production server were never observed and remain unknown. The investigation's recommended severity for the original gap, given the evidence (SameSite=Lax cookies, no XSS sinks in the app code, LAN-only HTTP), was Medium (defence in depth); the original rating above is kept for traceability.
- **What changed (Next.js only).**
  - NEW `frontend/config/security-headers.ts` and `frontend/next.config.ts` (`async headers()` returning `securityHeaderRules()`; +8 lines).
  - Enforced on every response Next.js produces (pages, redirects, 404s, static files, route handlers): `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` (not DENY - the monitoring page frames its own `/monitoring/panels/` route), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, and a `Permissions-Policy` that turns off camera, microphone, geolocation, payment, USB, display-capture and the motion sensors while leaving clipboard and fullscreen alone (the Copy buttons and the online editor frame need them). Share links (`/s/...`) get `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, since the address is the secret.
  - **`Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`** on pages (not on `_next/` assets and not on `/monitoring/panels/`, which forwards Grafana's own pages): `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' blob: data:; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'; media-src 'self' blob:; frame-src 'self' blob:; form-action 'self'; base-uri 'self'; object-src 'none'`. Every directive is taken from what the app does; no external origin, no wildcard, no `unsafe-eval` in production (development adds it only), no nonce yet (so `'unsafe-inline'` for scripts remains). Extra origins (the online editor's address, for `frame-src` and `form-action`) and a non-same-origin API base can be added only through the build-time `DMS_CSP_EXTRA_ORIGINS` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL`, parsed to plain http(s) origins. It is **report-only**: it never blocks; `frame-ancestors` is deliberately absent (ignored in report-only), so framing protection comes from `X-Frame-Options`. There is no report endpoint; violations appear in the browser console only.
  - Not set, on purpose: `Strict-Transport-Security` (the documented deployment is plain HTTP on the LAN, where browsers ignore HSTS; add it only after nginx serves HTTPS) and the Cross-Origin isolation headers (COEP would break the cross-origin editor frame).
  - Not changed: `frontend/proxy.ts` (another developer's external-sharing edit is preserved), the live Nginx configuration, dms-core's and Keycloak's own headers (those responses do not pass through Next.js), any other file.
- **Pre-application review.** The only Next.js route handler is `app/monitoring/panels/[...path]/route.ts`; it copies Grafana's response headers, deliberately removes Grafana's `X-Frame-Options`, and sets no stricter policy of its own, so the global values weaken nothing. The email-preview frame carries its own `<meta>` CSP (`default-src 'none'`), which is independent of HTTP headers. There are no Next.js download, preview or share route handlers. Header precedence was tested with a temporary probe route (isolated copy, removed): a handler that sets `X-Frame-Options: DENY` or `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` is **overridden** by the configured values (no duplicates), while a different header such as an enforced `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox` is preserved. This only affects responses that pass through Next.js.
- **Files changed (live development repository).** NEW `frontend/config/security-headers.ts`; NEW `frontend/config/security-headers.test.ts`; `frontend/next.config.ts`. Applied from the verified isolated copy (`verification\feh1-fix`; identical hashes); generated files (`next-env.d.ts`, `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo`, `.next`) are git-ignored and were not copied.
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Unit tests: 19 of 19 pass** (`config/security-headers.test.ts`: exact values of the four enforced headers; SAMEORIGIN not DENY; no HSTS or Cross-Origin headers; clipboard and fullscreen not restricted; the policy is report-only with no `frame-ancestors`, no wildcard or external origin, `unsafe-eval` only in development; extra-origin parsing drops `*`, `javascript:`, credentials and non-http schemes; the rules cover all paths, exclude `_next/` and the monitoring panels from the CSP, and give share links `no-referrer`). Run in the isolated copy and again in the live tree.
  - **`tsc --noEmit`: exit 0, no output. ESLint (`bun run lint`): exit 0, 0 errors and 3 warnings** (`react-hooks/incompatible-library` in `nav-progress.tsx`, `sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx`, `sign-document-dialog.tsx`; existing, not FE-H1). **`bun run build` (Next.js 16.4.3, Turbopack): exit 0**, 65 static pages; the routes manifest records the three header rules. All three were run in the isolated copy and in the live tree.
  - **Runtime, built app on a private localhost port** (isolated build and again the live-source build; plain GET, `curl`): `/login` 200, `/` and a protected route without a session 307 to `/login`, `/s/probe-token` 200, `/portal` 200, a protected route with a session cookie 200, an unknown route with a session cookie **404**, `/icon.png` 200 and `/monitoring/panels/...` 403 (dms-core unreachable; the handler's own response) all carried `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` and the `Permissions-Policy`; the CSP report-only header was present on every page response and absent on `_next/` assets and the monitoring route as designed; `/s/...` returned `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; no header was duplicated on any route; GET and HEAD matched on `/login`.
  - **Browser (isolated build, one Chrome tab, since closed):** the login page rendered and the share route rendered; the report-only policy is live and non-blocking (two deliberate controls - an image and a fetch to an external origin - were reported with disposition `report`); a `blob:` image, `data:` image, `blob:` audio, a sandboxed `srcdoc` iframe with inline style (the email-preview pattern) and a `blob:` PDF iframe produced no violation.
- **Remaining limitations.**
  - **Nginx:** the proposal (`X-Content-Type-Options` with `always` at the server level and a documented HSTS prerequisite) was prepared in the isolated copy only and is **not applied**; its syntax is **unverified** (no nginx binary or image is available locally, and nothing was installed).
  - **Authenticated browser compatibility was not tested:** document previews and downloads, the monitoring iframe with a live Grafana, the email preview inside the app, the external-sharing flow and Collabora editing all need dms-core, Keycloak and the nginx `/api` route that the isolated app lacks. Violations during a page's initial load could not be captured. Whether Chrome's PDF viewer renders inside the blob iframe was not confirmed visually.
  - The CSP remains **report-only** with `'unsafe-inline'` scripts until a nonce is added and the policy is enforced after a clean reporting period; HSTS is deferred until the deployment serves HTTPS (INF-01).
  - **Production deployment and the production server's effective headers were not verified**; Keycloak's own headers were not read. Configured headers override same-named headers set by Next route handlers.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\feh1-fix` (isolated implementation, manifest, logs) and all earlier verification resources.

### FE-M1 / FUN-02 – Safe post-login deep-link redirect implemented – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; production verification pending)

- **Original findings:** see §10 (FE-M1, "Login discards `?from=` deep link", Medium) and §5 (FUN-02, "Login ignores `?from=` redirect", Medium - "see FE-M1"), both kept as written with their original severity. **They are the same defect**, so there is a single remediation entry. Behaviour confirmed from source: `proxy.ts` sends an unauthenticated visitor of a protected page to `/login?from=<path>`, but `app/login/login-page-content.tsx` never read `from` and always did `router.push("/")` after a successful sign-in.
- **Root cause.** The `from` parameter was produced and never consumed. (Fixing it naively would have created an open redirect: `from` is attacker-controllable through a crafted link, so the value must be validated before it is used.)
- **What changed.**
  - NEW `frontend/lib/auth/safe-return-path.ts`: `safeReturnPath(value)` returns the value only if it is an ordinary path of this application, otherwise `/`; it never throws and is idempotent. Rules: a non-empty string of at most 2048 characters with no leading or trailing whitespace; one leading `/` and no `//`; no backslash or control character (tab, newline and NUL included) as written or after one decode; decoded exactly once - malformed percent-encoding, an encoded backslash or control character, an encoded `//` at the start and any second layer of encoding (a remaining `%XX`) are refused; parsed with `new URL` against a placeholder origin, which must be unchanged, with no credentials and no `//` path; the normalised path (dot segments resolved) and its decoded form must not be `/login`, `/session-expired`, `/login-timeout`, `/reset-password`, `/update-password` or `/error`, nor below them (case-insensitive); the canonical `pathname + search + hash` is returned. Legitimate paths such as `/loginfo`, `/errors` or `/documents/login` are not rejected.
  - `frontend/app/login/login-page-content.tsx`: `const returnPath = safeReturnPath(searchParams.get("from"))` and `router.push(returnPath)` instead of `router.push("/")`. The existing `queryClient.clear()`, the success toast and `router.refresh()` are unchanged and keep their order. The one-time-code step is component state, not a navigation, so `from` is still in the URL when sign-in completes and nothing navigates before the code is verified.
  - NEW tests: `lib/auth/safe-return-path.test.ts` (81) and `app/login/login-page-content.test.tsx` (22).
  - Not changed: `frontend/proxy.ts` (another developer's external-sharing edit is preserved), the FE-H1 headers, the authentication backend, Keycloak, any other file.
- **Files changed (live development repository).** The four files above, applied from the verified isolated copy (`verification\fem1-fix`) with identical hashes: `safe-return-path.ts` 30CC2F3E96D0, `safe-return-path.test.ts` ECA37A53E10F, `login-page-content.tsx` 523589B73C42, `login-page-content.test.tsx` 1C99F1C40AD7.
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Unit and component tests: 103 of 103 pass in the live tree** (81 validator tests: valid paths unchanged, ~35 unsafe values to `/`, sign-in pages and their normalised or encoded variants to `/`, invariants; 22 login tests: no `from`, five valid destinations, eleven unsafe or sign-in `from` values, MFA keeping the return path, no navigation before the code is verified, an unsafe `from` still ignored after the code step, a failed login not navigating or clearing the cache, and the clear, toast, push, refresh order). Mutation checks in the isolated copy: reverting the component to `router.push("/")` failed 6 of 22 login tests; a naive `startsWith("/")` validator failed 59 of 103.
  - **Live tree: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 with no output (20 s); ESLint `bun run lint` exit 0 with 0 errors and the same 3 existing warnings** (`nav-progress.tsx`, `sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx`, `sign-document-dialog.tsx`); **`bun run build` (Next.js 16.4.3) exit 0**, 65 static pages. All three also passed in the isolated copy. All commands ran under a 750 MB free-memory abort guard that never intervened (lowest free memory 1,450 MB during the live build).
  - **STUBBED RUNTIME VERIFICATION** (live-source build on a private localhost port, with a small local stand-in for the two login calls and made-up accounts - **not Keycloak and not dms-core**; the browser-side API base was baked to the stub's own origin, and every API call in the run went to it, none to the real dms-core): an unauthenticated `/documents`, `/documents/123` and `/tasks` returned 307 to `/login?from=<encoded path>`; opening `/documents`, signing in and landing on `/documents` worked (first navigation and final path `/documents`); 18 return-path cases run in the browser all ended on the expected path on the same origin with no loop - no `from` to `/`; a query string and fragment, an encoded path (`%20`), `/documents/123`, `/s/example-token` and `/portal` unchanged; `https://evil.example`, `//evil.example`, `/\evil.example`, `/%2F%2Fevil.example`, `/%252F%252Fevil.example`, malformed encoding, `javascript:alert(1)`, `/login`, `/session-expired`, `/reset-password`, `/documents/../login` and `/%6Cogin` all to `/`; MFA showed the code step with no navigation before the code, then reached the destination with the toast "Signed in successfully."; a wrong code and a wrong password did not navigate and showed "Invalid username or password."; `/s/...` and `/portal` stayed public (200); the FE-H1 headers were present on the login page, redirects, `/s/...`, `/portal` and a 404, with `no-referrer` on share links.
- **Remaining limitations.**
  - **Real Keycloak and dms-core authentication, and real MFA, remain unverified**; the runtime evidence above is from a stub, and the 103 component and unit tests simulate the login calls.
