  - **Deferred, out of scope:** preserving the deep link's query string in `proxy.ts` (it sets `from` to the pathname only, so `/documents?folder=abc` returns to `/documents`), and return paths after session expiry, logout or suspension (`/session-expired`, `/login-timeout` and the suspended-organisation redirect still go to `/login` without `from`).
  - Production deployment was not verified; no commit, push or deployment was made.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\fem1-fix` (isolated implementation, manifest, logs) and all earlier verification resources.

### FE-M2 – Monitoring proxy credential and identity-header forwarding restricted – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; production verification pending)

- **Original finding:** see §10 (FE-M2, "Monitoring proxy forwards user cookies/Authorization to Grafana", Medium), kept as written with its original **Medium** severity.
- **Root cause.** `app/monitoring/panels/[...path]/route.ts` built the request it sent to Grafana from a **deny-list** of hop-by-hop headers, so everything else the browser or the front proxy attached was forwarded: both DMS sign-in cookies (`dms_access_token`, `dms_refresh_token`, `Path=/`), the client's `Authorization`, `X-Forwarded-*` and any custom header. **Confirmed worse than the finding's wording, against a disposable Grafana 11.5.1 with the auth proxy on:** Grafana tries an `Authorization` header before the trusted `X-WEBAUTH-USER`, so a caller who sent Basic credentials was not the person dms-core had vouched for. The same caller was `monitor.user` (not admin) without the header and the Grafana admin (`isGrafanaAdmin=true`) with it. A second gap, found in review: `Set-Cookie` from Grafana was passed back to the browser unchanged, and because the route lives in the DMS origin a `Set-Cookie: dms_access_token=...; Path=/` could have replaced the caller's DMS sign-in.
- **What changed.**
  - NEW `frontend/lib/monitoring/panel-proxy-headers.ts`: `buildUpstreamHeaders(incoming, user)` is a strict **allow-list of 13 ordinary request headers** - accept, accept-encoding, accept-language, cache-control, content-type, if-modified-since, if-none-match, if-range, origin, pragma, range, referer, user-agent - matched case-insensitively, with no prefix rules. **No cookie of any kind** is forwarded (Grafana set none in auth-proxy mode and needs none), and no `Authorization`, `Proxy-Authorization`, `X-Forwarded-*`, `Forwarded`, `X-Real-IP`, identity header or unknown header. `X-WEBAUTH-USER` is set from dms-core's `X-Dms-User` answer and from nothing else; the helper throws if there is no user. The Grafana frontend's informational `X-Grafana-*`, `X-Datasource-Uid`, `X-Plugin-Id`, `X-Panel-Id`, `X-Dashboard-Uid` headers are deliberately not forwarded (the panel and its queries work without them).
  - `frontend/app/monitoring/panels/[...path]/route.ts`: uses the helper in place of the deny-list, and adds `set-cookie` to the response headers it drops. Authorisation (dms-core `panel-auth`, fail closed), the path and query pass-through, the body handling, the `d-solo` header-hiding CSS injection and every other response header are unchanged.
  - NEW tests: `lib/monitoring/panel-proxy-headers.test.ts` (14) and `app/monitoring/panels/[...path]/route.test.ts` (18).
  - Not changed: `proxy.ts`, the FE-H1 header files, the FE-M1 files, backend, Keycloak, Docker Compose, Nginx.
- **Files applied (live development repository), byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\fem2-fix`).** SHA-256: `panel-proxy-headers.ts` F09DD3F3C49A270663547FD4F7638FCACA586812A351B9444F129B5A932444BE; `panel-proxy-headers.test.ts` D73CF1D8CFF8C4150C66894E2DC7BC50666732B8F0D7525A78E4F50E1F8D355E; `route.test.ts` 3ACF140C2D74B701129FD31827AB7A4151B89D54D70222A19B83D85279AF4894; `route.ts` BC529F4D193F8EF31FC468A0DDC96CC5AE1CC08192EFAAB187CF427E5079E1DC (was AE556C183451, unmodified in git).
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Unit and route tests: 32 of 32 pass in the live tree.** They cover: DMS and other cookies dropped in any arrangement; every `Authorization` scheme and `Proxy-Authorization` dropped; `X-Forwarded-*`, `Forwarded` and `X-Real-IP` dropped; client `X-WEBAUTH-USER` and other identity headers dropped in any case, with the dms-core user sent once; unknown headers dropped; the 13 allowed headers preserved; missing identity throws; for each of GET, HEAD, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE what Grafana receives; request body preserved byte for byte; 403 without a cookie, on a dms-core refusal, when dms-core is unreachable and when it names nobody, with no Grafana call; the access token used only for the dms-core call; and `Set-Cookie` never returned while other response headers and the body still pass. Mutation checks in the isolated copy: the original deny-list route failed 8 of 16 route tests; widening the allow-list with cookie, authorization and x-forwarded-for failed 12 of 30; removing the `set-cookie` drop failed 1 of 18 route tests.
  - **Live tree: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (6 s); ESLint `bun run lint` exit 0 with 0 errors and the same 3 existing warnings** (`nav-progress.tsx`, `sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx`, `sign-document-dialog.tsx` - none in a monitoring file; 29 s, lowest free memory 6,618 MB); **`bun run build` (Next.js 16.4.3) exit 0**, compiled in 7.1 s, 65 of 65 static pages, no new warnings (19 s, lowest free memory 5,546 MB). All ran under a 750 MB abort guard that never intervened. An earlier isolated lint attempt was aborted by the guard (low memory) and was completed later; it was not claimed as passed in between.
  - **RUNTIME VERIFICATION (live-source production build served on a private localhost port, a disposable Grafana 11.5.1 container with the auth proxy on, a stand-in for dms-core's `panel-auth` and a header-capturing pass-through; synthetic credentials only - not the shared Grafana, not Keycloak, not dms-core):** the caller was `monitor.user`, `isGrafanaAdmin=false` in all three cases - normal, with a client `Authorization: Basic <admin>`, and with a spoofed `X-WEBAUTH-USER`, `X-Forwarded-For`, `X-Custom-Secret` and `Proxy-Authorization`. Across all 8 requests the pass-through logged, Grafana received only accept, accept-encoding, accept-language, connection, content-length, content-type, host, origin, sec-fetch-mode, user-agent and `x-webauth-user=monitor.user`: **0 requests carried a cookie, `Authorization`, `X-Forwarded-For` or `Proxy-Authorization`**. The panel page returned 200 with the `d-solo` CSS injected; its first scripts and stylesheets (relative `public/build/...`) returned 200; `/api/dashboards/uid/fem2` 200; `POST /api/ds/query` 200 with a data frame; all Grafana responses were 200. With the stand-in sending three forged `Set-Cookie` headers (`dms_access_token`, `dms_refresh_token`, `grafana_session`) the browser side received **0**. No cookie, a wrong token and an empty token returned 307, 403 and 307 with **0 Grafana calls**. **FE-H1 headers remained on the route** (X-Frame-Options SAMEORIGIN, X-Content-Type-Options nosniff, Referrer-Policy strict-origin-when-cross-origin, Permissions-Policy). A browser run of the panel (canvas and legend rendered, no response of 400 or above) was done on the isolated build of the same `route.ts` earlier; it was **not repeated** on the live build because the WSL Docker daemon was being stopped and restarted about every minute by something outside this work, repeatedly removing the disposable container, so the live run used HTTP-level checks.
- **Remaining limitations.**
  - **Real dms-core, Keycloak and the shared Grafana were not exercised**; `panel-auth` was stubbed.
  - **Separate deployment concern (not remediated here, no file changed):** `infra/production/docker-compose.yml` sets no `GF_AUTH_PROXY_*` for Grafana (it has `GF_AUTH_ANONYMOUS_ENABLED` false by default, the root URL and embedding), while `infra/local/docker-compose.yml` sets `GF_AUTH_PROXY_ENABLED`, `HEADER_NAME`, `HEADER_PROPERTY`, `AUTO_SIGN_UP` and `SYNC_TTL`; **neither file sets `GF_AUTH_PROXY_WHITELIST`**, although the route's comment relies on it. The deployed Grafana configuration was not verified.
  - Grafana is still served from the DMS origin; the Grafana Live WebSocket is not proxied; the `X-Grafana-*` informational headers are dropped on purpose and would need a demonstrated need to be added.
  - Production deployment was not verified; no commit, push or deployment was made. Not started: FE-M3.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\fem2-fix` (isolated implementation, manifest, logs) and the rig scripts and logs under the session scratchpad; the disposable Grafana container, the test app and the stand-in were stopped and removed.

### FE-M3-A – Logout failure handling corrected – **PARTIALLY FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; FE-M3-B login loop remains open)

**FE-M3 remains OPEN because FE-M3-B is confirmed and unresolved.** This entry covers FE-M3-A only.

- **Original finding:** see §10 (FE-M3, "Logout swallows errors; stuck on login redirect loop", Medium, partly unverified), kept as written with its original **Medium** severity. Related: SEC-06 (§7), which is **not** changed here.
- **Root cause (FE-M3-A).** `components/layout/app-topbar.tsx` called `POST /api/v1/auth/logout` inside `try { … } catch {}` and then cleared the query cache and pushed `/login` whatever the outcome. The session lives in httpOnly cookies that only dms-core can clear, so a logout that failed (network drop, 5xx) left the person signed in while the UI behaved as if it had worked; `proxy.ts` then redirected `/login` straight back to `/`, so they landed on the app again with no message. The 15-second "intentional logout" flag also stayed set after a failure, hiding a genuine session expiry in that window.
- **What changed.**
  - NEW `frontend/lib/auth/use-logout.ts`: `useLogout()` returns `{ logout, pending }`. It marks the logout intentional immediately before the request, then: on success clears the React Query cache, pushes `/login` and refreshes (as before); on any failure (network error or non-2xx, 401 included) it shows a toast, **does not navigate, does not clear the cache**, clears the intentional-logout flag and makes the action usable again. **No automatic retry**: a second request happens only after another click. A ref guard blocks repeated clicks while a request is pending (two clicks in the same tick cannot both pass). Messages: a server error shows "Could not sign out. Please try again."; a network failure, where the server may have processed the logout before the reply was lost, shows "Could not confirm that you were signed out. Check your connection and try again." so the person is told neither "signed in" nor "signed out". Logging out twice is harmless (dms-core's logout is idempotent), so a manual retry is safe.
  - `frontend/lib/api-fetch.ts`: added `cancelIntentionalLogout()`; `markIntentionalLogout()` now restarts one 15 s timer instead of stacking timers (a second mark could previously be ended early by the first). `isIntentionalLogout` and every other export are unchanged.
  - `frontend/components/layout/app-topbar.tsx`: uses the hook in place of the inline handler (unused imports removed); the menu row reads "Signing out…" while a request is pending.
  - NEW tests: `lib/auth/use-logout.test.tsx` (22) and `lib/api-fetch.logout-flag.test.ts` (5).
  - Not changed: `proxy.ts`, `providers.tsx`, the backend, Keycloak, FE-H1, FE-M1, FE-M2, SEC-06 in §7 of this report.
- **Files applied (live development repository), byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\fem3-fix`).** SHA-256: `use-logout.ts` ED1905BFB84551FE930ABB8BF5BB4AE13C190B3131EA2A2F393760A911D29BED; `use-logout.test.tsx` D0D3A76910633D362DBAA71842641C20A823623DF9F5113FDDB1EEE6FD944A99; `api-fetch.logout-flag.test.ts` 98A54A8E6C9255E718D36658B85437E847D54155FA0FCA2183F7BA9D58B0D1D4; `api-fetch.ts` 99469F6A514099639F2C0AF93BD0A97D7C7927EB3DDE2DF0268C71D8838D72BE (was 0EC4CDF82472); `app-topbar.tsx` 8E3714E837CDD10ED412AEA3C71E068AA40A14B64D0E1D95B2AAB7EFFC34011F (was FEB2237C2EE8). Immediately before applying, the whole live frontend matched the isolation manifest (0 differences), so no concurrent change was overwritten.
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Focused: 27 of 27 pass in the live tree.** They use the real `apiFetch` with a stubbed `fetch`: success posts once with the session cookie and marks the logout intentional before the request; success clears the cache, goes to `/login` and refreshes, in that order, and keeps the suppression for 15 s; a network failure, HTTP 500 and HTTP 401 each show the right message, do not navigate or refresh, keep the cached data, clear the flag, send exactly one request (no automatic retry, and a 401 on logout does not trigger a `/refresh` call) and leave the action usable; a manual retry after a 500 succeeds; a network failure followed by a retry succeeds (reply lost after the server already ended the session); repeated clicks while pending send one request; clicks that came in meanwhile are not replayed after a failure. Mutation checks in the isolated copy: the old swallow-and-navigate behaviour failed 12 of 22 hook tests; not clearing the flag on failure failed 4; removing the duplicate guard failed 3; clearing the cache before the request failed 5.
  - **Regression: 187 of 187 pass across 8 files in the live tree** (the two new files, FE-M1 `safe-return-path` and `login-page-content`, FE-M2 monitoring helper and route, FE-H1 `security-headers`, and `use-proactive-session-refresh`).
  - **Live tree: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (26 s); ESLint `bun run lint` exit 0 with 0 errors and the same 3 existing warnings** (`nav-progress.tsx`, `sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx`, `sign-document-dialog.tsx`; 32 s); **`bun run build` (Next.js 16.4.3) exit 0**, compiled in 7.3 s, 65 of 65 static pages, no new warnings (29 s). All ran sequentially under a 750 MB abort guard that never intervened (lowest free memory 1,404 MB during the build; 1,066 MB during a second build with the stub's address baked in for the runtime test). A final plain build restored the live `.next` with no stub address in it.
  - **STUBBED RUNTIME VERIFICATION** (live-source build served on a private localhost port behind a local stand-in for dms-core's auth endpoints with made-up accounts - **not Keycloak and not dms-core**; no shared containers): HTTP 200 logout sent one request and navigated to `/login`; HTTP 500 showed "Could not sign out. Please try again.", stayed on `/` and made one request with no navigation; a dropped reply showed the "Could not confirm that you were signed out…" message and stayed on `/`; a manual retry after the dropped reply (server back to 200) reached `/login`; three clicks in the same tick sent one request, showed "Signing out…" and made one navigation; FE-M1 still worked (`/documents` to `/login?from=%2Fdocuments`, and signing in returned to `/documents`). (Chrome's network stack itself re-sent a dropped POST, so the stub counted several hits for one application request; the app made one `fetch`. Cache preservation on failure is shown by the unit tests, not in the browser.)
- **FE-M3-B (login redirect loop): CONFIRMED, UNRESOLVED, NOT FIXED.** Reproduced against the live build with the stub: with an access cookie the backend rejects and **no refresh cookie**, a protected route gets 401, refresh gets 401 with no `Set-Cookie` (`AuthService.refresh` throws without clearing when the refresh cookie is absent, `AuthService.java:256-258`), the app goes to `/session-expired`; "Sign in again" goes to `/login`, and `proxy.ts` redirects `/login` to `/` (HTTP 307, confirmed with curl) because the access cookie still exists, which fails again, so the person can never reach the sign-in form until the cookie ages out (up to 30 minutes in production). It happens with **zero logout calls**, so it is independent of FE-M3-A, and it also produced a burst of refresh requests (105 in the earlier isolated run, 31 in a shorter live run). Options, not implemented: backend - clear the session cookies when the refresh cookie is absent; `proxy.ts` - let `/login` through after a session-expired redirect (another developer is editing `proxy.ts`). A frontend-only fix cannot clear httpOnly cookies. Track separately; do not treat FE-M3 as closed.
- **Remaining limitations.**
  - **Real Keycloak and dms-core logout were not exercised**; the runtime evidence is from a stub and the 27 tests simulate the endpoint.
  - **SEC-06 is unchanged:** the access token stays valid until it expires after logout, and a failed Keycloak revocation is only logged on the backend.
  - If dms-core keeps failing, the person cannot sign out from the UI; that visibility is intended.
  - No test renders the top bar itself; the hook is covered by unit tests and the wiring by the runtime browser run.
  - Production deployment was not verified; no commit, push or deployment was made. Not started: FE-M3-B, FE-M4.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\fem3-fix` (isolated implementation, manifest, logs, the stub) and all earlier verification resources.

### FE-M3-B / FE-M3 – Session-expired login loop and terminal refresh amplification corrected – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; production verification pending)

With this entry **FE-M3 is closed at the development level**: FE-M3-A (silent logout failure, see the "FE-M3-A – Logout failure handling corrected" entry above, which is unchanged) and FE-M3-B (below) are both fixed. Production was not verified, the original **Medium** severity is kept, and **SEC-06 stays open**.

- **Original finding:** §10 FE-M3, "Logout swallows errors; stuck on login redirect loop" (Medium, partly unverified), unchanged. FE-M3-B is the second half: the "login redirect loop", reproduced and measured in the investigation and the isolated work.
- **Root causes.**
  - **Login loop.** `proxy.ts` sends anyone holding an access cookie from `/login` to `/`, treating the cookie's presence as a session. When dms-core no longer accepts that cookie (rotated signing key, changed issuer, ...) and there is no refresh cookie, nothing ever clears it: `AuthService.refresh` throws without clearing when the refresh cookie is absent (`AuthService.java:256-258`; only the Keycloak-rejected branch clears). So `/` fails with 401, refresh fails with 401, the app goes to `/session-expired`, "Sign in again" went to `/login`, `proxy.ts` bounced it to `/`, and the person could not reach the form until the cookie aged out (up to 30 minutes).
  - **Repeated refreshes.** `refreshSession` was single-flight only while a refresh was in flight; once it settled, every later failing request (the next burst of queries, a poll, a retry, the next loop cycle) sent its own `/auth/refresh`, each certain to fail the same way, with no memory of a terminal failure. Measured on the original code (stub, one stale-cookie load): 14 to 17 failed requests and 2 to 3 refreshes; each "Sign in again" cycle added about 14 failures and 2 refreshes (31 failures and 5 refreshes after one cycle, 45 and 7 after two) while the person stayed on `/session-expired`. (The 105 and 31 refresh counts seen in the very first reproduction were not reproduced in the controlled runs; refresh volume scales with loop cycles.)
- **What changed.**
  - NEW `frontend/lib/auth/session-recovery.ts`: `SIGN_IN_AGAIN_PATH = "/login?expired=1"` and `isSignInAgainRequest()`, which accepts exactly `expired=1`.
  - `frontend/proxy.ts` (+4/-1): the `/login` to `/` redirect is skipped for that request only. The flag is a request to SEE the form: it is not authentication, grants nothing, clears nothing and carries no destination (a `from` is still validated by `safeReturnPath` on the login page); every other route, `/s/` and `/portal` (another developer's public paths, preserved) and plain `/login` behave as before. A person whose session is in fact valid just sees the form; their cookies change only if they sign in.
  - `frontend/app/session-expired/page.tsx`: "Sign in again" goes to `/login?expired=1`.
  - `frontend/lib/api-fetch.ts` (+44/-11): an explicit HTTP 401 from `/auth/refresh` starts a **30-second suppression window** (`TERMINAL_REFRESH_SUPPRESSION_MS`) in which `refreshSession` answers `false` without a request, so sequential failures no longer each call dms-core; concurrent callers still share one refresh (the single-flight guard is kept, failure included). Only a 401 counts: a network error, timeout, 403 or 5xx does not start it. The window is bounded (the next failure after 30 s tries again), and it resets after a successful refresh and after a successful `POST /auth/login` (detected inside `apiFetch`, so `login-page-content.tsx` is unchanged and the reset is reached for real sign-ins). Uploads (`apiUpload`) and downloads share `refreshSession` and are unaffected. No httpOnly value is read.
  - NEW tests: `proxy.test.ts` (25), `lib/auth/session-recovery.test.ts` (14), `app/session-expired/page.test.tsx` (2), `lib/api-fetch.refresh.test.ts` (21), `lib/api-fetch.query-retries.test.tsx` (3).
  - Not changed: the backend, Keycloak, `providers.tsx`, FE-H1, FE-M1, FE-M2 and FE-M3-A files (only the approved `api-fetch.ts` update touches a FE-M3-A-era file).
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, 9 in total (3 modified, 6 new), byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\fem3b-fix`). SHA-256: `proxy.ts` 322B585A85577C015849EF89437D26D8BBBE552FDB70F6D6D474B66C4E4CF824 (was FE5F15283249); `api-fetch.ts` 03197257A9C520D9056B6F80C9043B123EBCC55A66C49CA8E4F97D03823ACCEF (was 99469F6A5140); `session-expired/page.tsx` 4076909A196CB3BB1E5A999F8C46618D9969A94B3BCCBA3FD38B17090B09F532 (was E25BC2D028D0); `session-recovery.ts` 5FBD613271C3755526ED779C846B5D5219488B733243FDFA83FEA9E8714BE44E; `proxy.test.ts` 740D6ACA8CB5B4FA12B9C996830742EC80BEFD65D153AB8608F908B6B4A96064; `page.test.tsx` 28D3BF64F2519DC26A0A8FD45F1E4D93E8FA55C694D48A2850A03F2396317E64; `api-fetch.query-retries.test.tsx` DA39BA82D92A957EED93873AB7E5FD1CD88F5B595E40AE8F5505D29D672D7BE8; `api-fetch.refresh.test.ts` DBC9942C5C5D76DEDD74358A0510FAEF84A129FEBC5CAB4A82BF72D60CE4CE55; `session-recovery.test.ts` FD65A404722C65DAB837FFF601F30166CE3B34B0E131DE90E14EE12AD94841B6. Immediately before applying, the whole live frontend matched the isolation manifest (0 differences), so no concurrent change was overwritten; the changed-entry count went from 177 to 184 as predicted.
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Focused in the live tree: 138 of 138 (login recovery, FE-M1 `from` validation, session-expired page) and 35 of 35 (refresh coordination, proactive refresh) pass.** Covered: no cookies, access only, refresh only, both, plain `/login`, `/login?expired=1`, look-alike flags still bounced, protected routes and `/s/` `/portal` unchanged, the flag opening nothing else; successful refresh, terminal 401, network error, 403, 500 and 503 not terminal, concurrent and sequential failing requests (28 failing requests over 4 batches cause 1 refresh), the 30-second expiry, no permanent lockout, successful-login reset, failed login not resetting, no recursion, uploads, and React Query default retries (20 failing requests from 5 default-retry queries cause 1 refresh). Mutation checks in the isolated copy: suppression removed (8 failures; 4 refreshes instead of 1 in the batch test, 16 instead of 1 in the React Query test), proxy flag ignored (2), button back to plain `/login` (1), any error treated as terminal (3), login reset removed (1), permanent lockout (3), single-flight removed (6). Removing the post-refresh reset fails nothing (a refresh cannot succeed while suppressed, so it is correct but not independently observable).
  - **Regression: 246 of 246 pass across 13 files** (the new files, FE-M1 `safe-return-path` and login, FE-M2 monitoring, FE-H1 `security-headers`, FE-M3-A `use-logout`, the existing `api-fetch` tests and the proactive-refresh hook).
  - **Live tree: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (16 s); ESLint `bun run lint` exit 0 with 0 errors and the same 3 existing warnings** (`nav-progress.tsx`, `sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx`, `sign-document-dialog.tsx`; 29 s); **`bun run build` (Next.js 16.4.3) exit 0**, 65 of 65 static pages (29 s). All ran sequentially under a 750 MB abort guard that never intervened (lowest free memory 2,853 MB during the build). The runtime build (stub address baked in) also passed; a final plain build restored the live `.next` with no stub address in it.
  - **STUBBED RUNTIME VERIFICATION** (live-source build on a private localhost port behind a local stand-in for dms-core's auth endpoints with made-up accounts - **not Keycloak and not dms-core**; no shared containers). Invalid access cookie and no refresh cookie: `/` failed, the app went to `/session-expired`, and the first load produced 14 failed requests and **1 refresh** (the second burst of 7 got no refresh; before: 2 to 3); "Sign in again" went to `/login?expired=1` with the form rendered and **0 further requests** (before: stayed on `/session-expired` after 31 failures and 5 refreshes, then 45 and 7); signing in reached `/` with the top bar. Login resets the suppression: 9 seconds after a terminal failure, after a sign-in, the next 401 refreshed again (refresh 1 to 2, one success). A refresh answered with HTTP 500 is not terminal: 14 failures produced 2 refreshes (vs 1 for a terminal 401). Expired access cookie with a valid refresh cookie: 1 successful refresh, stayed on `/`. Both cookies missing: `/login?from=%2F`. Invalid access and invalid refresh: 1 refresh, `/session-expired`, then the form. FE-M1: `/documents` to `/login?from=%2Fdocuments` and back to `/documents`. FE-M3-A: a 500 logout showed "Could not sign out. Please try again." and stayed on `/documents`; a retry reached `/login`. A valid session on `/login?expired=1` showed the form and stayed valid, while plain `/login` still bounced to `/`. Two tabs: tab B signed in while tab A sat on `/session-expired`; tab A's "Sign in again" showed the form, its session stayed valid and `/documents` then opened normally. The 30-second expiry is covered by the unit tests (a browser page reload resets the module state, so it cannot be observed there).
- **Remaining limitations.**
  - **Real Keycloak and dms-core were not exercised**; the runtime evidence is from a stub.
  - **SEC-06 stays open** (access token not invalidated at logout; a failed Keycloak revocation is only logged).
  - **Unverified backend hypothesis, not fixed here:** Spring may reject `/api/v1/auth/refresh` and `/api/v1/auth/logout` before the controller when the access cookie is invalid, despite `permitAll`, because `CookieBearerTokenResolver` passes the access cookie to `BearerTokenAuthenticationFilter`. If so, an invalid access cookie would also block refresh and logout even with a valid refresh cookie. Verification plan (real dms-core and Keycloak, WSL session kept open): `POST /auth/refresh` with an invalid access cookie and a valid refresh cookie; the same with no refresh cookie; `POST /auth/logout` with an invalid access cookie; and note which cookies come back cleared. Possible backend fixes if confirmed: have the resolver ignore the access cookie on `/api/v1/auth/**`, and clear stale cookies when the refresh cookie is missing and the access token does not validate (careful: a valid access cookie with no refresh cookie is a legitimate state after a browser restart on a non-"Remember me" login).
  - A stale access cookie is replaced only when the person signs in; another tab can wait up to 30 s before refreshing again after a terminal failure; the flag helps only the "Sign in again" route (typing `/login` with a stale cookie still bounces to `/` and then to `/session-expired`).
  - Production deployment was not verified; no commit, push or deployment was made. Not started: FE-M4.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\fem3b-fix` (isolated implementation, manifest, logs, the stub) and all earlier verification resources.

### FE-M4 – User administration subpage permission gating corrected – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; production verification pending)

- **Original finding:** §10 FE-M4, "Admin route gating is client-side only; user activity page ungated" (Medium), kept as written with its original **Medium** severity. This is a defence-in-depth and user-experience fix: **no backend security vulnerability was fixed**, because dms-core already enforced access and these pages hold no data yet.
- **What the investigation confirmed.** `proxy.ts` checks only that a cookie is present, by design (it cannot evaluate permissions without an API call per navigation, and the backend validates every request). The admin pages use a client-side `PermissionGate` backed by backend `@PreAuthorize`. Four user subpages had no route-level gate: `app/admin/users/[userId]/activity`, `sessions`, `mfa` and `access-summary` (the audit named only `activity`; the other three were found by the investigation and use `hasPermission(...)` only to enable buttons). Pages that look ungated at page level (`audit-log`, `relationship-types`, `ownership`, `quarantine`, `roles/[roleId]/permissions`, `watermarks`, `workflows`) delegate to `*-content.tsx` components that gate, so they were not gaps.
- **Existing backend authorization.** All four pages load the user with `GET /api/v1/users/{userId}`, which dms-core protects with `@PreAuthorize(hasAnyPermission(OTUA_USERS_VIEW, UPDATE, RESET_PASSWORD, ACTIVATE, DEACTIVATE, DELETE, ASSIGN_ROLE))` (`UserController.java`, `getUser`). Before the fix an unauthorised user saw the page shell, heading and breadcrumb, and a "no permission" toast plus an empty placeholder, never an in-page denial.
- **What changed.**
  - NEW `frontend/lib/auth/user-view-permissions.ts`: `USER_VIEW_PERMISSIONS`, the seven codes above, built from `PermissionCodes` and verified identical to the backend rule; `OTUA_USERS_CREATE` (not accepted by that endpoint) and `OTUA_USERS_IMPERSONATE` are excluded. Typed `string[]` because `PermissionGate` takes `string | string[]`. A new file, because `permission-codes.ts` carries another developer's uncommitted edit.
  - The four pages: each now renders `DashboardShell`, then the existing `PermissionGate permission={USER_VIEW_PERMISSIONS}`, then an inner content component holding the original `useUser`, breadcrumb, heading and body unchanged. So the protected content, and the request for the user it needs, exist only after the gate approves: while the page is denied, loading its permissions, or showing the permissions-error retry state, `GET /users/{id}`, `/roles` and `/platform-roles` are not requested.
  - The action-level checks are unchanged and not replaced by the gate: `sessions` and `mfa` still need `OTUA_USERS_UPDATE` for `canManage`, `access-summary` still needs `ACTIVATE` or `DEACTIVATE`. A view-only user can open the pages but gets none of the actions.
  - NEW tests: `lib/auth/user-view-permissions.test.ts` (3) and `app/admin/users/[userId]/user-subpages.test.tsx` (83).
  - Not changed: `PermissionGate`, `permission-codes.ts`, the user-detail page (it keeps its inline list and, unlike the new pages, still requests the user before its gate), `proxy.ts`, backend, Keycloak, FE-H1, FE-M1, FE-M2, FE-M3, the untracked `app/admin/sharing/`, and FE-I1.
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, 7 in total (4 modified, 3 new), byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\fem4-fix`). SHA-256: `user-view-permissions.ts` 84005F42DDCFB31A00339FA6789AFE8AA40B1C020E6D9C6015CC7CB75068B5B9; `user-view-permissions.test.ts` F2ED0550A05331B54D28267A2A715B8DAE9E8EFB35F8C53D07131A496907829D; `user-subpages.test.tsx` 2D1E86720F82ABD7C2726BB44AA1DDC2D93E6BC82AE351748E22E6711283B5CB; `activity/page.tsx` 823872F55ED924EFBD980C65F79F79C9C16D094CF373859398B1E210DDFAE68A (was EEF62B47152D); `sessions/page.tsx` 071738BC62FECE42E0FC2CD2AD081B6DEB19465E83149F20D9B3F7E3D48443C8 (was 6FFE6209384F); `mfa/page.tsx` 0340E1BBA127C9ADCA8DD90A7310462955B1919CE7301EA6F098F4BCC18B3102 (was 8B75A4008A27); `access-summary/page.tsx` 51ED08414F303993CB21E0CB2A34079502C2B13C2A90E88234DC71B51BCA4C25 (was CE2281F82C27). Immediately before applying, the whole live frontend matched the isolation manifest (0 differences), so no concurrent change was overwritten; the changed-entry count went from 184 to 191 as predicted.
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Focused in the live tree: 86 of 86 pass**, using the real `PermissionGate` and the real `usePermissions` with only the current-user data source controlled. For each of the four pages: someone with none of the accepted permissions (including holding only `CREATE` or an unrelated code) is denied inside the shell with no protected content; each of the seven codes alone lets the page render, with the shell, breadcrumb and heading intact; loading shows a spinner and never a denial; a permissions fetch failure shows the retry state (not a denial) and the retry calls `refetch`; the user request is not made while denied, loading or failed; the page's own user-loading and load-failure states are unchanged; and `canManage` is `true` only for the same permissions as before. Mutation checks in the isolated copy: the original ungated pages failed 20 tests; a gate that denies everyone failed 19; widening the sessions action check failed 4; issuing the user request before the gate failed 3; dropping a code from the constant failed 2; adding `CREATE` failed 7.
  - **Full regression: 661 of 661 pass across 64 files** in the live tree (FE-H1 headers, FE-M1 return paths, FE-M2 monitoring, FE-M3 logout and session recovery, the existing user-management tests and the 86 new tests).
  - **Live tree: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (7 s); ESLint `bun run lint` exit 0 with 0 errors and the same 3 existing warnings** (`nav-progress.tsx`, `sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx`, `sign-document-dialog.tsx`; 29 s); **`bun run build` (Next.js 16.4.3) exit 0**, 65 of 65 static pages (19 s). All ran sequentially under a 750 MB abort guard that never intervened (lowest free memory 3,148 MB, during the full test run). A runtime build with the stub address baked in also passed; a final plain build restored the live `.next` with no stub address in it.
  - **STUBBED RUNTIME VERIFICATION** (live-source build on a private localhost port behind a local stand-in for the current-user and user endpoints with made-up data - **not dms-core, not Keycloak**; no shared containers). Permission list with none of the accepted codes: all four pages showed "You don't have permission to view this page.", no user data, the sidebar and top bar intact, and **0 requests** for `/users/u1`, `/roles` and `/platform-roles`. With `OTUA_USERS_VIEW` only: all four pages rendered (activity showed Dashboard, Users, Pat Example, Activity); the user requests were made once each after approval; sessions and MFA showed no management buttons. With `OTUA_USERS_UPDATE`: MFA showed "Require MFA Setup" and "Reset MFA Credentials". With the permissions endpoint delayed 4 s: the loading indicator showed, no denial and 0 user requests, then the content loaded with 1 request each. With the permissions endpoint failing (500): the retry state showed, no denial and 0 user requests; after the stub recovered, "Try again" loaded the page. (`UserAccessSummary` accepts `canManage` but does not use it for rendering, a pre-existing quirk, so that page's action-level check is covered by the unit tests only.)
- **Remaining limitations.**
  - **Real dms-core and Keycloak were not exercised**, and a real 403 for a user without these permissions on `GET /users/{id}` was not tested.
  - **FE-I1 stays separate and open:** the fragile `error.message.includes("not found")` check is unchanged in all four pages. The only interaction is that `notFound()` now runs only after the gate approves.
  - The user-detail page still requests the user before its own gate (out of scope, unchanged).
  - The dynamic page title is set inside the gated content, so a denied page keeps its default title.
  - The page-level gate is client-side, as the finding noted; backend `@PreAuthorize` remains the real enforcement.
  - Production deployment was not verified; no commit, push or deployment was made. Not started: FE-L1.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\fem4-fix` (isolated implementation, manifest, logs, the stub) and all earlier verification resources.

### FE-L1 – Public-path prefix matching replaced with exact and segment-boundary matching – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; production verification pending)

- **Original finding:** §10 FE-L1, "Public-path `startsWith` matching (Low)" - "`proxy.ts:6,19`: `/login-anything`, `/error-foo` treated as public (redirect bypass only, no data impact)." Kept as written with its original **Low** severity. This was a wrong-page and latent-risk issue, **not a data leak and not an exploitable authentication bypass**: the look-alike paths are not pages, and every call the app makes is checked by dms-core.
- **Root cause.** `proxy.ts` held a list of prefixes (`/login`, `/reset-password`, `/update-password`, `/error`, `/login-timeout`, `/session-expired`, `/s/`, `/portal`) and tested `pathname.startsWith(path)`, so any path that merely began with one of them skipped the sign-in redirect. Confirmed on a production build before the change: signed out, `/loginfoo`, `/login-anything`, `/error-foo`, `/errors`, `/reset-passwordx`, `/session-expiredx`, `/portalx`, `/portal-admin` and `/login%2Ffoo` all returned **404 Not Found** instead of the login redirect. The latent risk: a future route named, for example, `/login-audit` or `/portal-admin` would have been silently exempt from the proxy's redirect.
- **What changed.**
  - NEW `frontend/lib/auth/public-paths.ts`: a pure `isPublicPath(pathname)`. **Exact match only** for the six standalone pages `/login`, `/login-timeout`, `/reset-password`, `/update-password`, `/error`, `/session-expired` (nothing may follow them); **whole-segment matching** for the `/portal` section (`/portal` itself and everything under `/portal/`, e.g. `/portal/accept`); and **`/s/<token>` only** (a non-empty token is required; bare `/s` and `/s/` are not pages and stay protected, as `/s` already was). Everything else, including `/shared`, is protected. No broad patterns, no decoding or normalising.
  - `frontend/proxy.ts` (+6/-6): imports the helper in place of the `PUBLIC_PATHS` array and the `startsWith` line; `isPublic` replaces the old local name. Preserved: the other developer's `/s/<token>` and `/portal` public behaviour, cookie-presence redirects, the `/login?from=<path>` redirect format, the FE-M3-B `/login?expired=1` recovery, FE-M1's `from` handling (validated by the login page, not the proxy) and the Next matcher config.
  - NEW tests: `lib/auth/public-paths.test.ts` (34); `proxy.test.ts` extended from 21 to 60 tests (the existing FE-M3-B tests kept).
  - Not changed: `session-recovery.ts`, `safe-return-path.ts`, FE-H1, FE-M2, FE-M3 and FE-M4 files, backend, Keycloak, the untracked `app/admin/sharing/`. The comments in several pages that still say "see PUBLIC_PATHS in proxy.ts" were left as they are (comment-only).
- **How Next hands paths to the proxy (checked against a production build, and why the helper does no normalising).** A trailing slash (`/portal/`, `/login/`, `/s/`) and repeated slashes (`//login`, `///documents`) are redirected with a 308 before the proxy decides anything; dot segments (`/login/../documents`, `/portal/%2e%2e/documents`) are resolved first and the result is protected; a percent-encoded look-alike (`/%6Cogin`, `/login%2Ffoo`) reaches the proxy still encoded, matches nothing and is treated as protected (the safe direction); `/LOGIN` is case-sensitive and protected; a query string is not part of the pathname.
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, 4 in total (2 modified, 2 new), byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\fel1-fix`). SHA-256: `public-paths.ts` EA0A4E2B88065977FA05CC71D1CB966A421EEAF99F79101AFEEDFFB569B84091; `public-paths.test.ts` B3E6E33CC6D60233FD1778F2A618E0536747CB53DD94A9A399BD07C714D3FD38; `proxy.ts` 17A46585E9C9E69A37ADAFE8AA68898E375CC1ED276800669E313B61E146FB82 (was 322B585A85577C015849EF89437D26D8BBBE552FDB70F6D6D474B66C4E4CF824); `proxy.test.ts` 35B7074BB0C08D118D5734A68B50994B546C34358F00BB3AD2A9F7BC2025C745 (was 740D6ACA8CB5B4FA12B9C996830742EC80BEFD65D153AB8608F908B6B4A96064). Immediately before applying, the whole live frontend matched the isolation manifest (639 files, 0 differences), so no concurrent change was overwritten; the changed-entry count went from 191 to 193 (the two new files; `proxy.ts` and `proxy.test.ts` were already listed).
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Focused in the live tree: 34 of 34 helper tests and 60 of 60 proxy tests pass.** Helper: every public page, the portal and its descendants, share links, 18 look-alikes (`/loginfoo`, `/login-anything`, `/error-foo`, `/errors`, `/reset-passwordx`, `/session-expiredx`, `/portalx`, `/portal-admin`, `/shared`, ...), nothing allowed after a standalone page, bare `/s` and `/s/` protected, protected application routes, case sensitivity, encoded variants, dot segments and repeated slashes, query strings, empty and odd input. Proxy: the cookie matrix (neither cookie, access only, refresh only, both), look-alikes redirected when signed out and passed through when a cookie is present, `/admin` and `/monitoring` protected, public pages, `/portal/accept` and `/s/abc123` open, a query string unable to open a path, plain `/login`, `/login?expired=1` and FE-M1's `from`. Mutation checks in the isolated copy: the original `startsWith` behaviour failed 30 tests; a portal section without a segment boundary failed 6; a public bare `/s/` failed 2; public pages matched by prefix failed 23; `/portal` removed from the public set failed 5; a proxy that treats everything as public failed 16.
  - **Full regression: 734 of 734 pass across 65 files** in the live tree (the earlier 661 plus the 73 new tests), covering FE-H1, FE-M1, FE-M2, FE-M3-A/B and FE-M4.
  - **Live tree: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (7 s); ESLint `bun run lint` exit 0 with 0 errors and the same 3 existing warnings** (`nav-progress.tsx`, `sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx`, `sign-document-dialog.tsx`; 29 s); **`bun run build` (Next.js 16.4.3) exit 0**, 65 of 65 static pages (19 s). All ran sequentially under a 750 MB abort guard that never intervened (lowest free memory 3,074 MB, during the full test run). A runtime build with a stub address baked in also passed; a final plain build restored the live `.next` with no stub address in it.
  - **HTTP and browser verification** (live-source production build on a private localhost port behind a local stand-in for the auth calls with made-up accounts - **not dms-core, not Keycloak**; no shared containers). Before (original proxy), signed out: the nine look-alike paths above returned 404. After, live build: **307 with `Location: /login?from=<path>`** for `/loginfoo`, `/login-anything`, `/error-foo`, `/errors`, `/reset-passwordx`, `/session-expiredx`, `/portalx`, `/portal-admin`, `/login%2Ffoo` (for example `/loginfoo` goes to `/login?from=%2Floginfoo`); `/shared`, `/s`, `/documents`, `/admin/users`, `/monitoring` and `/monitoring/panels/x` also 307 to the login page; `/login`, `/login-timeout`, `/reset-password`, `/update-password`, `/error`, `/session-expired`, `/portal`, `/portal/accept` and `/s/abc123` returned 200 with no redirect; trailing-slash, repeated-slash, dot-segment, encoded and case variants behaved as described above. Across the 35 probed paths exactly the nine look-alikes changed between before and after, and the live results are identical to the isolated results. In the browser: signed out, `/loginfoo`, `/portal-admin` and `/error-foo` landed on the sign-in form with no not-found page; `/portal/accept` and `/s/abc123` stayed open; FE-M1 still returned `/documents` to `/documents` after sign-in; FE-M3-B still worked (stale cookie, `/session-expired`, "Sign in again" to `/login?expired=1`, the form rendered, signing in reached the app); a signed-in visitor to plain `/login` still bounced to `/`. (The "before" evidence is HTTP, from the isolated work; it was not repeated in a browser.)
- **Remaining limitations.**
  - **Real dms-core and Keycloak were not exercised**; the runtime evidence is from a stub.
  - Any future public route must be added to `public-paths.ts` (a new public page to `PUBLIC_PAGES`, or a new section); an unlisted route is protected, which is the intended safe default. The sharing developer owns the `/portal` and `/s/` entries.
  - `/s/<token>/extra` is still a not-found page (there is no such route), as before.
  - For an encoded path the redirect's `from` value is double-encoded (for example `from=%2Flogin%252Ffoo`); that is existing behaviour and FE-M1's validation rejects it as a destination.
  - FE-L2, FE-L3 and FE-I1 are untouched and remain open. Production deployment was not verified; no commit, push or deployment was made.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\fel1-fix` (isolated implementation, manifest, probe results, the stub) and all earlier verification resources.

### FE-L2 – Scan upload file validation and size-display improvements – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; production verification pending)

**No client-side maximum upload-size limit was introduced.** Upload size stays unlimited, as approved under DOC-03 ("upload size stays UNLIMITED (product requirement)"); DOC-03 is unchanged.

- **Original finding:** §10 FE-L2, "Upload UI has no size/MIME validation (Low)" - "`components/documents/document-upload-form.tsx:375` (`accept` only; bypassable); `:472` raw byte formatting vs `formatBytes` elsewhere. Large file transfers fully before 413/415." Kept as written with its original **Low** severity. The finding was **partly confirmed**: the file-type and display parts are real; the "no size validation" part is closed by the unlimited-size decision, so it was deliberately not implemented. Not a security issue: dms-core validates the extension and the content (magic bytes) independently.
- **What was confirmed.** The form is the Scan Documents (OCR) page at `/documents/scan`. Its file field had `accept=".pdf,.png,.jpg,.jpeg,.tiff"`, which is advisory only (the picker's "All files" option gets past it), omitted `.tif` (which the server accepts), and `onChange` stored any file with no check, so a wrong file type was uploaded in full before the server refused it. The size line printed raw bytes, e.g. "2,345,678 bytes", although the app has a shared `formatBytes`.
- **What changed.**
  - NEW `frontend/lib/documents/scan-upload-files.ts`: `SCAN_FILE_EXTENSIONS` (`.pdf`, `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.tif`, `.tiff`), `SCAN_FILE_ACCEPT`, `SCAN_FILE_HINT`, a pure `validateScanFile(file)` and `describeUploadSize()`. It reads only the file's name and size - never the browser MIME type, and it does not repeat the backend's content check. The last extension is matched case-insensitively and is decided exactly as dms-core's `FileTypeInspector.extensionOf` does (path separators dropped; a missing, leading-dot or trailing-dot name has no extension), so `document.exe.pdf` passes (its final extension is allowed; the server checks the content) while `document.pdf.exe` is refused. A zero-byte file is refused with its own message. The only size rule is "not empty": nothing compares a size to a threshold.
  - `frontend/components/documents/document-upload-form.tsx` (+57/-5): the file field uses the shared `accept` string (so `.tif` is now offered); a new `handleFileChange` keeps a chosen file only if it passes, otherwise drops it, clears the native input and shows an inline `role="alert"` message (with `aria-invalid`), and a valid choice clears the message; Upload cannot start for a rejected file; `handleUpload` revalidates the file immediately before `uploadDocument` is called; "Upload another document" clears the message; the size line now shows a human-readable size through the existing `formatBytes` (no NaN or undefined; "unknown size" for an invalid value), with the exact byte count on hover.
  - NEW tests: `lib/documents/scan-upload-files.test.ts` (42) and `components/documents/document-upload-form.validation.test.tsx` (37), in a separate file so the existing form tests stay untouched.
  - Not changed: the backend (extension allow-list, magic-byte validation, size policy), `formatBytes`, `lib/documents/api.ts`, `document-browser.tsx`, `lib/api-fetch.ts`, `proxy.ts`, `permission-codes.ts` and the untracked `app/admin/sharing/` (the last five carry or sit near other developers' work).
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, 4 in total (3 new, 1 modified), byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\fel2-fix`). SHA-256: `scan-upload-files.ts` 7FD347C056AF5C572FEBA43CFEFDECCA14A82A2C2D6069CB2809C85F8A6EC40A; `scan-upload-files.test.ts` 3628525A221AC4C6F0922B5EF73A0CDAC34CDCDB73493B698C11C9AE31F668CA; `document-upload-form.validation.test.tsx` 5B0816508765204A3DBFF857C267E8D3342A47A46976076BD49A64F819A12A60; `document-upload-form.tsx` 0AA26EF38D303F1423CED8700F17ABE4E20F1DC8FE35D5BE840F5E83C53FEBB6 (was 37E0598B717344DC...). Immediately before applying, the whole live frontend matched the isolation manifest (641 files, 0 differences), so no concurrent change was overwritten; the changed-entry count went from 193 to 197 (three new files plus the previously unmodified form).
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Focused in the live tree: 42 of 42 helper tests and 46 of 46 form tests pass** (the 37 new ones plus the 9 existing polling and retry tests). Covered: the six extensions and mixed case, multiple dots, double extensions, unusual names, rejected types, missing, trailing-dot and leading-dot names, zero-byte files, a very large file-like object (no multi-gigabyte buffer), MIME independence, the accept string being exactly the six extensions; and in the form: unsupported and zero-byte files (message shown, Upload blocked, `uploadDocument` never called), invalid-to-valid and valid-to-invalid recovery, submit-time revalidation (a file whose name changes between selection and submit), a file reporting 6 GB accepted and uploaded, the size line, the server's 415 message and a malware-gate refusal shown unchanged (not as a client validation error), polling (processing, queued, done), retry (shown only for retryable failures) and the reset. Mutation checks in the isolated copy: the original `onChange` failed 13 tests; removing `.tif` failed 9; case-sensitive matching 10; extension matched by `includes` 6; no zero-byte check 3; raw bytes display 3; a 100 MB cap added 2; a leading dot accepted 2; no submit-time revalidation 1; an invalid file kept 1.
  - **Unlimited-size check on the applied source:** no size constant or threshold exists; the only size comparisons are `file.size === 0` (permitted) and the display guards for a non-finite or negative number; `formatBytes` is used for display only.
  - **Full regression: 813 of 813 pass across 67 files** in the live tree (FE-H1, FE-M1, FE-M2, FE-M3-A/B, FE-M4, FE-L1 and the existing upload tests).
  - **Live tree: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (7 s); ESLint `bun run lint` exit 0 with 0 errors and the same 3 existing warnings** (`nav-progress.tsx`, `sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx`, `sign-document-dialog.tsx`; 29 s); **`bun run build` (Next.js 16.4.3) exit 0**, 65 of 65 static pages (19 s). All ran sequentially under a 750 MB abort guard that never intervened (lowest free memory 2,554 MB, during the full test run). A runtime build with a stub address baked in also passed; a final plain build restored the live `.next` with no stub address in it.
  - **BROWSER VERIFICATION** (live-source production build behind a local stand-in for the page's few API calls with made-up data - **not dms-core**; no shared containers): the picker's `accept` is `.pdf,.png,.jpg,.jpeg,.tif,.tiff`; `setup.exe` showed the message at once, the file input was cleared, Upload stayed disabled and 0 upload requests were sent; a zero-byte `empty.pdf` was refused the same way; `SCAN.TIF` and `scan.tif` were accepted; a valid `scan.pdf` (2,345,874 bytes) with a document type chosen enabled Upload and produced exactly one upload request under that name, then "Reviewing", polling (3 status checks) and "Done"; the size line read "2.2 MB" with the hover "2,345,874 bytes" and no NaN or undefined; a 64 MB `.tiff` was accepted and uploaded (64 MB reached the stand-in) with no client size rejection; a simulated server 415 showed the server's own message in the existing upload-error banner, with no client field error and the form still usable; and choosing `good.pdf` then `bad.exe` left Upload disabled and sent nothing. No multi-gigabyte upload was attempted.
- **Remaining limitations.**
  - **Real dms-core was not exercised**; the browser evidence is from a stand-in. The server remains the authority: a file with an allowed extension but the wrong content is refused only by dms-core.
  - The form's list (PDF, PNG, JPEG, TIFF) is intentionally narrower than the server's 32 accepted types, because this form is for scanned documents.
  - Whether the server answers a rejected type before the browser finishes sending a very large file was not measured.
  - **A separate, pre-existing issue observed, not fixed here:** on this form the Upload button and `handleUpload` read the raw `documentType` state (`document-upload-form.tsx`, state at line 114, the button at line 494, `handleUpload` at lines 274 and 283), which stays empty until the user changes the dropdown, although the dropdown displays the default type ("General", via `effectiveDocumentType`, lines 159-160). In the live browser run Upload stayed disabled for a valid file until a type was chosen explicitly. It is not covered by any existing finding and has no audit ID yet; it should be tracked as a separate follow-up.
  - FE-L3 and FE-I1 are untouched and remain open. Production deployment was not verified; no commit, push or deployment was made.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\fel2-fix` (isolated implementation, manifest, logs, the stand-in) and all earlier verification resources.

### FE-L3 – ESLint warnings resolved in navigation progress and signing dialogs – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; production verification pending)

- **Original finding:** §10 FE-L3, "ESLint warnings (Low)" - "`components/layout/nav-progress.tsx:71` exhaustive-deps; `signatures/sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx:117`, `sign-document-dialog.tsx:81` `watch()` incompatible-library." Kept as written with its original **Low** severity. All three were lint-only: the React Compiler is not enabled in this project, so none of them had a runtime effect, and no signing or navigation behaviour was wrong. The `watch()` warnings said the two dialogs would be skipped by the compiler if it were ever turned on.
- **The three warnings.** (1) `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` at `nav-progress.tsx:71`: the effect's cleanup read `timers.current` again when it ran. (2) and (3) `react-hooks/incompatible-library` at `sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx:117` and `sign-document-dialog.tsx:81`: five bare `watch()` calls on each dialog's form.
- **What changed.** No lint rule was disabled and `eslint.config.mjs` was not touched.
  - `components/layout/nav-progress.tsx` (+5/-1): the ref's object is captured once inside the effect (`const timerRefs = timers.current`) and the cleanup uses it. The ref holds one object for the life of the component, mutated and never reassigned, so behaviour is identical.
  - `components/signatures/sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx` and `sign-document-dialog.tsx` (+8/-7 each): the five `watch()` calls became one `useWatch({ control, name: [...] })`, following the repository's existing `watermark-dialog.tsx` pattern, with `control` taken from `useForm`. Unchanged: the schema, default values, the saved-signature pre-fill effect, every `setValue` and `trigger` call, the step flow, the payload construction and the error handling.
  - NEW tests: `components/layout/nav-progress.test.tsx` (20) and `components/signatures/signing-dialogs.test.tsx` (55, run against both dialogs).
  - Not changed: `app-topbar.tsx`, the FE-L2 upload form (FE-L4 stays open), `lib/documents/api.ts`, `document-browser.tsx`, `lib/api-fetch.ts`, `proxy.ts`, `permission-codes.ts`, the untracked `app/admin/sharing/`, the backend and Keycloak.
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, 5 in total (3 modified, 2 new), byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\fel3-fix`). SHA-256: `nav-progress.tsx` 4D036D439E7CEAF115BFAC2BE2A1B5DA9C5259D9280893E0674650FDCE377553 (was EAFB03F9E2DF); `sign-and-approve-dialog.tsx` 165BD368F3AF212A1BB170213E5781B77124B5129B46976EC4E6EA6792815454 (was 74D3DFEB172B); `sign-document-dialog.tsx` FD4F2EE251FF236A7DDBBC676BCD8618C4C73F29EAD2F62D2BA2E0A123A7AA0F (was 5220B284111B); `nav-progress.test.tsx` 2B4BB5FB88351C6F8795D2D69FBC9384E17A28C8181EBFF357258C693752A14B; `signing-dialogs.test.tsx` CD7A8E8829417982D0392AE8B48283ECD3A0573A604D707F8A8529CB806DBFD5. Immediately before applying, the whole live frontend matched the isolation manifest (644 files, 0 differences), so no concurrent change was overwritten; the changed-entry count went from 197 to 202 (three previously unmodified sources plus two new tests).
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Behaviour was pinned before the change.** The 75 tests were written first and run against the original, unchanged files (55 dialog tests and 20 navigation tests, all passing), then run unchanged after the refactor. They drive the real dialogs - the real react-hook-form, schema and step logic - and replace only the canvas editor and the two placement steps, whose replacements echo their props.
  - **Focused in the live tree: 20 of 20 navigation and 55 of 55 signing-dialog tests pass.** The signing tests assert the exact mutation payloads: typed (text, no image), drawn and uploaded (the matching image only, no text), `meaning` `approved` for Sign and Approve and `acknowledged` for Sign Document (and no `meaning` in the Sign Document request body of the component), a trimmed empty comment sent as null, the consent and signature validation messages, the 150-character limit, saved-signature pre-fill into the correct slot only, independent drawn and uploaded slots, Back clearing the password, the placement step's Back keeping everything, a failed signing keeping the dialog open on step 3 with only the password cleared, a retry succeeding, and the disabled state while signing. The navigation tests cover starting on an internal link, completing on a route change, the 260 ms hide delay, the 20-second safety timer, unmounting (including during the hide delay) clearing every timer, no state updates after unmount, and repeated navigation. Mutation checks in the isolated copy: consent dropped from `useWatch` failed 21 tests, the signature kind dropped 11, the drawn and uploaded names swapped 6, `meaning` changed 10, the password not cleared after a failure 1, and removing either timer clear from the navigation cleanup failed 1 each.
  - **Full regression: 888 of 888 pass across 69 files** in the live tree (the 813 baseline plus 75 new), covering FE-H1, FE-M1, FE-M2, FE-M3, FE-M4, FE-L1 and FE-L2. Lowest free memory 2,097 MB.
  - **Live tree: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (13 s); ESLint `bun run lint` exit 0 with no output at all - 0 errors and 0 warnings, down from 3 (64 s); `bun run build` (Next.js 16.4.3) exit 0, 65 of 65 static pages (35 s).** All ran sequentially under a 750 MB abort guard.
  - **Memory-guard interruption and recovery.** The first live attempt at the full suite was aborted by the guard when free memory dipped to 597 MB (below 750 MB) after the five files had been applied and the 75 focused tests had passed; the remaining checks were deliberately not retried automatically. After approval the full suite was run once more under the same guard and passed, then `tsc`, lint and build in turn; free memory had returned to about 4 GB and the dip was transient (cause not identified).

Sign Document: typed (blocked until consent, "Enter your password" on an empty password, payload `typed` with the name and `meaning` `acknowledged`), uploaded (a generated PNG through the real background-removal flow; payload `uploaded` with a base64 PNG and no text), and drawn (real mouse strokes on the canvas; payload `drawn` with a base64 PNG). Sign and Approve: opened with the document and stage, typed (payload `meaning` `approved`), uploaded and drawn (each the matching image only). A rejected signing (step-up failure) showed "Your password or authenticator app code is incorrect.", cleared only the password, stayed on step 3 and kept the dialog open, and a retry sent the same signature with the new password and closed the dialog, in both dialogs; Back from step 3 cleared the password. ### FE-L4 – Scan upload default document type usable without touching the dropdown – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; production verification pending)

- **Original finding:** §10 FE-L4 (found during the FE-L2 verification, not in the original audit). The Scan Documents dropdown displayed a default ("General", or the first active type) while the Upload button, `handleUpload` and an inner lookup read the raw `documentType` state, which starts as `""`. A valid file therefore left Upload disabled until the person changed the dropdown.
- **Root cause (confirmed):** one value shown, another used. Also found: the OCR `requestedFields` come from the selected type's schema, which loads asynchronously, so an upload before it arrived would have carried no fields.
- **What changed** (one file modified, +18/-6 against the FE-L2 version, CRLF kept; one test file added). No upload size cap was added and FE-L2 validation is unchanged.
  - `components/documents/document-upload-form.tsx`: `selectedType` is the person's explicit choice while that type is still ACTIVE, otherwise the preferred type (GENERAL, then the first active type, then none). The dropdown, the field schema, the Upload button, the handler guard and the upload payload all read it, so they cannot disagree.
  - **Invalid choices are discarded, not just hidden.** Once a loaded catalogue no longer offers the explicit choice as an active type (deactivated or removed), the choice is cleared (state adjusted during render; lint forbids setting state in an effect). If that type later becomes active again it is **not** re-selected; the person must pick it again. A loading or failed catalogue fetch never clears a choice.
  - **Upload is disabled while the selected type's schema is loading** (`!file || !selectedType || schemaQuery.isLoading || uploading`), so a payload cannot go out with no OCR fields. A schema *error* does not block the upload (existing behaviour kept). This gate goes beyond the original proposal and was approved explicitly.
  - NEW `components/documents/document-upload-form.document-type.test.tsx` (29 tests): default type, late catalogue, no usable type, explicit choice (kept on refetch, fallback on deactivation and on removal, no re-selection after reactivation or re-appearance, re-pick works, fallback to first active when GENERAL is absent), OCR fields follow the submitted type, schema-loading gate, "Upload another document", and the FE-L2 file rules (unsupported, zero-byte, very large accepted).
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verificationel4-fix`). ### FE-I1 – HTTP-status-based not-found handling across eight frontend pages – **FIXED – TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; production verification pending)

- **Original finding:** §10 FE-I1, "Stale copy / fragile not-found handling" (**Informational**), kept as written: `app/admin/users/[userId]/activity/page.tsx:20-24,29` decided "this user does not exist" with `error.message.includes("not found")` instead of `ApiError.status === 404`, and its comment said an audit endpoint did not exist. The register row keeps its historical FAIL convention.
- **Root cause (confirmed).** Eight pages (user detail, edit, activity, sessions, MFA, access summary, audit-log detail, quarantine detail) tested the words of the error message. It worked only because dms-core happens to word its 404 "<Thing> not found: <id>" and `include-message: always` (API-07) is on. Consequences: a **500 or 403 worded that way was shown as a missing resource**; a real 404 worded differently ("There is nothing at this address.", an empty body) fell through to a load failure; and turning API-07 off would have silently stopped every one of these pages reaching `notFound()`. Every fetch these pages use goes through `apiFetchJson`, which throws a genuine `ApiError`, so the status is always available. No other message-based not-found check exists in the frontend.
- **What changed (11 files; no backend, no `api-fetch.ts`, no change to `ApiError`).**
  - `lib/api-error.ts` (+11): one new export, `isNotFound(error)` = `error instanceof ApiError && error.status === 404`. The file already existed, so the helper was added to it rather than creating a new one.
  - The eight pages: one import and one changed condition each, `if (isError && isNotFound(error))` (3 changed lines per page). `notFound()` placement, gates, loading and error rendering, requests and ordering are untouched.
  - `activity/page.tsx` additionally: the stale comment now says the feed is still always empty because `toUserRecord` sets `recentActivity: []` and nothing reads the audit log, that `GET /api/v1/audit-events` exists (filterable by `objectId`/`actorId`, needing `ABDR_VIEW_AUDIT_HISTORY`) but is not used here yet, and that wiring it is separate work. Documentation only; the activity feed was **not** wired up.
  - NEW `lib/api-error.test.ts` (14) and NEW `app/admin/not-found-handling.test.tsx` (172).
  - **Deliberate behaviour change:** a plain `Error` worded "not found" no longer shows the not-found screen - only a status can say 404.
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\fei1-fix`). SHA-256 (was): `api-error.ts` 8EC8AD9A882B569E64A72F1F45159884636ADCAB71ADC973903E0197687AD6C0 (4EA7613AA183); `users/[userId]/page.tsx` ADF306A39CCAEB1FEAE2D247B9817047607352568A3BE1B365A8658B57C28B32 (A0B04818E2CE); `edit/page.tsx` BF144D45DEB97745139852E3A2D8E379928F7032198F1D1F43C734B975FC8894 (EEDCF9F64F32); `activity/page.tsx` A33EF1CBB37AB1C5E6AB3860351B81F3063DBD2F686A8964C4E21D46D75CDC7E (823872F55ED9); `sessions/page.tsx` CA905793F13F36DFC59BDD6C11565029F038B86EFBA91DA3DC7FD43A25A791BA (071738BC62FE); `mfa/page.tsx` 24F273E39E9A678B51D742D27251A338EB459B95A00A1C022824ECB3E4D90287 (0340E1BBA127); `access-summary/page.tsx` EFA1DC4D0799063A1EA0660D185EE10026BF2768622FE728ED61ABDF753C8E28 (51ED08414F30); `audit-log/[id]/page.tsx` 4BDB112359D6D22BDA43AE8ADCA8254B6D290E0F390B8B1C04F9DAF7A4DEC5DE (C5544192E1E0); `quarantine/[id]/page.tsx` 5D2B1ED6FFC786D6D48B7D09081575C99E3035F46530111F699424D2F27B49E9 (7432A8D636F7); `api-error.test.ts` 91182948F8F68C35A9A5A36AE25DB1C1E5B30761086D3CD0EBD39F7B8EAF0863 (new); `not-found-handling.test.tsx` F5EFA8825AB7A48EA85DA4F8FCDDF6F77D5001519469BA3FA39AE8059CEE16B5 (new).
