### NOT-01 – Abandoned notification deliveries are recovered instead of staying `sending` forever – **FIXED – TARGETED TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; full integration suite not green; production verification pending)

- **Original finding:** §11 NOT-01, "Deliveries stuck in `sending` forever" (**High**), kept as written. The register row now points here; the original finding text is unchanged.
- **Root cause (confirmed in the current source and reproduced).** The dispatcher claim (`DeliveryClaimRepository`) moves a due `pending` delivery to `sending` and leases it, but its predicate accepts only `pending` rows, and nothing else ever looked at a `sending` row again. A delivery therefore stayed `sending` for ever whenever anything stopped between the claim and the result write: the instance stopping or crashing (a restart in the middle of a batch strands up to the batch size, 50), an exception from rendering or the mail API that is not a `MailException`, a missing sender, a missing notification row, or a failure to save the result. The comments claiming "the lease lets another instance reclaim a row" and "the lease expires and it is retried" were false, and the admin retry refuses `sending` rows, so the UI could not recover them either. Reproduced before the fix: 3 of 4 baseline unit tests failed on the original dispatcher (sender throws, missing sender, missing notification each left the row `sending`).
- **What changed (5 files, under `backend/dms-core/src/`; no migration, no change to the email templates, the admin retry API, `NotificationProperties`, `EmailChannelSender` or the other developer's notification edits).**
  - `notification/dispatch/DeliveryClaimRepository.java` (+58/-6): the normal claim keeps its `pending`-only predicate and now also returns the lease it granted (`claimLeases`; `claimDue` is kept). New `lockExpiredLeases` selects `status = 'sending'` rows whose lease is null or past, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, bounded by the batch size. New `lockIfStillLeased` row-locks one delivery only while it is still `sending` under exactly the lease the caller was given.
  - `notification/dispatch/NotificationDispatcher.java` (+175/-34): `runOnce` first calls `recoverExpiredLeases()` (a failure there is logged and never stops new deliveries), then claims as before.
    - **Recovery and attempt counting.** An abandoned attempt is counted exactly once, by whichever of the worker or the recovery writes its outcome; the two cannot both win. Recovery records it as attempt n+1 with outcome `failed` and error code `lease_expired` in the append-only attempt history, then returns the row to `pending` with the existing backoff, or sets `failed` at `max_attempts` and writes the usual `NOTIFICATION_DELIVERY_FAILED` audit event after commit. A poison delivery therefore ends `failed` after exactly `max_attempts` recoveries; it cannot retry for ever.
    - **Stale-worker fencing.** The worker writes its result only while `lockIfStillLeased` still finds the row `sending` under the lease it was claimed with (the lease timestamp is the token; no schema change). A worker that finishes after its lease was recovered or re-claimed finds the token gone and discards its result: no save, no attempt row, no audit.
    - **Unexpected failures.** A sender that throws a `RuntimeException`, and a channel with no sender, are now recorded as ordinary failed attempts (`unexpected_error`, `no_sender`) and retried with backoff instead of stranding the row. A missing notification row (unreachable in practice because of the foreign key) closes the delivery as `failed` rather than leaving it leased. A failure to save the result is not swallowed and not reported as success: the row stays `sending` until its lease runs out and recovery then counts the attempt. `Error`s are not caught.
    - **Audit detail corrected:** the final-failure audit event now carries the delivery as updated in the transaction, so its `attempts` value is right (the original passed the pre-transaction copy, one too low).
  - NEW `NotificationDispatcherRecoveryTest.java` (21 mock-based tests), NEW `DeliveryClaimRepositorySqlTest.java` (6 SQL-text tests), NEW `NotificationLeaseRecoveryIT.java` (6 PostgreSQL tests).
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\not01-fix`). SHA-256 (was): `DeliveryClaimRepository.java` 05630594DD7ED037FBF65298948DED78DB4D2F6558B7A2C76FDC112A54A58C29 (469EAC57A4E4); `NotificationDispatcher.java` F084DE33F7D1AEFCF39C9DB91B4E7979129059848F91941F020D247316FD5730 (FFA5E2833DD7); `NotificationDispatcherRecoveryTest.java` D0EDA1FC29C4F898407749E4E9579ACD193E9B2D99DF0AFD041523CF2BFD68D3 (new); `DeliveryClaimRepositorySqlTest.java` 42E1E0D8258408525192D51A3C32C52C2374B17332864D676CC9E3AFC3B1C757 (new); `NotificationLeaseRecoveryIT.java` 16A1EF7156812FE8A04976B0D784A9C6D0EA69090CABCEF1B13724CC5F66112F (new). The two modified files were unmodified in the working tree and matched their baseline hashes at application time.
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Mutation checks (isolated), all caught:** no fencing (2 failures); recovery not counting the attempt (9); no max-attempts cap (4); sender exception not caught (2); no recovery call in `runOnce` (2); missing sender not handled (1); recovery ignoring the row's status (1).
  - **LIVE development tree, executed:** the 27 new unit tests pass, together with the existing `NotificationMailRendererTest` (30 of 30, exit 0). The backend unit regression (`mvnw -o test`) ran 1,246 tests with **0 failures, 39 errors and 5 skipped**; the 39 errors are exactly the 11 database-backed classes (`DmsCoreApplicationTests`, `ExportJobRepositoryTest` and the nine `V*MigrationTest` classes) with `Connection to localhost:5433 refused`, because no PostgreSQL was running for this live run (the temporary instance had been stopped and was deliberately not restarted); this is the same set and count as the original audit. `mvnw -o -DskipTests package` succeeded and built `dms-core.jar`. **No PostgreSQL integration test was run against the live tree.**
  - **PostgreSQL integration evidence (isolated workspace only, not re-run against live):** a temporary, portable PostgreSQL 18.7 (EnterpriseDB Windows x64 zip, SHA-256 EAD0BBC27FB678FA24DCF86B738981323042ABD073C744103BF7F3AED23F9A33; executables validly signed by EnterpriseDB Corporation) bound to `127.0.0.1:55433` with its own data directory and random credentials; the database identity was verified before every run and RabbitMQ, Redis, Keycloak and ClamAV were pinned to unused ports. 137 Flyway migrations applied to v138. `NotificationLeaseRecoveryIT` 6 of 6 pass (recovery then send; live lease left alone; null lease; exhaustion fails and is audited; two dispatchers racing record exactly one `lease_expired`; a worker that lost its lease cannot write a result and only the new lease is valid). Re-run three more times together with `NotificationConcurrencyIT`: 33 of 33 pass. Existing notification integration tests: `NotificationApiIT` 5/5, `NotificationConcurrencyIT` 5/5, `NotificationDispatcherIT` 2/2, `NotificationServiceIT` 8/8, `NotificationTriggersIT` 7/8 (the failure is unrelated, below). The 11 database-backed unit classes pass on that instance, 39 of 39. Full `mvnw verify` in the isolated workspace: unit 1,246 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 5 skipped; **integration 961 run, 22 failures, 1 error, 7 skipped - the full integration suite is NOT green.**
- **Full integration-suite limitations (23 failing tests, accounted for individually from the failure reports; none of the failing classes references the dispatcher, `DeliveryClaimRepository` or the delivery tables, which does not by itself prove they are unaffected).**
  - **16 - attributed to the unavailable ClamAV scan service:** `IngestionZipLimitsIT` (2), `IngestionFailureCleanupIT` (5), `VersionPromotionRefusalIT` (9). Their reports show the scan left `pending` ("expected clean but was pending", "The scan service is temporarily unavailable"), and one records `clamd at 127.0.0.1:55310 is unreachable`.
  - **1 - attributed to the unavailable RabbitMQ:** `OcrEventsConsumerLiveIT` (`AmqpConnectException: Connection refused`).
  - **1 - unrelated, pre-existing mismatch:** `NotificationTriggersIT.sharingAFolder_tellsTheNewPeople_andLinksToSharedWithMe` expects the link `/shared` but gets `/documents?folder=...`, because another developer's uncommitted edit to `AccessGrantedNotificationListener` changed the link while the committed test still expects the old one.
  - **5 - cause NOT confirmed (outstanding verification limitation):** `OcrJobTokenHealthGroupIT` (a 503 from the `core` health group, consistent with the unavailable RabbitMQ and Redis but not directly confirmed), `BulkImportConcurrencyIT` (expects infected content to be refused; consistent with the unavailable ClamAV), `ConcurrentVersionUploadIT` (empty result list) and `WopiSaveSpoolIT` (2; expected 2 versions, found 1). They may well be dependency-related, but that was not proven, and they were not run with the dependencies available.
  - **Correction recorded:** an earlier summary of the same 23 failures was given as 17 ClamAV, 3 RabbitMQ/Redis, 1 unrelated and 2 unconfirmed; the per-test recount above (16 / 1 / 1 / 5) is the accurate split. Either way, **not all 23 are proven unrelated to NOT-01**, ClamAV, RabbitMQ, Redis, Keycloak and OpenBao were not started (the 7 skipped integration tests are `OpenBaoTransitKeyServiceIT`), and the full integration suite has **not** passed.
- **Delivery semantics and remaining risks.**
  - **NOT-02 stays open: delivery is at-least-once, not exactly-once.** If the mail server accepts a message but the result is never saved (a crash in between), or a stale worker has already sent before losing its lease, the recovered attempt sends the message again and the recipient may receive a duplicate. A unit test pins this behaviour.
  - **No index for the recovery scan.** `sending` rows have no partial index, so the recovery query scans the table on every 30-second poll; on a large delivery table that cost grows. A partial index needs a migration, which was not added without approval.
  - Recovery, like the claim, works across all tenants.
  - Real SMTP and the real two-instance deployment were not exercised; the mail went to an in-process test server.
  - **Production verification pending.** No commit, push or deployment was made, and no database migration was run.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\not01-fix` (isolated implementation, manifests, logs, and the stopped temporary PostgreSQL cluster and binaries, not yet deleted) and all earlier verification resources.

### OBX-01 – The outbox relay no longer lets two instances publish the same pending events – **FIXED – TARGETED TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; full integration suite not green; production verification pending)

- **Original finding:** §11 OBX-01, "Outbox relay not multi-instance safe" (**High**), kept as written. The register row now points here; the original finding text is unchanged.
- **Root cause (confirmed in the source and reproduced).** `OutboxEventRepository.findBatchToPublish` was a plain select of `pending` rows ordered by `created_at`, with no row lock. Every replica's scheduled `OutboxRelay` therefore selected the same batch (up to 100 rows), and each published every event in it: duplicate messages on the broker, and so duplicate downstream work such as OCR requests, with the number of copies growing with the number of instances.
- **What changed (5 files, under `backend/dms-core/src/`; no migration, no change to RabbitMQ publisher confirmation, consumers, the wire format, or NOT-01).**
  - `outbox/OutboxEventRepository.java`: `findBatchToPublish(Pageable)` is now a default method that delegates to a native query, `lockPendingBatch(limit)`: `select * from outbox_events where status = 'pending' order by created_at asc limit :limit for update skip locked`. A relay that finds rows locked by another relay skips them and takes the next ones, so concurrent relays work on disjoint rows. Both methods are `@Transactional(propagation = MANDATORY)`, so the select cannot run outside a transaction (a lock taken outside one would be released at once and protect nothing); an unpaged request is refused. `countDeadLettered` is unchanged.
  - `outbox/OutboxRelay.java`: comment-only change documenting the multi-instance behaviour, the at-least-once limit and the lock duration. No logic change.
  - NEW `OutboxEventRepositoryQueryTest.java` (5 tests), NEW `OutboxRelayBehaviourTest.java` (9 tests), NEW `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT.java` (13 PostgreSQL tests, real database with a mocked `RabbitTemplate`).
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\obx01-fix`). SHA-256 (was): `OutboxEventRepository.java` 32E5D96B6942B772EBB29B9F111F3D7E2D24AA77D013F5848224687D80293A97 (BB96C11FF978); `OutboxRelay.java` 5867F3E1BE3C83C950D3DD0C486245AB1A3D14E91974DD175E3300C270745934 (07E9C4254D28); `OutboxEventRepositoryQueryTest.java` 3D21D695F7BD3D5227675DDD07E655EFE19F0F2A380CA14EF3FC412CBE84ABDA (new); `OutboxRelayBehaviourTest.java` 154EF112E294B06BF64865380040FF2CCD9B2064F0683ADA6D7264C756CDEAA1 (new); `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT.java` 717F7407C83994E6C2033B9F0B8D8C8D733C6F783BE075DF4776A528E8424CD7 (new). The two modified files matched their baseline hashes at application time; the three new files did not exist.
- **Tests and results.**
  - **Baseline reproduction (isolated, PostgreSQL 18.7, original query):** `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT` 13 run, **6 failures**. Two transactions selected the same rows; four relays published 1,360 messages for 430 events; two failing relays sent 8 messages for 4 events; two concurrent relays duplicated the first hundred rows; a second transaction could see rows the first held; the select did not refuse to run outside a transaction.
  - **Fixed implementation (isolated, PostgreSQL 18.7):** 137 Flyway migrations applied to v138; `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT` **13 of 13 pass**, and three further complete runs passed 13 of 13 each (**52 executions passed in total**). Concurrent relays take disjoint rows (250 rows, no duplicate; four relays drain 430 events with none published twice); locks are held until commit and released by rollback; rolled-back rows stay `pending` and are retried; `published` and `failed` rows are never selected; attempt counts are not lost or doubled; a failing row does not stop its siblings; ordering within a batch is oldest first. Database-backed regression (the 11 classes): 39 of 39 pass. Four mutation checks, all caught.
  - **Wire format preserved:** `OutboxRelayWireFormatTest` passes 6 of 6 unchanged.
  - **LIVE development tree, executed:** the focused run (the 5 + 9 new unit tests, `OutboxRelayWireFormatTest`, and the NOT-01 `NotificationDispatcherRecoveryTest` and `DeliveryClaimRepositorySqlTest`) passed 47 of 47. The backend unit regression (`mvnw -o test`) ran 1,260 tests with **0 failures, 39 errors and 5 skipped**; the 39 errors are exactly the 11 database-backed classes (`DmsCoreApplicationTests`, `ExportJobRepositoryTest` and the nine `V*MigrationTest` classes) because no PostgreSQL was running (the database address was pinned to an unused port); same set and count as before. `mvnw -o -DskipTests package` succeeded and built `dms-core.jar`. **No PostgreSQL integration test, including `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT`, was re-run against the live tree**; the isolated PostgreSQL evidence applies to the implementation hashes above.
- **Full integration-suite limitations (isolated `mvnw verify`).** Unit: 1,260 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 5 skipped. **Integration: 974 run, 22 failures, 1 error, 7 skipped; the full integration suite is NOT green.** These are the same 23 failures recorded for NOT-01, in the same classes and counts, none in the outbox classes: 16 attributed to the unavailable ClamAV (`IngestionZipLimitsIT`, `IngestionFailureCleanupIT`, `VersionPromotionRefusalIT`), 1 to the unavailable RabbitMQ (`OcrEventsConsumerLiveIT`), 1 unrelated mismatch (`NotificationTriggersIT` folder link), and **5 whose cause was not confirmed** (`OcrJobTokenHealthGroupIT`, `BulkImportConcurrencyIT`, `ConcurrentVersionUploadIT`, `WopiSaveSpoolIT` x2). ClamAV, RabbitMQ, Redis, Keycloak and OpenBao were not started (the 7 skipped are `OpenBaoTransitKeyServiceIT`).
- **Delivery semantics and remaining risks.**
  - **At-least-once, not exactly-once.** If a relay publishes and then fails before its transaction commits (a crash, or a rollback), the rows stay `pending` and the next poll publishes them again. Duplicates therefore remain possible; consumers must stay idempotent (a test pins this behaviour).
  - **Lock duration.** A batch of up to 100 publishes holds its row locks and one database connection for the whole batch, so a slow broker lengthens it. Rows locked by one relay are skipped, not waited for, by the others.
  - Ordering across several instances is not guaranteed.
  - **Not addressed (separate findings, still open):** OBX-02 (marking events published without broker confirmation), OBX-03, OBX-04.
  - Two real application instances against a real RabbitMQ were not exercised.
  - **Production verification pending.** No commit, push or deployment was made, and no database migration was added.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\obx01-fix` (isolated implementation, manifests, logs, and the stopped temporary PostgreSQL cluster, not deleted), `verification\not01-fix` (including the retained PostgreSQL binaries) and all earlier verification resources.

### OBX-02 – Outbox events are marked `published` only after the broker confirms them and does not return them – **FIXED – TARGETED TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; full integration suite not green; production verification pending)

- **Original finding:** §11 OBX-02, "Events marked published without broker confirmation / routing" (**High**), kept as written. The register row now points here; the original finding text is unchanged.
- **Root cause (confirmed in the source and reproduced).** `OutboxRelay` called `RabbitTemplate.send` and marked the row `published` as soon as the call returned. `send` returns once the frame is written to the channel; it says nothing about whether the broker took the message. The template was built by hand, so `spring.rabbitmq.publisher-confirm-type=correlated` and `publisher-returns=true` changed nothing: no `CorrelationData` was passed, nothing waited for a confirm, and without `mandatory` the broker silently dropped a message that no queue is bound for. A broker nack, an unroutable message (including any event type without a binding), a lost connection after the write, and a confirmation that never arrived were all recorded as delivered.
- **Baseline reproduction (isolated, controlled broker outcomes, not real RabbitMQ).** `OutboxRelayObx02DefectTest` against the original relay: **3 of 3 fail** (a nack, an unroutable return, and a publish that is never confirmed each left the row `published`). Rerun against the final test files and passed on the fix.
- **What changed (12 files, under `backend/dms-core/src/`; no migration, no change to queue topology, routing keys, consumers, the wire format, NOT-01 or OBX-01 locking, and `application.yml` was not touched).**
  - `outbox/OutboxRelay.java`: every publish carries its own `CorrelationData` (id = event id + a random UUID, so an answer can only match the publish it belongs to). The whole batch is sent first on ONE dedicated channel (`RabbitTemplate.invoke`) and the confirmations are then awaited against ONE deadline for the batch, `dms.outbox.confirm-timeout-ms` (default **5,000 ms**; non-positive values are refused, so there is no unbounded wait; an answer already there is read even after the deadline). A row becomes `published` only after a positive confirm with no return. A nack, a timeout, a failed confirmation future, a returned message, a send that throws, and a channel that cannot be opened each count one attempt, leave the row `pending`, and the fifth attempt fails it for good with the existing audit event: the attempt policy, ordering and wire format are unchanged. A late confirmation completes only its own abandoned `CorrelationData`. If the waiting thread is interrupted, the relay stops waiting, keeps the interrupt flag, and leaves the rows it did not hear about untouched (`pending`, attempt not counted); the transaction commits what was settled.
  - `messaging/RabbitMqConfig.java`: the template is `mandatory`, so an unroutable message is returned (`312 NO_ROUTE`) and recorded on that publish's own `CorrelationData` before its confirm; a positive confirm after a return is still a failure. A returns callback only logs a warning, without the payload. The relay publishes on a **dedicated publisher connection** (`OutboxPublisherConnection`, deliberately not a `ConnectionFactory` bean, so Spring Boot keeps its own connection factory for the listeners), built from the same Boot properties, with confirms and returns forced on and a **publisher-only channel RPC timeout**, `dms.outbox.channel-rpc-timeout-ms` (default **10,000 ms**). Reason: a channel with unconfirmed publishes is not reused, so after a stalled batch the next batch must open a new channel, a broker round trip the confirmation deadline does not cover; the Rabbit client waits 10 minutes for it by default, and against a paused broker the relay was seen blocked for 130+ s holding its transaction's row locks and a database connection. Setting the timeout on the shared client factory would also have changed the OCR and bulk-import consumers' declarations, bindings and startup; it is scoped to the publisher only (a test checks the listener connection keeps the client default of 600,000 ms). `spring.rabbitmq.ssl.*` is refused at startup rather than silently ignored (see the TLS prerequisite below).
  - Tests: NEW `OutboxRelayObx02DefectTest.java` (3), NEW `OutboxRelayConfirmationTest.java` (23), NEW `OutboxConfirms.java` (stand-in for broker outcomes), NEW `OutboxRelayConfirmationIT.java` (9, PostgreSQL), NEW `OutboxRelayInterruptIT.java` (1, PostgreSQL), NEW `OutboxRelayBrokerIT.java` and NEW `OutboxRelayBrokerSpecialIT.java` (real RabbitMQ; skipped unless `OBX02_RABBIT_VHOST` names a disposable `obx02*` virtual host); the OBX-01 `OutboxRelayBehaviourTest.java`, `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT.java` and `OutboxRelayWireFormatTest.java` were adapted to the confirm-aware send only.
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\obx02-fix`). SHA-256 (was): `RabbitMqConfig.java` 4572F004EC6AEABE6AEA215AEFB18B0DB40B8B7E30A61C41BA4717179B0A4955 (3F1652E1B369); `OutboxRelay.java` 4CED2CCEBD28647420BDF6BCC577B82615F9875144CC215FB998A9B5E948D43A (5867F3E1BE3C); `OutboxRelayBehaviourTest.java` 46278C8194615083625A82AD0E813B88CB5F9ABFABF35065FF7DEA0AB02C53B3 (154EF112E294); `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT.java` 41DD58B0FD6C37E7CDC43C62B096D438F5BB1AA2B1B94F060E625F0B8C4E601B (717F7407C839); `OutboxRelayWireFormatTest.java` E25FAD1234F9311C4C6D804D4DFA1657F9FE577355256A6436681905739DE631 (0DBE86912A46); new: `OutboxConfirms.java` 9C9FCA2474C78B56D3913809E4835325D48673DB077C3D262EDD69889A53962C; `OutboxRelayConfirmationTest.java` 7EA3AE47D75909F7B09D6A50E06654231A4937CAAF355DB1344FAB5BE40891FD; `OutboxRelayConfirmationIT.java` E32A36612D43E022AFA0406ED654B1DE826D347C2E8E25069BC71C41C11DA1DB; `OutboxRelayBrokerIT.java` 7F089216C0C4D1E5A8C1183C44C89C11AEDD1A0C68CDF1B433D4B1DAD2D27874; `OutboxRelayBrokerSpecialIT.java` 5DE35BE4A041F700081EC8B8B0975663FAF2130A47EDBD1EF997C6DCA90AF1E7; `OutboxRelayObx02DefectTest.java` 892A5592F1CBBCB28D1E4286831A53E4B308EC4CE7E65E9F275AD894C86BC92A; `OutboxRelayInterruptIT.java` F00B6BA385684FA9B2BD08CB12FED6202D041408CCD94234CF57FA85B50439B4. Every target matched its recorded baseline (or was absent) at application time. The working tree went from 219 to 228 entries (2 modified source files, one of which was already modified; 3 modified tests, two of which were already untracked; 7 new files); the full before/after path-and-status manifests were compared and only these paths changed. `NOT-01` and `OBX-01` implementation files are unchanged.
- **Isolated verification evidence (NOT live reruns).** Temporary, portable, loopback-only PostgreSQL 18.7 and RabbitMQ 4.4.1 (Erlang/OTP 28.5.0.8; official GitHub release zips, SHA-256 matched the published digests; GPG signatures not verified), random credentials, disposable `obx02_it` virtual host, default `guest` user deleted.
  - **PostgreSQL integration (137 migrations to v138):** `OutboxRelayConfirmationIT` 9, `OutboxRelayInterruptIT` 1 and the OBX-01 `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT` **13 of 13**, **23 of 23 pass** on the final code (earlier design: 21/21 then 22/22, 22/22, 22/22). Confirms persist `published`; nacks, returns and timeouts leave rows `pending` with one attempt; mixed batches keep per-event outcomes; a rollback after a confirmed publish leaves the row retryable (sent twice: at-least-once); two relays, one waiting for confirms while holding its locks, take disjoint rows with no duplicate and no lost attempt counts; an interrupted wait commits the confirmed rows and leaves the unheard ones `pending` with no attempt counted and no locks left.
  - **Real RabbitMQ:** `OutboxRelayBrokerIT` **7 of 7** and `OutboxRelayBrokerSpecialIT` 2 of 2 (plus the stall tests below): a routed message is confirmed and arrives with the same format and routing key; an unroutable one is returned `NO_ROUTE` even though the broker confirms it (in 299 of 299 publishes the return was recorded before the confirm); a missing exchange is a 404 nack; a real nack (full reject-publish queue) leaves the row `pending`; 100 in-flight messages are each confirmed and arrive once; mixed batches settle per event; a refused connection fails one attempt and the same row publishes once the broker is reachable. **Stall tests (broker process paused, so a confirmation really never arrives):** the first batch took 2.04 s against a 2 s deadline and later batches about 4.0 s each (the channel-open bound shortened to 4 s for the test); after the broker resumed, the 20 held correlations cleared, the late confirmations changed nothing, and the retry published; the first stalled batch was delivered on resume and sent again by the retry. **Cold-connect stall** (broker frozen before the first connection): a batch of 20 took 5.05 s and failed one attempt per row. Before the publisher-only timeout, the same stall blocked the relay for 130+ s.
  - **Full isolated unit suite (with PostgreSQL): 1,286 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 5 skipped**, including the 39 database-backed unit tests. Mutation checks (isolated, earlier design): ignoring a nack, ignoring a return, treating a timeout as success, and sharing one correlation id per event were all caught. **NOT-01 focused regression: 27 of 27** (`NotificationDispatcherRecoveryTest` 21, `DeliveryClaimRepositorySqlTest` 6). OBX-01 query, behaviour and wire-format tests: 5, 9 and 6, passing.
- **LIVE development tree, executed:** the focused run (`OutboxRelayConfirmationTest` 23, `OutboxRelayObx02DefectTest` 3, `OutboxRelayBehaviourTest` 9, `OutboxEventRepositoryQueryTest` 5, `OutboxRelayWireFormatTest` 6, `NotificationDispatcherRecoveryTest` 21, `DeliveryClaimRepositorySqlTest` 6) passed **73 of 73**, exit 0, with the timeout-scoping test among them. The backend unit regression (`mvnw -o test`, no database or broker) ran 1,286 tests with **0 failures, 39 errors and 5 skipped**; the 39 errors are exactly the 11 database-backed classes (`DmsCoreApplicationTests`, `ExportJobRepositoryTest` and the nine `V*MigrationTest` classes) because no PostgreSQL was running (the database address was pinned to an unused port); the same classes pass 39 of 39 on the isolated PostgreSQL. `mvnw -o -DskipTests package` succeeded and built `dms-core.jar`. **No PostgreSQL or RabbitMQ integration test was re-run against the live tree**; the isolated evidence applies to the implementation hashes above.
- **Full integration-suite limitations.** An isolated `mvnw verify` was started for this finding and was killed by the system's low-memory protection during the unit phase, so it was not completed and not repeated. The most recent complete integration run (OBX-01, isolated) was **974 run, 22 failures, 1 error, 7 skipped; the full integration suite is NOT green** (ClamAV-dependent scan tests, `OcrEventsConsumerLiveIT` needing RabbitMQ, an unrelated `NotificationTriggersIT` folder-link mismatch, and 5 failures whose cause was never confirmed). ClamAV, Redis, Keycloak and OpenBao were not started.
- **Production dependencies and limitations (unresolved, not fixed).**
  - **`user.created` routing.** `UserServiceImpl.java` (user provisioning) emits it; no consumer, queue or binding for it was found in this repository or its infra files, and none was verified in any deployment (an external consumer is possible but unverified). The broker returns it as unroutable, so every `user.created` event now fails after five attempts (about ten seconds) and writes a permanent-failure audit event, where it was previously dropped silently. This needs a separate decision before deployment: bind a queue or provision a consumer, stop emitting the event, or accept the failed rows. No queue, consumer or routing was added.
  - **Worker startup ordering and retry budget.** Worker-owned queues (for example the OCR worker's) are declared by the workers themselves; an event relayed before a worker has first started is now returned and retried instead of being dropped, but the existing five-attempt, no-backoff policy (OBX-03) can exhaust before a slow worker starts, and a sustained broker outage can spend the budget in about a minute.
  - **RabbitMQ TLS.** The dedicated publisher connection cannot use Spring Boot's internal SSL-bundle support, so enabling `spring.rabbitmq.ssl.*` makes the application refuse to start until `RabbitMqConfig.outboxPublisherConnection` is extended. This is a prerequisite before production TLS to the broker.
  - **Timeout limits.** Bounded and measured: the batch confirmation deadline, and the channel-open wait on an established or a cold connection to a frozen broker. NOT fully verified: a TCP connect to a black-holed host (the client's default is 60 s; `spring.rabbitmq.connection-timeout` applies to the publisher connection too and should be set), a blocked socket write, the client's 10 s handshake bound, and what happens over many consecutive confirm timeouts on a live but silent channel. No absolute upper bound is claimed for a stalled batch.
  - **At-least-once, not exactly-once.** After a confirmed publish followed by a database rollback or a process failure, or after a timed-out publish the broker did accept, the row stays `pending` and is published again; consumers must stay idempotent.
  - The 5 s deadline and 10 s channel-open bound add to the time one relay holds its row locks and database connection; the relay shares the scheduler thread with other jobs (PERF-03).
  - A real multi-instance deployment against a real broker, and the full integration suite, remain pending. **Production verification pending.** No commit, push or deployment was made, and no database migration was added. OBX-03 and OBX-04 are untouched.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\obx02-fix` (isolated implementation, before/after working-tree manifests, logs, and the stopped temporary PostgreSQL cluster and portable RabbitMQ/Erlang, not deleted), `verification\obx01-fix`, `verification\not01-fix` and all earlier verification resources.

### OBX-03 – Failed outbox publishes are retried on a persisted backoff schedule, inside a bounded retry window – **FIXED – TARGETED TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; full integration suite not green; production verification pending)

> **Renumbering note (2026-11-19).** The migration this entry calls `V139__outbox_next_attempt_at.sql` is now `V145__outbox_next_attempt_at.sql`, and its test `V139OutboxNextAttemptMigrationTest` is now `V145OutboxNextAttemptMigrationTest` (renamed because upstream took V138 to V143; see DB-08). The SQL is byte-identical; the text below is kept as written and still says V139. Updated manifest: `verification\obx03-fix\logs\final_manifest_v3.txt`.

- **Original finding:** §11 OBX-03, "Weak retry policy" (**Medium**), kept as written. The register row now points here; the original finding text is unchanged.
- **Root cause (confirmed in the source and reproduced).** After a failed publish the relay retried at the very next poll, two seconds later, and the fifth failure - about eight seconds into a broker outage - failed the event permanently. Nothing in the application ever re-published a `failed` event. The table had no retry time, so the batch query could neither wait for a retry nor skip an event that had to wait; unroutable events (a missing binding, a worker queue not declared yet) used up the same five-attempt budget in seconds. Recoverable events (OCR requests, bulk-import submissions) were therefore lost by an outage of about ten seconds or by a worker that started late. The long-transaction part of the finding was already reduced by OBX-02 (bounded confirmation deadline and channel-open timeout); the transaction model is unchanged here.
- **Baseline reproduction (isolated, original OBX-02 source; mock repository that returns only pending rows).** `OutboxRelayObx03DefectTest`: **5 of 5 fail** - five quick failures permanently failed a recoverable event; a recovered broker did not get the events that failed during the outage; an unroutable event used up its whole retry budget in five polls; a failed publish had no retry time (no `nextAttemptAt`); the batch query had no eligibility test. All five pass on the fix.
- **What changed (19 files, under `backend/dms-core/src/`; no change to `RabbitMqConfig`, queue topology, routing keys, consumers, the wire format, NOT-01 or `application.yml`).**
  - **Migration `V139__outbox_next_attempt_at.sql` (source only; NOT executed against any database):** `next_attempt_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()` (a stable default: PostgreSQL stores a fast default, no table rewrite; every existing row gets the migration time, so every pending row stays eligible; status, attempts, `last_error`, payload, `created_at` and `published_at` are untouched); nullable `first_failed_at TIMESTAMPTZ` with no default (null = not failed yet, or failed before the column existed; nothing is invented for existing rows); partial index `ix_outbox_events_pending_due` on `(next_attempt_at, created_at, outbox_event_id) WHERE status = 'pending'`, which does not replace `ix_outbox_events_status_created_at` (OBX-04's decision). V139 was verified free (the latest migration is V138, another developer's untracked file).
  - `outbox/OutboxEventRepository.java`: the OBX-01 locking query now selects `status = 'pending' AND next_attempt_at <= now()` ordered by `next_attempt_at, created_at, outbox_event_id`, still `LIMIT :limit FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and still `MANDATORY`, so a row waiting for its retry is not selected and cannot starve rows that are ready; new `databaseClockMillis()` reads the DATABASE's `clock_timestamp()`.
  - `outbox/OutboxEvent.java`: `nextAttemptAt` (not inserted by the application - the column default makes a new event due at once on the database clock) and `firstFailedAt`.
  - NEW `outbox/OutboxRetryPolicy.java`: delay after the n-th failed attempt = `initial * multiplier^(n-1)`, capped at the maximum, then spread by random jitter; always positive (never in the past); jitter is deterministic in tests; safe arithmetic (exponent capped, multiplier limited to 1-10). **Approved development defaults** (approved for development testing, NOT yet validated against production workload requirements), all configurable and validated at startup: `dms.outbox.retry.initial-delay-ms` 10 s, `multiplier` 2, `max-delay-ms` 15 min nominal, `jitter` +-20% (so the actual maximum delay is 18 min), `max-age-ms` 24 h retry window, `max-attempts` 150 (a runaway guard: about 126 attempts is the most the window can hold at the minimum jitter spacing).
  - `outbox/OutboxRelay.java`: after a failed publish (nack, return, confirmation timeout, failed confirmation, send that threw, channel that could not be opened) it counts one attempt, stores the error, sets `first_failed_at` on the first failure observed, and sets `next_attempt_at` = database clock + backoff. The database clock is read ONCE per batch, only when something failed, BEFORE any row is touched, and there is NO fallback to the application clock (a failed read rolls the whole batch back; the rows stay as they were and are picked up again). **The retry window starts at the first failed publish, not at creation**: an event that waited any length of time for the relay still gets the whole window. An event fails permanently only when (1) its first failure is at least the window old (exactly at the boundary included; one millisecond inside, one final attempt is scheduled, which may fall up to 18 min after the window ends), (2) the 150th failed attempt is reached, or (3) its payload cannot be encoded as a message (deterministic; retried never). Every other outcome - including an unbound routing key and a missing exchange, which become routable when a worker starts or a binding is added - is retried on the same schedule. Attempts are never reset; the terminal failure writes the existing `OUTBOX_EVENT_PUBLISH_FAILED` audit event; an interrupted wait leaves unheard rows untouched; a rollback leaves the previous state.
  - Tests: NEW `OutboxRetryPolicyTest` (22), `OutboxRelayRetryTest` (23), `OutboxRelayObx03DefectTest` (5), `OutboxRetrySchedulingIT` (16, PostgreSQL), `V139OutboxNextAttemptMigrationTest` (5, PostgreSQL scratch databases), `OutboxRelayRetryBrokerIT` (RabbitMQ; skipped unless `OBX02_RABBIT_VHOST` names a disposable `obx02*`/`obx03*` virtual host); the OBX-01/OBX-02 tests `OutboxConfirms`, `OutboxEventRepositoryQueryTest`, `OutboxRelayBehaviourTest`, `OutboxRelayConfirmationTest`, `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT`, `OutboxRelayConfirmationIT`, `OutboxRelayBrokerIT` and `OutboxRelayBrokerSpecialIT` were adapted only where the old five-attempt rule or an immediate retry was pinned (no assertion was weakened to pass).
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\obx03-fix`, manifest `logs\final_manifest_v2.txt`; 11 modified, 8 new). `OutboxRetrySchedulingIT.java` 67941E19195363038A5096ECAE28B14D57D660E8FD4BE59071BC4CBFE007C1EC; `V139OutboxNextAttemptMigrationTest.java` FBC3A0BCF123B87540A1CC9431641E687004A730C1101147BAF07672DCDFD65D. Every modified target matched its recorded baseline and every new target was absent at application time. The working tree went from 228 to 237 entries (`OutboxEvent.java` became modified; 8 new files); the full before/after path-and-status manifests were compared and only these paths changed. `OutboxRelay.java`, `RabbitMqConfig.java`, the NOT-01 files and `application.yml` (another developer's uncommitted edit) are as they were or, for `OutboxRelay.java`, as intended above.
- **Isolated verification evidence (NOT live reruns).** Temporary, loopback-only, disposable PostgreSQL 18.7 and RabbitMQ 4.4.1 (Erlang/OTP 28.5.0.8), random credentials, disposable `obx03_it` virtual host, `guest` deleted; both stopped afterwards.
  - **Migration (5 of 5):** empty database; a populated outbox table with pending, published and failed rows (every other column of every row unchanged, every pending row eligible, no first-failure invented, the table not rewritten); Flyway ordering through V139; a failure half way through the migration rolled the whole migration back (column included) and it then applied cleanly. Disposable large-table measurement: 500,414 rows (75 MB): migration 329 ms, a concurrent writer's slowest insert 118 ms.
  - **PostgreSQL integration (39 of 39, two complete runs; 138 migrations to v139):** `OutboxRetrySchedulingIT` 16, OBX-01 `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT` 13, `OutboxRelayConfirmationIT` 9, `OutboxRelayInterruptIT` 1. Retry times persist on the database clock (about 10 s ahead); a row waiting for its retry is not selected; 150 older waiting rows did not starve 10 ready rows; the order among due rows is due time, creation, id; a 450-row backlog drains in batches with no row sent twice; a relay holding the due rows makes another skip them; rollbacks leave the retry state exactly as it was; terminal failures write the audit event; an event 25 h old at its first failure keeps a whole window; the first-failure origin survives later failures. **Query plan** (`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)`, disposable table of 308,751 rows with 7,000 pending, 54 MB): an index scan on `ix_outbox_events_pending_due`, then LockRows and Limit, no Sort, 105 buffers, 0.077 ms; the partial index is 600 kB against 2,136 kB for the existing non-partial index. These are disposable-database figures, not production estimates.
  - **RabbitMQ:** `OutboxRelayRetryBrokerIT` 4 of 4 (a broker away then back: the event waits for its retry then goes out after one failed attempt; a missing worker queue is returned `NO_ROUTE`, retried with delay and delivered once declared; a missing exchange is retried and delivered once declared; at-least-once duplicate after a rollback) and one stall test with only the OBX-03 broker process paused (a confirmation that never arrives): one timeout, then five polls with no attempt (the retry is not due), a longer backoff on the next, delivery after resume, the queue holding the duplicate. OBX-02 regression on the same broker: `OutboxRelayBrokerIT` 7 of 7, `OutboxRelayBrokerSpecialIT` and both stall tests unchanged (2.06 s / about 4.0 s per batch; cold connect 5.06 s).
  - **Unit regression:** full isolated unit suite with PostgreSQL **1,331 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 5 skipped** (including the 39 database-backed unit tests and the 5 V139 tests). **NOT-01 focused regression: 27 of 27.** Mutation checks, all caught: retrying on the application clock, no delay cap, no jitter, the window ignored, an unencodable payload retried, never giving up, the window measured from creation, the first-failure origin never set, and the clock fallback put back.
  - Two defects found and corrected during isolated verification: an application-clock fallback for the retry time (removed: a failed database-clock read means a broken transaction, so the fallback could only hide skew), and a retry window measured from creation (now from the first failed publish, new `first_failed_at`); the multiplier was also made configurable and validated.
- **LIVE development tree, executed:** the focused run (`OutboxRetryPolicyTest` 22, `OutboxRelayRetryTest` 23, `OutboxRelayObx03DefectTest` 5, `OutboxRelayBehaviourTest` 9, `OutboxRelayConfirmationTest` 23, `OutboxRelayObx02DefectTest` 3, `OutboxEventRepositoryQueryTest` 7, `OutboxRelayWireFormatTest` 6, `NotificationDispatcherRecoveryTest` 21, `DeliveryClaimRepositorySqlTest` 6) passed **125 of 125**, exit 0. The backend unit regression (`mvnw -o -B test`, no database or broker) ran 1,343 tests with **0 failures, 44 errors and 5 skipped**; the 44 errors are exactly the 11 database-backed classes (`DmsCoreApplicationTests`, `ExportJobRepositoryTest`, the nine `V*MigrationTest` classes: 39 tests) plus the 5 `V139OutboxNextAttemptMigrationTest` tests, because no PostgreSQL was running (the database address was pinned to an unused port); the same classes pass on the isolated PostgreSQL. `mvnw -o -DskipTests package` succeeded and built `dms-core.jar`. **No PostgreSQL or RabbitMQ integration test was re-run against the live tree, and the migration was not executed anywhere against an existing database**; the isolated evidence applies to the implementation hashes above.
- **Full integration-suite limitations.** The most recent complete integration run (OBX-01, isolated) was **974 run, 22 failures, 1 error, 7 skipped; the full integration suite is NOT green** (ClamAV-dependent scan tests, `OcrEventsConsumerLiveIT` needing RabbitMQ, an unrelated `NotificationTriggersIT` folder-link mismatch, and 5 failures whose cause was never confirmed). A later isolated `mvnw verify` was killed by the system's low-memory protection and not repeated. ClamAV, Redis, Keycloak and OpenBao were not started for this finding.
- **Production dependencies and limitations (unresolved, NOT fixed).**
  1. **Audited manual replay of terminally failed events** is required before production (not before development) and is not implemented: it needs administrator authorization, tenant isolation, a row lock, an audit event, the payload and attempt history preserved, and explicit replay-generation or attempt-reset semantics (resetting only `first_failed_at` would leave a 150-attempt event exhausted again at once). No direct SQL replay is authorized. Until then a failed event is final, and any state that depends on it (an OCR job left `queued`, a bulk-import batch left `queued`) is not recovered by the application.
  2. **`user.created` routing decision:** no consumer or binding was verified; every such event is returned as unroutable, stays pending for the 24 h window, and then fails with the audit event. The team must decide whether to provision a consumer, stop emitting it, or accept terminal failures.
  3. **No outbox metrics:** pending count, failed count, oldest pending age and retry attempts are not exported. 4. **No terminal-failure alert.**
  5. **Production migration and index-build review:** the non-concurrent index build was measured only on disposable data and blocks writes for roughly one table scan; it must be reviewed against the real table size and write load, and a `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` build needs a separate migration plan and approval. V139 must be confirmed free when applied.
  6. **The retry defaults are approved for development testing, not validated against production workload requirements.**
  7. **Multi-instance deployment** against a real broker was not exercised. 8. **RabbitMQ TLS:** the dedicated publisher connection refuses `spring.rabbitmq.ssl.*` (OBX-02). 9. The **full integration suite** remains to be run.
  10. **At-least-once, not exactly-once:** a confirmed publish followed by a rollback or crash, and a timed-out publish the broker did accept, are published again; consumers must stay idempotent.
  11. **Remaining broker timeout and transaction-duration limits:** a TCP connect to a black-holed host, a blocked socket write, the client's handshake bound and repeated silent-channel timeouts are not fully verified; a stalled batch still holds its row locks and one database connection for up to the confirmation deadline plus the channel-open bound. The relay shares a two-thread scheduler pool with about twenty other jobs (PERF-03). OBX-04 (retention, the existing non-partial index) is untouched.
  - **Production verification pending.** No commit, push or deployment was made.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\obx03-fix` (isolated implementation, manifests, logs, and the stopped temporary PostgreSQL cluster and RabbitMQ base directory, not deleted), `verification\obx02-fix` (including the portable RabbitMQ/Erlang binaries), `verification\obx01-fix`, `verification\not01-fix` and all earlier verification resources.

### OBX-04 – Published outbox rows are deleted after a retention period by a bounded, disabled-by-default cleanup – **FIXED – TARGETED TESTS PASSED** (development repository only; full integration suite not green; production verification pending)

> **Renumbering note (2026-11-19).** The migration this entry calls `V140__outbox_published_cleanup_index.sql` is now `V146__outbox_published_cleanup_index.sql`, and its test `V140OutboxPublishedCleanupIndexMigrationTest` is now `V146OutboxPublishedCleanupIndexMigrationTest` (renamed because upstream took V138 to V143; see DB-08). The SQL is byte-identical (one comment inside it still names the old test class); the text below is kept as written and still says V140 (and V139 for the OBX-03 migration, now V145). Updated manifest: `verification\obx04-fix\logs\final_manifest_v3.txt`.

- **Original finding:** §11 OBX-04, "Outbox table never purged; non-partial index" (**Medium**), kept as written. The register row now points here; the original finding text is unchanged. PERF-06 (Low, table growth) is NOT marked fixed.
- **Root cause (confirmed and reproduced).** Nothing in the application ever deleted a row from `outbox_events`: `published` and `failed` rows accumulated for ever, every event costing a row and five index entries (and, since a status change touches an indexed column, a second row version and further entries at publish), and the table could only grow. The original non-partial indexes were also never reviewed for the relay's query; OBX-03's partial pending/due index already serves that query, and the three older indexes (`ix_outbox_events_status_created_at`, `_aggregate`, `_tenant_id`) remain, none removed here. The OCR worker (Python) also inserts outbox rows directly, so any cleanup must not depend on which service created an event.
- **Baseline reproduction (isolated, original source).** `OutboxCleanupObx04DefectTest`: **2 of 2 fail** (no cleanup class exists; no code deletes from `outbox_events`). Both pass on the fix.
- **What changed (10 new files, under `backend/dms-core/src/`; no existing file modified; no change to OBX-01/02/03 or NOT-01 source, the OCR worker or `application.yml`).**
  - `outbox/OutboxCleanupStore.java`: the ONE statement that deletes from `outbox_events`. A materialized CTE picks and row-locks candidates (`status = 'published' AND published_at < now() - retention`, `ORDER BY published_at, outbox_event_id`, `LIMIT ?`, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), then the `DELETE ... USING picked` repeats the status and cutoff checks itself, so a delete never rests on the select alone. The cutoff is the DATABASE's `now()`; the comparison is strict (a row exactly at the cutoff is kept). All three values are bound parameters. It refuses to run outside a transaction (`MANDATORY`), a retention under 24 hours, and an out-of-range batch, whatever the caller passes. It can never delete a pending (including retry-scheduled), failed, or null-`published_at` row. No table lock.
  - `outbox/OutboxCleanupPolicy.java`: the settings, all validated at startup (also while disabled). **Disabled by default.** Development defaults, approved for development testing only and NOT approved production settings: `dms.outbox.cleanup.enabled` false; `published-retention-hours` 720 (30 days; minimum 24, maximum 10 years); `batch-size` 100 (1-10,000); `interval-ms` 3,600,000 (60 min; minimum 1,000); `max-batches-per-run` 1 (1-100).
  - `outbox/OutboxCleanup.java`: `@Scheduled` with the interval as both the delay and the initial delay (no run at startup, none while disabled and then no database access). A run is a counted loop of at most `max-batches-per-run` batches, each its own `REQUIRES_NEW` transaction with a 30 s timeout, and no new batch starts once a run has taken 30 s (worst case about 60 s). Runs cannot overlap on one instance; across instances the `SKIP LOCKED` batches take different rows. A database failure ends the run and is logged without any event data (count, duration, exception type only), and never kills the scheduler thread.
  - NEW `V140__outbox_published_cleanup_index.sql` (source only; NOT executed against any database): `CREATE INDEX ix_outbox_events_published_cleanup ON outbox_events (published_at, outbox_event_id) WHERE status = 'published'`. It removes or replaces no existing index. Its header comment and the index's stored comment still call it "optional"; that wording is outdated (inclusion was approved after the benchmarks) and was deliberately preserved byte-for-byte so the verified migration hash is unchanged - a non-functional documentation issue for a separately reviewed correction.
  - Tests: NEW `OutboxCleanupObx04DefectTest` (2), `OutboxCleanupPolicyTest` (8), `OutboxCleanupSqlSafetyTest` (14; it also runs the safety rules over broader, tempting deletes - by age alone, ignoring status, including failed rows, trusting the select alone, unbounded, non-strict or creation-time cutoff - and every one is rejected), `OutboxCleanupTest` (17), `OutboxCleanupIT` (16, PostgreSQL), `V140OutboxPublishedCleanupIndexMigrationTest` (3, PostgreSQL scratch databases).
- **Files applied (live development repository)**, byte-identical to the verified isolated copy (`verification\obx04-fix`, manifest `logs\final_manifest_v2.txt`). SHA-256: `OutboxCleanup.java` 0BF579C8C07A814D8B7B96DA07967D44F71992F8145EA26A07B323EC81CC735E; `OutboxCleanupPolicy.java` 375514DE0F82DBA7C79203BB2B4EE447F0F7A738DF3CA781EDB9C28F7D17ADF0; `OutboxCleanupStore.java` 5D951BE980E7DDEF61ABFD6606E10405EC2A8FF757DF9F2A8B5C6EAD97D9803A; `V140__outbox_published_cleanup_index.sql` 7FA4A021BFE131EE791E90A8C40C4B740C2E54FC51FD908822E3408B34492775; `OutboxCleanupIT.java` 5DB6DEC9EBDE28264B9782FFB9682AFA11E75FF797067C2CB78A95B8CE199960; `OutboxCleanupObx04DefectTest.java` 40EB52F9630073FAE12EFF969BEC7331D21D676E9EB935D96867FB8B31E8B483; `OutboxCleanupPolicyTest.java` 16F69AD51BDA713E94CBA57F78415A6F4E0C3D805100F5F2E3EB69AD95D88ED7; `OutboxCleanupSqlSafetyTest.java` 58BEA59B84FC289E0378312D1D4B776467C16E669FE8D86FFB11288F85702C34; `OutboxCleanupTest.java` E18201323FF431478A44A10CAAF2A8685203A90D908B708C3B44DA901158BB82; `V140OutboxPublishedCleanupIndexMigrationTest.java` 54C5097CD5B480F217BB2362175116B6632B164F50FD6B770D7745B8D675DA54. None of the 10 existed in live. The working tree went from 237 to 247 entries (10 new files); the full before/after path-and-status manifests were compared and only these 10 paths changed. The 19 OBX-03 files, `RabbitMqConfig.java` and the NOT-01 files are unchanged.
- **Isolated verification evidence (NOT live reruns).** Temporary, loopback-only, disposable PostgreSQL 18.7 (own data directory, random SCRAM credentials, identity verified before every run; stopped afterwards). RabbitMQ was NOT started and no broker integration was rerun for OBX-04.
  - **Migrations (8 of 8):** V139 5 and V140 3 - on populated tables nothing changes (every column of every row identical, the table not rewritten, all four existing indexes kept), a failure half way rolls the whole migration back and it then applies cleanly, Flyway ordering ends at V140. Large-table measurement: 500,117 published rows: V140 took 563 ms (19 MB index) and a writer inserting every ~5 ms saw its slowest insert at 339 ms (writers wait about as long as the build).
  - **`OutboxCleanupIT` 16 of 16** (two complete runs): only old published rows are deleted; pending, retry-scheduled, failed and null-`published_at` rows survive; the cutoff holds to the minute and a row exactly at it is kept; batch size and per-run quota enforced, oldest first; two cleanups at once take different rows (100 + 100 of 300, none blocked); a row another transaction holds is skipped; cleanup and relay run together without blocking each other (the relay's pending rows are never in the cleanup's set) and the relay still publishes; a rolled-back cleanup deletes nothing; rows inserted exactly like the OCR worker's (only its columns; database defaults apply) work and are cleaned like any other; four cleanup loops delete each row at most once. The first run had 3 failures, all count assertions confirming only the test's own rows were eligible: an earlier test of the class had left 13 old published rows behind and the cleanup correctly deleted them (a test-isolation flaw, not an application defect); only the test's `setUp` was corrected (it parks those leftovers, as pending rows already were), no assertion weakened.
  - **Timeouts (psql stand-in, not through the application):** with another session holding an exclusive table lock, a batch with a 3 s statement timeout was cancelled at 3.08 s and deleted nothing; a 1 s lock timeout cancelled at 1.06 s; once released the same batch deleted its 100 rows.
  - **Benchmarks (disposable data; NOT production estimates; mix 70% old published, 20% recent, 8% pending, 2% failed; default 128 MB shared buffers).** Batch selection (first 100 eligible rows), `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)`: at 1,000,000 rows **with V140 0.38 ms / 204 buffers (ordered index scan, no sort); without V140 using the original ordering 359 ms / 16,232 buffers (sequential scan and sort of every eligible row, cost proportional to the backlog); the existing-index alternative (ordering by `created_at`) 1.4 ms**; at 500,000 rows 0.29 / 190 / 0.45 ms; at 100,000 rows 0.30 / 60 / 0.38 ms; with 50,000 rows whose `created_at` is the oldest but were published recently (adverse distribution) V140 0.33 ms, existing-index alternative 12 ms. Throughput draining 50,000 rows in batches of 1,000 (about 75 ms of process overhead per batch included): with V140 10,073 rows/s, without 3,805, alternative 11,349. Concurrent load (`pgbench`, 6 clients, 20 s: relay-style job, cleanup batches, live writers): with V140 the cleanup batch averaged 2.1 ms, 0 failed transactions, 0 lock waits or deadlocks; without V140 145 ms. WAL about 112 bytes per deleted row in every variant. Cost of V140: publishing 20,000 pending rows wrote about 15.3 MB of WAL with the index against about 12.2 MB without (about 25% more); inserting pending rows is unchanged (V140 indexes published rows only); index size 6.4 MB / 31 MB / 63 MB at 100k / 500k / 1M rows against 4.1 / 20 / 40 MB for the existing status/created index. Decision: V140 INCLUDED, intended to be applied together with enabling the cleanup.
  - **Unit regression:** isolated full suite with PostgreSQL **1,387 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 5 skipped** (including the 39 database-backed tests and both migration tests); integration regression **68 of 68** (OBX-04 16, OBX-03 `OutboxRetrySchedulingIT` 16, OBX-01 `OutboxRelayConcurrencyIT` 13, OBX-02 `OutboxRelayConfirmationIT` 9 and `OutboxRelayInterruptIT` 1, NOT-01 `NotificationConcurrencyIT` 5, `NotificationDispatcherIT` 2, `NotificationLeaseRecoveryIT` 6). Mutation checks, all caught: no `SKIP LOCKED`, a non-strict cutoff, the status recheck removed from the DELETE, the disabled check ignored, the overlap guard removed, a partial batch not ending the run.
- **LIVE development tree, executed:** the focused run (`OutboxCleanupObx04DefectTest` 2, `OutboxCleanupPolicyTest` 8, `OutboxCleanupSqlSafetyTest` 14, `OutboxCleanupTest` 17, plus the OBX-03 `OutboxRetryPolicyTest` 22, `OutboxRelayRetryTest` 23, `OutboxRelayObx03DefectTest` 5, the OBX-01/02 `OutboxRelayBehaviourTest` 9, `OutboxRelayConfirmationTest` 23, `OutboxRelayObx02DefectTest` 3, `OutboxEventRepositoryQueryTest` 7, `OutboxRelayWireFormatTest` 6, and the NOT-01 `NotificationDispatcherRecoveryTest` 21 and `DeliveryClaimRepositorySqlTest` 6) passed **166 of 166**, exit 0, lowest free memory 2,576 MB. The backend unit regression (`mvnw -o -B test`, no database or broker) ran 1,387 tests with **0 failures, 47 errors and 5 skipped**; the 47 errors are exactly the 11 database-backed classes (39 tests) plus the 5 `V139OutboxNextAttemptMigrationTest` and 3 `V140OutboxPublishedCleanupIndexMigrationTest` tests, because no PostgreSQL was running (the database address was pinned to an unused port); the same classes pass on the isolated PostgreSQL. `mvnw -o -DskipTests package` succeeded and built `dms-core.jar`. **No PostgreSQL integration test, including `OutboxCleanupIT`, was re-run against the live tree, no migration was executed anywhere, and no infrastructure was started.**
- **Full integration-suite limitations.** The most recent complete integration run (OBX-01, isolated) was 974 run, 22 failures, 1 error, 7 skipped; **the full integration suite is NOT green** (ClamAV-dependent scan tests, `OcrEventsConsumerLiveIT` needing RabbitMQ, an unrelated `NotificationTriggersIT` folder-link mismatch, and 5 failures whose cause was never confirmed). A later isolated `mvnw verify` was killed by the system's low-memory protection and not repeated. ClamAV, Redis, Keycloak, OpenBao and RabbitMQ were not started for this finding.
- **Migration risk (V140).** Flyway will apply V140 at application startup against any database the application is pointed at, REGARDLESS of `dms.outbox.cleanup.enabled`; with the cleanup disabled (the default) the index then grows with every published row for nothing. `CREATE INDEX` is non-concurrent (Flyway runs it in a transaction): it blocks writes to `outbox_events` for about one scan of the table (measured only on disposable data, above). The production index-build strategy is unresolved; the production table size and write load must be reviewed before deployment, and a `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` build needs its own migration plan and approval.
- **Retention capacity.** Capacity per day = batch size x batches per run x runs per day. The enabled defaults (100 x 1 x 24) give at most 2,400 rows a day, which is insufficient for any workload publishing more than that - the backlog would still grow - and must be sized from measured event volume (disposable-environment suggestions: batch 500 for under about 2,000 events a day; batch 1,000 x 2 for about 20,000; batch 2,000 x 5 every 15 minutes for about 200,000; WAL is about 112 bytes per deleted row). The cleanup is never enabled automatically.
- **Production dependencies and limitations (unresolved, NOT fixed).** (1) Production retention period approval. (2) Cleanup-capacity sizing from real event volume. (3) V140 production migration strategy and writer-lock review. (4) Audited manual replay of terminally failed events (terminally failed events are permanently outside automated cleanup until a separate retention and replay policy is approved; no replay was implemented and no attempts or retry times were reset). (5) Failed-event retention policy. (6) Personal-data minimisation: the `user.created` payload carries the user's e-mail address and stays in the table until cleaned. (7) Outbox monitoring and alerts: no pending, failed, oldest-pending, published-awaiting-cleanup or cleanup-run metrics exist. (8) Autovacuum and database maintenance review for the table. (9) Shared scheduler contention: the cleanup shares the two-thread `@Scheduled` pool with about twenty jobs including the relay (PERF-03); normally a run takes milliseconds an hour, but a database stall could hold a thread for about a minute. (10) Multi-instance deployment testing. (11) RabbitMQ integration was not exercised. (12) Full production-like integration testing. (13) **JDBC statement-timeout propagation under database stalls is unverified**: the batch transaction's 30 s timeout is relied on to bound a blocked statement through Spring's transaction machinery, but only the server-side `statement_timeout` stand-in was exercised, never the application's own timeout path. (14) At-least-once delivery, and the remaining OBX-01/02/03 limitations, are unchanged. **Production verification pending.** No commit, push or deployment was made.
- **Resources kept:** `verification\obx04-fix` (isolated implementation, benchmark results, logs, and the stopped disposable PostgreSQL data directory, not deleted), `verification\obx03-fix`, `verification\obx02-fix` (including the portable RabbitMQ/Erlang binaries), `verification\obx01-fix`, `verification\not01-fix` and all earlier verification resources.
